# Changelog

Each milestone as it was built, newest first, with what the tests measured on the development Mac. Releases are marked where they were cut. Torqwave was briefly called SpectraBlade, up to 0.15.10, and was renamed in 0.16.0 because another audio product already used a similar name: the entries for those releases keep the name it had then.

## Unreleased

**Decrypt: it goes on until it comes no closer, a chain to hear, Again, and drops from the Files panel** (0.20.0)
- **A search goes on while it comes closer.** It ran 16 rounds and stopped, whether or not it was still gaining. In the window it now runs its 16 and then on, for as long as a round raises the best lock by a twentieth of a point, ending when six rounds in a row have not (64 rounds at most) with "NO CLOSER FOUND" and the round it ended on (fix log 83). Tools and tests still run a set number of rounds.
- **The chain**: each time the search comes a point nearer the signal, its best patch becomes a step in a row above the buttons, with its lock. A click plays a step; **Play** plays the signal and then every step in turn; a double-click goes back to a step (it goes on the keys, the later steps are dropped, and Again goes on from it). The last six are shown (fix log 84).
- **Again** carries a search on: from the candidate chosen (taken, or double-clicked onto the keys) the next twelve are all nearer versions of that one, in smaller steps (0.25 down to 0.06 of the range, against 0.45 to 0.15 for a first search) and with other variations each time; with none chosen, from all twelve. Choosing one had started nothing (fix log 82).
- **An open file dragged from the Files panel** lands on Decrypt's scope and on an oscillator's table view: both took only files dragged from outside the app (fix log 81).
- Tested (`--decrypt`, on an optimized build): a search until settled ended after 19 rounds and 5.4 s on a reese the synth made (97.9 % lock, the same as at 16 rounds: there was nothing left to gain); Again offered only once a search has ended, run three times with the lock never falling, and after Take going on from the candidate taken alone; the chain gaining steps (7 on that clip), Play going through it, a click only playing, and going back to its first step; a drag from the Files panel taken and any other not. Not tried by hand: any of it in the running app, the drag between the two windows, and a recording from outside the synth, where a search has further to go and will run longer.

**Torqwave Synth: Decrypt, a patch from a recording** (0.20.0, steps 17, 19 and 20 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md); the model's part experimental)
- **Decrypt**, the second page of the synth's window (**Knobs / Decrypt**, where the withdrawn Trace switch was), finds a Torqwave Synth patch that plays like a recording of one note. Drop the note on the scope or click **Decrypt a Sound...**: its pitch is found, it is trimmed and levelled, and up to 4 seconds of it are matched. Every factory patch is played at that note and compared with it by mel spectra at three window sizes and by the loudness over time; the nearest 48 (and the recording's own spectrum as a Sampled table on the best six) are scored over the whole note, then 16 rounds of variations of the best four, each round a smaller step. It runs in the background: the session keeps playing.
- **The scope** shows the twelve nearest with their locks, each in the sector of the part where it differs most from the best, nearer the middle the closer it is; the recording's spectrum is in the middle with the chosen patch's over it. A click hears a candidate, a click in the middle hears the recording, a double-click puts the candidate on the keys while the search goes on. **Take** keeps the best (named "Node" and its address); **Stop** puts back the patch you had. **Tables only from the factory** leaves the recording's own table out. The effects are off while matching, and a taken patch has them off.
- **What a lock can and cannot do.** The lock is how near the patch measures: 100 % the same spectra and loudness over time, 0 % 24 dB or more apart. A recording of Torqwave Synth itself is found again closely; anything else gets a patch in its spirit, close for other synths and simple tones, loose for voices, acoustic instruments, drums and samples, which a wavetable synth can only suggest. A lock says the two measure alike, not that they sound alike to you. One note only: a chord, a phrase or a loop is matched as one sound, badly, and a recording's effects and room are matched as part of the sound.
- **Ask Torq** has two new tools on a session: `decrypt_clip` gives a track playing Torqwave Synth the patch Decrypt finds for an audio clip ("lock 87 %, grown from Steel Rope" in the plan), and `table_from_clip` makes a clip (up to 30 s) a Sampled table on oscillator 1, 2 or 3. Both refuse, with the reason, a track without Torqwave Synth (or one whose instrument the same plan changes), a clip that isn't audio or is silent, and another oscillator. **Not in the undo step:** like other instrument changes, the patch and the table are set once the plan is applied, and the window says so.
- **The model helps Decrypt** when Ask Torq is on: it suggests factory categories and patches to start from (and parts to hold, if your words in the box under the scope call for it), proposes changes of structure to the best so far (a table, a filter type, a layer an octave up), and offers a name, category, description and macro names for a patch you take (**Use** or **No**). Each proposal is scored with a few variations of its own and kept only if it beats the best: its starting points change the path the search takes, so a result can end a little either side of the one without it. Decrypt asks the model to answer without thinking first (`chat_template_kwargs`; a server that refuses the field by name is asked again without it): with thinking, one answer took 107 s on the model below, without it 1.1 s. With a model, the search waits after its rounds for up to three more answers (30 s at most); a request over 30 s is dropped. **Let the model hear clips** (Settings > AI, off to begin with) sends the note to the server once, as a 16 kHz WAV, for a model that takes audio to describe; it is the only audio Ask Torq ever sends.
- Trace's scope and drawing (steps 15 and 16) are Decrypt's scope; `SignalConsole::variations` is the nodes' moves, factored out of `nodesAround` unchanged.
- **Measured with a real model** (`TorqwaveTests --decrypt-eval <url> <model>`, Qwen3.8 27B on a local server, told each patch's category in words): on the ten clips of the search test (measured before the 40 new basses, so with Warehouse Sub as its bass), the mean lock was 93.7 % with and without it; better on 5 (Bare Shiver by 0.6 points, the rest by 0.1 or less), worse on 1 (Abyss Reese by 0.1), 13 of 143 proposals kept, 7.1 s a search against 2.0 s. On the synth's own sounds the search needs no help; sounds from elsewhere and words about what to match were not measured. The model's part stays experimental.
- **Ask Torq with the real model** (`--ai-eval-set decrypt`, 4 requests on a session with two recorded notes, a track playing Torqwave Synth and one with no instrument): 4 of 4. "Make the Bass synth sound like the clip called reese note" and the same with the synth's own tables only plan one patch (the second with no clip table); "use the bell hit clip as a wavetable on oscillator 2" plans that table; "make the Keys track sound like the bell hit clip" plans nothing for a track without Torqwave Synth.
- **How long:** on a Mac Studio (M5 Ultra, 18 threads by default), 1.1 to 2.6 s a note for the ten patches of the test (about 3,400 patches played each), written in the guide.
- Not tried by hand: the window was driven by its own code in the tests (clicks, double-click, Take, Stop, the name offer, pictures at 70, 100 and 160 %), not used in the running app; no recording from outside the synth was decrypted, and Let the model hear clips was tested against a stand-in server only (the model above takes no audio).
- Tested (`--decrypt`, 11 checks): a note recorded at 44.1 kHz after silence is found at its pitch (0.1 cents from 220 Hz), trimmed and levelled; silence and a note under a tenth of a second are refused with a reason; a sound is no distance from itself and a bell 31.2 dB from a reese; changes at the edge of hearing (3 cents, filters 3 % up, envelopes 5 % longer, a 44.1 kHz recording) measure 0.98 dB at most on the ten patches, under the 3.0 dB a match must come within; **ten factory patches** (a bass, reese, acid line, sub, lead, pad, pluck, bell, stab and brass), recorded dry and decrypted without their own names to start from, all come back within it (0.2 to 2.9 dB, locks 88 to 99 %); the same clip and seed give the same twelve on 2 threads and on 5; a synth playing a chord beside a search is never late (its slowest block 0.14 ms against 10.7 ms), Stop is heard within a quarter of a second and the playing patch is untouched; a made-up model answering nonsense, late or never leaves the result as it is without one; a proposal of the reese's own settings is kept and a worse one dropped; the window refuses a silent clip, switches the tables, puts a candidate on the keys, gives the old patch back on Stop, and Take names the patch for its address or by an offered name. (`--ai`, 8 more checks, 181 in all): `decrypt_clip` plans a patch for a reese clip at A2 that the synth holds once applied, and nothing before; `table_from_clip` plans oscillator 2's table; a track without Torqwave Synth, a missing clip, a silent clip and a fourth oscillator are refused; a stopped search plans nothing; a model's answers are read through thinking and fences; the request asks for no thinking and falls back; audio goes to the server only with Let the model hear clips. (`--console`, 18 checks): unchanged, the window's switch now showing Decrypt.

**Instruments and effects follow the session's tempo** (0.20.0)
- Torqwave never gave its plug-ins the session's tempo: every tempo-synced setting (Torqwave Synth's delay, LFOs and step patterns in note values, StringSwarm's Strum Sync, Basic Drums' delay, and any third-party plug-in's synced delays and arps) ran at 120 BPM whatever the session's tempo, played or stopped. Every plug-in on a track, bus or the master now has a play head that gives the session's tempo and time signature at the frame it is working on (tempo changes included), its place in beats and its bar's start, and whether the session plays; stopped, it reads where the playhead stands, so a tempo typed in is heard at once while auditioning (fix log 80).
- Tested (the track effects checks, in the full suite): a plug-in is given 140 BPM in 4/4 at the start and 90 in 3/4 past a change at bar 2, with its place in beats and its bar's start. Not tried by hand: third-party plug-ins' synced effects.

**Torqwave Synth: 40 basses that move** (0.20.0)
- **40 new basses lead the Bass category**, each built on all three oscillators with several things moving it at once (6 to 15 modulation routes: synced LFOs, step patterns, envelopes on the warps, the tables and the filters) and four macros that change its character rather than its level. Four kinds, ten of each: **growls and wobbles** folded and driven under LFOs synced to the beat (Hydraulic Maw, Slag Wobble, Rotor Gnash, Cinder Mandible); **sync and bite**, hard sync swept by an envelope into drive (Riveted Snap, Torque Fang, Spark Gap, Bolt Cutter); **pumping unison**, wide detuned stacks gated and echoed in time (Pylon Bloom, Grid Pump, Strobe Saw Bass, Ribbon Throb); and **talking**, vowel tables and band filters that move as a mouth does (Mouth Of Iron, Yammer Bass, Formant Furnace, Wah Crane). The basses that were there before are all kept and come after them. Torqwave Synth now has 2,641 patches, 178 of them hand-made.
- Not tried by hand: the 40 were measured, not played in the running app.
- Tested (`--instruments`, no new checks; the bank's checks now cover them): each sounds in its register and passes the click phrase; each plays a held chord at -9.4 to -6.6 dB; none is a near-copy of another bass.

**Torqwave Synth: presets keep their tables and macro names** (0.20.0)
- **A preset saved from the synth's window keeps everything that isn't a knob**: each oscillator's Sampled table (as a session keeps it), the four macros' names and the patch's name. Loading it puts them back with the knobs; one with no Sampled table takes away a table the synth had, so a preset sounds the same wherever it is loaded. Before, a preset was the knobs alone: a table made from a clip was lost, and the macros kept the names of whatever was loaded before.
- Presets saved before this (and every effect's) are numbers alone; they load as before and leave the tables and macro names as they are. A preset file is read the same by an older Torqwave, which skips what it doesn't know.
- A Sampled table's root note is now written to 17 digits in a session and a preset, so it comes back exactly (it was rounded).
- Not tried by hand: no preset was saved and loaded in the running app (the presets bar is driven in the tests).
- Tested (`--instruments`, 3 more checks): a preset with a Sampled table on oscillator 2 and named macros, saved and loaded into a synth on another patch, comes back whole (the table's 64 frames, its root and name, the names, and its render sample for sample); a preset file of values alone loads the same values with no texts and leaves the names and tables as they were; choosing a preset in the presets bar sets its table and names.

**Torqwave Synth: 41 patches on all three oscillators** (0.20.0)
- **41 hand-made patches that use every layer**: all three oscillators, each on its own table and most with its own level envelope, so a sound changes from layer to layer as it plays (a click layer that dies away under a body that swells, a sub that arrives late, an octave that fades in), with the sub and noise where they help. Six basses, six leads and six pads, four stabs and four plucks, three each of reeses, drones and bells, two each of acid lines, sequences and textures: Three Stage Bass, Triple Reese, Stacked Acid, Tri Blade Lead, Triad Engine Stab, Layer Pluck, Cathedral Stack Pad, Three Engine Drone, Layer Bell, Layer Gate Sequence, Three Layer Static and the rest. They come after the other hand-made patches of their category. Torqwave Synth now has 2,601 patches, 138 of them hand-made.
- **A delay longer than its line**: a patch whose delay was set longer than the 4-second line (2, 4 or 8 bars at 120 BPM) read memory before the line's start on its first block, which could crash. It now starts at the line's length, as it already settled once playing.
- Not tried by hand: the 41 were measured, not played in the running app.
- Tested (`--instruments`, 2 more checks): each of the 41 sounds in its register and passes the click phrase (the hand-made patches were not put through it before); every factory patch still plays a held chord between -24 and -1 dB (the 41 at -11.2 to -7.6 dB for one note) and none is a near-copy of another in its category; a delay of 8 bars plays from the first block.

**Torqwave Synth: favourites** (0.20.0)
- **A heart beside the patch's name** marks a factory patch as a favourite; click it again to unmark. **Favourites** is the first entry of the patch list, shaped as the tree is: a sub-menu for each category that holds a favourite, in the tree's order ("None yet: click the heart beside a patch's name" until there is one). It was a flat list of names at first, which didn't say what kind of sound each was (fix log 77).
- **The arrows follow what you are browsing**: a patch chosen from Favourites puts the window's arrows, and the left and right arrow keys, on the favourites alone, in the tree's order, round their ends; one chosen from a category puts them back on every patch. The window's arrows and the keys go through one list (`BasicSynthProcessor::browseList`), so they cannot disagree.
- Favourites are kept by name, a line each, in `Synth Favourites.txt` in Torqwave's application data: the same in every session, and a name that is no longer a factory patch is dropped when the file is read.
- No key marks a favourite yet (`InstrumentKeys::toggleFavourite` is there for one).
- Not tried by hand: the heart and the menu were not clicked in the running app (the heart's code, the list and the arrows are tested; the heart is in `--synth-window`'s pictures).
- Tested (`--instruments`, 3 more checks; `--keys` runs them alone): the Favourites menu shows them under their categories in the tree's order with the patch it is on ticked; a patch is marked and unmarked by name, listed by name, kept in a file that reads back the same and drops a name that is no patch any more, and a sound that is no factory patch can't be marked; the arrows go through every patch until the favourites are browsed, then through the favourites alone (Abyss Reese, Cold Bell, Warehouse Sub), from a patch outside them to their first or last, and the window's heart marks the patch it is on.

**The swarm instrument is named StringSwarm** (0.20.0)
- The built-in instrument of strings all tuned to one note, built on this branch under the working name "Swarm" + "Guitar", is named **StringSwarm**, in the instrument menus, its window, Ask Torq and the guide. Nothing was released under the old name. Its plug-in ID is unchanged, so sessions made with builds of this branch still find it.

**Play an instrument from the computer's keys** (0.20.0; fix log 74)
- **The on-screen keyboard plays from any window.** **Window > On-Screen Keyboard** (Option-K; it was Window > Keyboard) shows it, docked or floating, and while it shows the computer's keys play the selected track's instrument from whichever of Torqwave's windows has the keyboard, in place of the shortcuts on those keys. Before, its keys played only while the panel itself had the keyboard.
- **The keys are Cubase's to begin with:** Q W E R T Y U I O P are the white keys from C, 2 3 5 6 7 9 0 the black. A button on the keyboard changes them to the A row (A S D F G H J K L ; ' with W E T Y U O P), and the choice is kept. **Z and X** move the octave down and up (C0 to C7), **C and V** the velocity (20 to 127).
- **Every instrument's window has the keys too**, without the panel: the same keys play that window's instrument, on its own track whichever is selected, and a strip of keys along the bottom of the window (C1 to C8) can be clicked. There, the **left and right arrows** load the previous and the next sound: Torqwave Synth's factory patches, any other instrument's presets. The transport's keys still work from the window.
- **Audition:** a small toggle (♪) in an instrument's presets row, after the arrows and beside Torqwave Synth's patch browser, on to begin with. With it on, a sound chosen in that window (the browser's arrows or list, the presets row, the arrow keys) is played at once by a phrase that suits it: a low line for basses, subs, reeses and acid; a held chord for pads, strings, organs, chords, textures and voices; its chord twice for stabs and brass; a rising figure for leads, plucks, bells and keys; one long note for sequences, arps, drones and risers; hits for percussion, drums, impacts and effects; kick, hat and snare on Basic Drums; a low E held on StringSwarm. The keys light as it plays. A key played by hand ends it, and it keeps quiet while the session plays or records. One switch for every instrument, kept in the settings.
- **Audition can loop, and has a Stop.** **Loop** (↻), beside the Audition button in an instrument's presets row, makes the phrase go round (every two or four beats at 125 BPM) until **Stop** is pressed, another sound is chosen (its phrase takes over) or the session starts playing. With Loop on, playing keys by hand leaves it going, so you can play over it. The Audition button is written out ("♪ Audition") where the presets row has room for it, and **instrument windows now show tooltips**: they showed none, so the button's meaning could not be found by hovering (fix log 75). Loop and **Stop** (■) were first put beside the keys at the bottom of the window, where they went unseen; they sit beside Audition now, written out as words where the row has room (fix log 76). The audition plays in the keys' octave: Z and X move it up and down from where it is written, and a phrase playing moves with them (a drum kit's stays on its drums) (fix log 78). **A menu of audition patterns** (the ▾ after Stop, or the pattern's name where the row is wide) chooses what the audition plays: a dozen for basses (rolling 16ths, offbeat 8ths, an EBM gallop, broken techno, an acid line with slides and accents, octave jumps, long notes for wobbles and step patterns, half-time, syncopated, a fast trill and more), for chords and pads (progressions, swells, dub offbeats), stabs, leads (arps, riffs, melodies, gated 16ths) and Basic Drums' kits (four on the floor, techno 16ths, EBM, industrial, electro, a fill), and eight for held sounds, hits and StringSwarm; whole bars at 125 BPM, so a loop goes round on the bar. Each kind of sound remembers its own choice, kept in the settings; choosing one plays it at once. The audition plays at the session's tempo where its playhead stands (125 BPM with no session), and moves with it when the tempo is changed.
- Keys play nothing while a text is being typed, or with Command, Control or Option held. A note is let go when its window goes behind, and a key whose release went unheard lets go of its note within a thirtieth of a second.
- A plug-in whose own window takes the keyboard for itself hears the keys before Torqwave does; its strip still plays by the mouse.
- Seen in the app: the strip under Torqwave Synth's window, X moving it to C4 and two typed keys sounding two voices. Not tried by hand: the panel's keys from the main window, Option-K, the A row, the arrows in a window, a real keyboard held for long notes, and Audition (not seen or heard in the app).
- Tested (`--keys`, 11 checks): the 85 patterns each named once, in range and inside whole bars, the one chosen played and kept by name; the audition moving with the keys' octave, a kit's staying put; a looping phrase going round on whole beats until stopped, played over by hand and ended by Stop; the phrase for each kind of sound; a phrase's notes starting and ending when due, a new phrase ending the one playing, a key played by hand ending it; the Q row and the A row's notes; a key held plays once and ends the note it began though the octave has moved; the octave and velocity keys and their ranges; everything let go at once; the next and previous sound for Torqwave Synth (round the ends of its list) and for StringSwarm through its presets row.

**Torqwave Synth: six faults in the engine and the first patches** (0.20.0; found by the second patch bank's checks)
- **Pitch modulation moves smoothly.** An oscillator's Pitch knob steps in semitones, and modulation of it was rounded to those steps too: an LFO at 1 % or less did nothing at all, so 63 patches had a vibrato that was never heard (Siren Burn, Industrial Whistle, Ghost Signal and 60 of the first bank's leads), and every pitch envelope moved in stairs. Modulated pitch is now continuous; a Pitch nothing modulates reads as before. **This changes how 515 factory patches sound** (13 hand-made, 179 in the first bank, 323 in the second: everything that routes a source to an oscillator's Pitch): drops and slides glide, vibratos are heard.
- **Step patterns play in time.** A synced Steps LFO plays its sixteen steps over its division, and 83 patches (4 hand-made, 79 in the first bank) had that set to a sixteenth or eighth note, so all sixteen steps went by inside one note: a buzz. They now take a bar (a step each sixteenth), or two bars where they were set to an eighth.
- **Wobble Press**'s Rate macro did nothing, because its LFO was synced to the tempo; the LFO is free now (4 Hz, an eighth note at 120 BPM) and Rate moves it.
- **Concrete Debris** clicked when struck again while ringing (its random LFO now moves more smoothly); **Rotten Chime, Toxic Chime and Oily Gong** were nearly silent up at C6, their filters fixed low: the filters now follow the key.
- **The same note renders the same whatever came before**: a voice's coloured noise (pink, crackle, digital) started from where its last note left it.
- Still as it was: about half the first bank's patches have a near neighbour by the second bank's similarity measure. None was removed or redrawn; presets name them.
- 90 patches' settings changed (`Scripts/patch_settings.txt`); the sequence presets that play on any of the 515 need their levels measured again.
- The console's nodes (kept for Decrypt): one that levelling can't bring within 6 dB of the sound it lies round is drawn again.
- Tested (`--instruments`, 5 more checks): an LFO to Pitch at 1 % is a vibrato 95 cents from its lowest to its highest (96 in full; none without it); each of the 83 synced Steps LFOs in the hand-made patches and the first bank takes a bar or more; Wobble Press's Rate changes its render; a note of pink noise renders the same, sample for sample, after other noise was played; Concrete Debris passes the click phrase and the three bells sound in their register; and every factory patch still passes the level, click and macro checks.

**Torqwave Synth: the signal console's scope, held for Decrypt** (0.20.0, steps 15 and 16 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- Built and then **withdrawn from the window**: Trace, a map of twelve sounds within reach of the one you hold, to hear and jump to. Tried in a build, it did not make plain why you would use it over the knobs or the patch browser, so it is not a feature of 0.20.0. Its **Knobs / Trace** switch is hidden, and nothing in the window opens it.
- What stays, for **Decrypt** (below): the scope and its drawing (five sectors, a sound drawn as its signature, a ring of spokes from the spectrum of its first second, with a four-digit address made from its settings), the nodes round a patch and their levelling to it, hearing a node on a second synth without touching the patch, taking one as the patch, and the route back. All of it is in `Source/SignalConsole.*` and `Source/SignalConsoleView.*`, tested.
- Not tried by hand by its author: the view's clicks, jumps, route and firewalls are tested through its own code (`TorqwaveTests --console-window` saves pictures).
- Tested (`--console`, 18 checks): the same node, Reach and seed give the same twelve, another seed others, each differing from the node held; at Reach 0.5 they lie from 0.15 to 0.50 away in every part, each within 0.24 dB of the held node's peak; a firewalled part is unchanged in every node and no node lies in its sector; at Reach 0 every node is the node held; the pan, velocity, voice mode, glide, bend and macros never move; bridged, each node is a step nearer the second patch; an address is the same for the same settings and another when a setting moves by 1 Hz; all 600 nodes round 50 factory patches sound and none clips; hearing a node sounds it and leaves the patch as it was; a jump sets the knobs, names the patch and maps twelve more; a click on the route's first entry returns its settings exactly; a knob turned by hand makes the patch as it is the node held; the scope draws at 70 %, 100 % and 160 % with each node in its part's sector; a click, Jump, a double-click, the route and a firewall chip do what they say, and with motion off nothing moves; a node's signature and address follow its settings; and the window shows no switch to the scope.

**1,493 more patches for Torqwave Synth, and thirteen new categories** (0.20.0, step 9b of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- **A second bank of 1,493 factory patches** takes Torqwave Synth from 1,067 to 2,560. Dark techno, industrial and EBM come first, as before: 102 more basses, 35 reeses, 28 acid lines, 54 leads, 53 stabs, 39 plucks, 60 pads, 47 drones, 32 bells, 68 sequences, 35 risers, 40 impacts, 70 pieces of percussion, 48 textures and 29 voices. Then what sound design, picture and game work reach for, in **thirteen new categories**: **Sub** (24), **Chord** (60: a chord from one key), **Arp** (60: a pattern of notes from one held key, in time with the song), **Keys** (50: FM electric pianos, reed pianos, clavs, plucked-string pianos, harpsichords), **Organ** (44: drawbar, pipe, combo and reed organs), **Brass** (49), **Strings** (45: string machines, pizzicato, short bows, tremolo, a bowed comb), **Cinematic** (67: braams, low hits, pulses, tension beds, swells, horns, heartbeats, stingers, clocks), **Drum** (97: kicks, snares, claps, hats, toms and cymbals from the oscillators, C3 each drum's own pitch), **FX** (72: lasers, alarms, computers, sonar, fly-bys, power failing, telemetry, sparks, saucers), **Glitch** (48), **Game** (84: pickups, power-ups, jumps, shots, explosions, menu sounds, fanfares that play a tune from one key press) and **Chip** (53: the pulse, triangle and noise voices of old sound chips). Keys, brass and strings are synthesised, not sampled: an electric piano and a string machine, not a grand and an orchestra.
- **Nothing that was there has moved.** Every existing patch keeps its name, its settings and its place, and a category's new patches come after its old ones, so the sequence presets pick the same voices.
- **Made by measurement, not by hand.** `Scripts/make_patch_bank.py` draws each of 193 kinds of sound (`Scripts/patch_bank_kinds.py`: an FM bass, a clap, a braam, a pickup) four times for every patch wanted, plays each of the 6,292 candidates through the synth and measures it, sets its level, and keeps it only if it passes every check below. Within a category it takes the candidates furthest from what it has already kept. The file it writes, `Source/WaveSynthPatchBank2.cpp`, is plain settings: the same on every build and machine.
- **No near-copies.** A patch is told by a held note's level over time, its spectrum at four moments, how its level moves and how wide it is: 122 numbers in dB. Two patches' distance is the mean difference over the numbers either has above its floor. No patch of the second bank is within 3.0 of another in its category, the old patches included (the nearest pair is Welded Breach and Far Sinkhole, 3.21 apart; half are 5.05 or more from their nearest neighbour). For scale: a saw bass with its cutoff an octave higher is 2.6 away, the same bass on a square 3.8, and half of the first bank's patches have a neighbour within 3.0.
- **Every new patch has four named macros that are heard**: from 0 to 100 each moves the level of some eighth of a second, or some band of the sound, by at least 1 dB (the least, Boarded Feed's Space, 1.5 dB).
- **No clicks**: each new patch is played through a phrase that strikes its note again twice while it rings, releases it, takes its voice with seventeen notes 10 ms apart, and releases a note 1.3 ms into its attack. The sequence presets' click measure finds nothing (the nearest, Wan Spark, 3.2 times its surroundings, where 4 fails).
- **The patch menu** opens a category with more than 40 patches as pages by name ("Ab - Co"), so no list runs off the screen.
- **A fault in the synth, found on the way and fixed: a free-running LFO's rate ignored modulation.** Only a retriggered LFO's rate answered a slot, so the Motion macro on the first bank's 80 pads did nothing. A free-running LFO's rate now follows the sources every voice shares (the macros, the wheel, pressure and the other free-running LFO); with the macro at 0 those pads sound as they did.
- Not done: **nobody has listened to these patches.** They were chosen by the measurements above, which catch silence, clicks, dead macros and copies, and cannot tell a good sound from a dull one. The first bank's Sequence patches set their Steps LFOs to cycle every sixteenth note, which plays all 16 steps inside it (a Steps LFO plays its steps once a cycle): a buzz rather than a pattern in sixteenths. They were left as they are, since nothing existing was to change; the second bank's patterns cycle once a bar.
- Tested (`--instruments`, 10 more checks): `Scripts/patch_settings.txt` lists all 2,560 patches with their settings' hashes as they are (a changed patch shows as a changed line); the second bank adds 1,493 patches with no two names alike whatever the case and four macros routed on each; the patch menu holds every patch once, its longest page 40 and at most 6 pages to a category; every second-bank patch's note peaks above -30 dB in its category's register (the quietest, Languid Elder, -26.5 dB); none clicks over the phrase (the nearest, Wan Spark, 3.2 times where 4 fails); every macro moves the sound by 1 dB or more (the least, Boarded Feed's Space, 1.5 dB); the 119 patches with an envelope that takes more than 1.5 s to rise stay under -1 dB with their chord held ten seconds (the loudest, Floating Hermit, -3.3 dB); the first second-bank patch of each of its 28 categories renders bit for bit the same in blocks of 512, 64 and 37 samples; no second-bank patch is within 3.0 of another in its category (the nearest, 3.21); a 1 Hz free-running LFO on the level crosses its mean 3 times in two seconds and 34 times with a macro on its rate at 100. All 2,560 patches play their held chord between -24 and -1 dB (the second bank's between -14.8 and -8.0 dB). The level check and the bank's checks play eight patches at a time, each on a synth of its own, and the instrument tests take 4 minutes 18 seconds on the Mac Studio in an unoptimised build (44 seconds before the second bank).

**Ask Torq finds factory sounds, sequences, kits and drum maps** (0.20.0, the first half of step 19 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md), experimental)
- **Factory sounds by name.** In a session, `list_instrument_presets` now also lists the sounds Torqwave's own instruments ship with: Torqwave Synth's factory patches, Basic Drums' kits and StringSwarm's presets. It takes a `category` and a `search` word, returns at most 60 with how many more there are, and names the categories. `set_instrument` takes one of them by name. A name that matches nothing is refused with the five nearest names (those sharing the most words, then the fewest letters away), and the plan stays as it was. A preset the user saved comes before a factory sound of the same name.
- **Factory sequences and kits.** `find_sequence_presets` searches the Sequence Library's Presets by role, style or genre, energy, character and words, 40 at a time. `add_sequence_preset` puts one at a bar, on a new track with the instrument it brings or on a track named (its notes only); `add_kit` puts a whole kit at a bar, a folder with a track for each part. Both run the code a drop from the library runs (`SequenceFile::place`, `SequenceKit::place`), so the tempo handling, the voice, the move into the session's key when that setting is on, and the move to a mapped track's drum keys are the same.
- **Drum maps.** `list_drum_maps` names the saved maps and a track's own, `set_drum_map` gives a track a saved map or General MIDI's layout back, and `remap_clip_notes` moves a clip's notes to the map's keys or back, each note once, as the piano roll's Remap Notes does.
- **The new parameters** reach the model as the others do: all 27 of StringSwarm's are read with their choices and set by `set_track_effect`, Source and Strum Sync among them, and all 235 of Torqwave Synth's are read, each oscillator's level envelope and modulation slots 9 to 16 among them (`list_effect_parameters` lists 150 at a time; `find` reaches the rest). Nothing had to change for this; it is now tested. The Sequencer's tools name up to 256 of an instrument's parameters, which 235 fits under.
- **What is in the undo step and what is not:** the tracks, clips, drum map and moved notes are in the plan's one undo step. The instruments are not: a factory sound, and the instrument a sequence or kit brings, load once the plan is applied, and the window says so. An instrument that arrives with a sequence can't be changed in the same plan; the tools refuse and say to ask again afterwards.
- The model's instructions describe the six new tools, and the guide's Ask Torq page has a section on them with requests to try.
- **Tried with a real model.** A new evaluation set, `--ai-eval-set library`, has 14 requests on a session with the shipped presets and one saved drum map, each checked against the plan's session and loads, not the model's words: a reese for the bass track, a patch, a Basic Drums kit and a StringSwarm preset by name, a patch and a kit that don't exist (no load, nothing added), "Give me an EBM kit at 124" (the tempo set and a factory EBM kit's clips exactly as a drop places them), a kit by name at bar 9, a techno bass line on a new track at bar 5, a hi-hat pattern on a named track at bar 3, which drum maps are saved, a track's map set, set and remapped, and remapped back. A local Qwen3.8-27B got **14 of 14, twice**, with the wording as first written; no description or instruction was changed. A request took 4 to 43 s, most about 6 s (162 s and 138 s for the set). For "an EBM kit at 124" it set the tempo first and then added a factory EBM kit, a different one in each run.
- **The session set with 43 tools:** 16 of 16 in one run (99 s), as recorded before. The run before that counted 14 of 16. One of the two was the set's own mistake: "Put a compressor on the vocal track" still expected no plan, from before sessions had track effects; the model added the Compressor, which is right, and the check now expects that. The other was the model's: for snares on beats 2 and 4 it wrote a 4 s clip with two notes that were not at 0.5 s and 1.5 s. It did not repeat.
- Not done: the requests were run through the evaluation, not typed into the window, so the window's own Apply path was not used with the real model. The tools find factory presets only, not the sequences in the user's own library. Trace, Decrypt and the table from a clip are the second half of step 19.
- Tested (`--ai`, 39 more checks, 173 in all): the nearest names are the same every time; each instrument's factory sounds are known (1,067 patches, 7 kits, 296 StringSwarm presets); a listing with a category and a search word returns only the matches and says how many more; a patch by name is one plan step and a factory load; words that match nothing are refused with the nearest names and leave the plan and the track as they were; a factory load's state is the instrument's own with the sound in it, and on the engine a planned patch changes nothing until applied; a sequence added by the tool gives the same session as `SequenceFile::place` with the same arguments (at 124 BPM, on a new track and on a track with a drum map, and with drops moved into the session's key), with the same voice to load; the 1,134 presets the app ships are found through the library the app hands over (read once a request, 0.4 s here), and a shipped EBM kit added by the tool is the session `SequenceKit::place` makes; a kit added by the tool and applied equals the kit dropped the usual way on a second engine, session and each track's instrument state alike, and one undo takes it out; `set_drum_map` then `remap_clip_notes` moves the notes as `NoteEditor::remapNotes` does, and applied and undone gives the notes and the map back exactly; StringSwarm's Source and Strum Sync are listed and set on the engine's instrument, and the synth's level envelopes and slot 16 are found; asked by the made-up model, the window shows both plan steps and that the instruments load outside the undo step. What the main window does on Apply (loading the planned sounds and voices onto the tracks) is the same two calls the checks make, but the window's own code path is not run by a check. The whole suite passed (2404 checks).

**Next and previous preset, without the menu** (0.20.0; fix log 73)
- The presets row (in StringSwarm, Basic Drums, Torqwave Synth and every built-in effect) has two arrows before its menu: **previous** and **next** preset, round from the last to the first. The **left and right arrow keys** do the same while the row has the keyboard, and so does the **mouse wheel** over the menu.
- They go in the menu's order: each factory sub-menu in turn, then the factory presets without one, then your own. StringSwarm's 296 presets are one click each.
- Torqwave Synth's factory patches already stepped with the arrows beside the patch's name; this is for its saved presets and for everything else.
- Not tried by hand: the arrows, keys and wheel in the running app.
- Tested (1 more check, `--effect-previews`): next, next, previous; back from the first to the last and on to the first again; each applied as it is chosen; by the left and right keys; StringSwarm's 296 by one sub-menu after another, each once; with no presets the arrows do nothing.

**Torqwave Synth: a table from a sound, a level envelope for each oscillator, sixteen slots** (0.20.0, step 14 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md): groundwork for the signal console)
- **Sampled**, an eleventh table: **drop an audio file on an oscillator's display** (or double-click it to choose one) and the sound becomes that oscillator's wavetable. Its spectrum is taken at up to 64 steps from start to end, each a band-limited frame of 256 harmonics, so **Position** plays through the sound's tone. A sound with a clear pitch (found by autocorrelation, 30 Hz to 2 kHz) is measured at that pitch; one without is measured on a low root (23.4 Hz at 48 kHz). Every frame has the same phases, chosen to keep its peaks low, so frames blend without cancelling. The table is kept in the instrument's state (about 47 KB for 64 frames), so a session with one plays anywhere; an oscillator set to Sampled without a table plays a sawtooth.
- **A level envelope for each oscillator** (**Osc Levels**, a seventh tab beside the envelopes and LFOs): Attack, Hold, Decay and Sustain on that oscillator's level, off until switched on. A note struck again starts it from where it is.
- **Sixteen modulation slots** (from eight), eight to a page: **Slots 1-8** and **Slots 9-16** beside **Effects**. Sessions and presets saved with eight load as they were.
- **Every factory patch plays as before**: a hash of each of the 1,067 patches' renders (a held chord and its tail, `TorqwaveTests --patch-hashes`) is the same before and after this step, on the development Mac. The new settings come after all the earlier ones, so every earlier parameter keeps its place (235 parameters, from 196).
- The **On** switch of each oscillator stands clear of its title.
- Known gap: **a preset saved from the presets bar keeps the settings but not the Sampled table** (presets hold numbers only); the session and the instrument's state keep it.
- Not tried by hand: dropping a file and the file chooser were not used in the running app (the table, its state and its display are tested; `TorqwaveTests --synth-window` saves pictures of the window with one). Automation of an oscillator's Table drawn before this version reads one step off at its far end, since the list is one longer.
- Tested (`--instruments`, 6 more checks): a sine swept from 220 Hz to 1.98 kHz made into a table on a 55 Hz root (64 frames) plays the sweep's own harmonic loudest at the five positions where the sweep crosses harmonics 8, 14, 20, 26 and 32, with that harmonic and its neighbours holding all the power to within 0.01 dB; a table saved in the state comes back the same (frames, root, name) and plays the same sample for sample, and a factory patch loaded after has none; a table from a 110 Hz sawtooth takes 110 Hz as its root, one from noise the low root, and under a tenth of a second makes none; a 200 ms level attack is at 0.500 of its level 100 ms in with the amp envelope open, holds at 1.000, and its 300 ms decay to 25 % is at 0.2575 after 300 ms (99 % of the way); switched off, the oscillator plays as it did, sample for sample; envelope 2 to the cutoff in slot 16 renders the same as in slot 1; a state with the 39 new settings taken out loads and plays as it was; no click with a Sampled table and its level envelope struck sixteen times in mono, then All Sound Off.

**Basic Drums' delay no longer reads past its line** (0.20.0)
- The kit's ping-pong delay could read one sample past the end of its delay line when its read position landed a hair under the start (the position plus the line's length rounds up to the length itself). What it read was whatever memory held, so an echo could carry a stray sample. The position is now wrapped again after the add. The same fault was found first in Torqwave Synth's comb filter, where it changed a patch's level by 30 dB under scribbled memory.
- Tested: the drum checks (`--drums`, 44) pass the same plainly and with memory scribbled before and after use (`MallocPreScribble`, `MallocScribble`). No check reproduces the stray read itself; it was found by reading the code.

**Sessions start in bars and beats, and the lanes show the grid** (0.20.0, fix log 72)
- **A new session counts in bars and beats** (New Session, the session at start-up, a project without a session). A session saved before opens as it was saved: in time, unless it was saved in bars. A session for picture or sound design switches to time with B, as before. Attaching a video does not switch it, since scoring to picture counts in bars too.
- **The lanes show the grid plainly** in every theme. It is drawn behind the clips: a bar's line strongest, then a beat's, then the lines between beats. Before, it was drawn over the clips, so faintly it hardly showed.
- **Counting in time, the lanes draw a line at each of the ruler's labelled ticks**, following the zoom as the labels do; before, the lanes had no lines at all in time. There is no switch to hide either grid, as there was none before.
- Tested (multitrack view, 3 more checks): a new session is in bars and beats and a file without the setting opens in time; on a 2x render the bar, beat and eighth lines stand out from the lane in Dark, Light and High Contrast, in that order (contrast against the lane, bar/beat/eighth: Dark 2.71/1.59/1.22, Light 2.08/1.40/1.17, High Contrast 2.57/1.48/1.15); in time the lanes draw the ruler's ticks (contrast 1.45 in Dark). The whole suite passed (2346 checks).

**1,018 presets and 116 kits, on the new voices** (0.20.0, step 12 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- **1,018 factory sequence presets and 116 starter kits** (from 160 and 8) across 37 styles: techno and industrial deeper, electro, breakbeat, drum and bass, ambient, deep house, darksynth and psytrance, and game audio, with **UI Sounds** and **Stingers** as new SFX sub-roles. By role: Drums 254, Bass 177, Harmony 128, Arps 128, Pads 113, SFX 111, Melody 107.
- **Each preset plays on a named factory patch or kit**: 103 different voices, 96 patches of Torqwave Synth and the seven Basic Drums kits. A voice names its patch or kit rather than carrying settings, so a preset follows the patch as it is improved.
- **Levels are measured, not guessed**: each preset's track gain comes from its rendered peak (`Scripts/make_presets.py --calibrate`, kept in `Scripts/preset_gains.txt`). Rendered on their voices the presets peak from -19.0 dB (Falling Note Ramp) to -8.7 dB. The levels were measured again after the synth's pitch modulation became continuous and its step patterns were put in time: 34 presets' gains moved.
- **Every preset is checked for clicks** on its own voice: a jump between two samples of 0.03 or more that stands alone (three times the steps beside it, four times the largest step in the 30 ms either side) fails. A drum hit's first 3 ms are left out, since a hit starts with a jump of its own, and a fast attack that rises over several samples is not counted. The check found one: **Glass Shard** jumped 0.28 in one sample when a note was struck again while it rang, because the envelope on its oscillator's position restarts at once. The synth's oscillators now move their position and warp no faster than the amp may rise (1.5 ms from end to end), on every patch.
- The guide's **Styles** page covers all 37 styles: Techno › Dub, Industrial › Dark electro and Coldwave, the styles around them (electro, breaks, drum and bass, deep house, darksynth, psytrance, ambient) and the six game styles, each with its tempos and what marks it.
- **A kit no longer clips the master.** Each preset was levelled alone, near -9 dB, so a kit's six parts playing together went over: 106 of the 116 kits peaked above -1 dB, the loudest (Terror Kit) at +4.7 dB. A part inside a kit now sits 6 dB lower than the same preset alone; the loudest kit peaks at -1.3 dB. Found while taking the guide's screenshots, where the Basement Kit lit the master's red.
- **A patch added to the synth later changes no preset:** the script lists each part's patches by name, where it used to take the first few of a category.
- Not done: the new presets were not listened to one by one.
- Tested (`--sequences`): 1,018 sequences and 116 kits unpack; every one keeps the preset rules; `Scripts/voice_names.txt` matches the instruments' patches and kits; every voice names a patch or kit there is and sounds on it between -24 and -1 dB; the script run again writes the same files byte for byte; a kit's parts added together peak below -1 dB; no preset clicks on its voice (the nearest, Creeping Loop, 2.8 times its surroundings where 4 fails; before the fix, Grinder Played Bass on Glass Shard at 11.1).

**Drum patterns on any kit: Remap Notes** (0.20.0, step 11 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- A drum map's sound can say which General MIDI drum it **stands for** (a kit's snare on key 40 stands for 38), in **Edit Sound...** or by **Learn Its Note from MIDI**: right-click a row, play the kit's key on a MIDI input, and the row moves there, standing for the note it had.
- **Remap Notes to This Map** moves a clip's General MIDI notes to the kit's keys in one undo step, each from where it was (sounds that trade keys move once); **Remap Notes to General MIDI** moves them back.
- **A drum preset dropped on a track whose map remaps lands on the kit's keys.** Other parts, and presets dropped on empty space, keep their notes.
- **Maps are saved by name** (**Save Map As...**, **Maps**, **Delete a Saved Map**) in Torqwave's application data, a `.tqdrums` file each, for any track in any session. The session keeps what each sound stands for.
- Not tried by hand: the menu, the dialog and Learn with a real MIDI device were not used in the running app; no Battery kit was mapped.
- Tested (`--drumgrid`, 5 more checks): four of a clip's five notes move to the kit's keys in one step and come back exactly; General MIDI's own map moves nothing; a note learnt (47) puts the Acoustic Snare on 47 standing for 38; a map saved as "Battery: Dark Kit" comes back the same and can be deleted; a drum preset dropped on a mapped track lands on 60, 40 and 42 where a bass part and a new track keep 36, 38 and 42.

**Basic Synth becomes Torqwave Synth, a wavetable synth, and stops clicking** (0.20.0, steps 8 and 9 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- **Its new name is Torqwave Synth**, in the window, the instrument menus and Ask Torq. Its plug-in ID is unchanged, so sessions and presets saved under the old name still find it, and show it by its new name (a track named after it keeps the name it was given).
- **A new engine** in place of the old one, under the same plug-in ID: three **wavetable oscillators** (Analog, Pulse, Vowel, FM, Metal, Sub, Additive, Sync, Growl and a plucked **String**), each with a Position through its table, a **warp** (Sync, Bend, Squeeze, Mirror, FM, Crush, Fold), pitch, level, pan and **unison** of up to 8 voices with detune, stereo spread, **strum** and **drift**; a **sub** and a **noise** oscillator; two filters (**ladder** 24 and 12 dB low-pass, high-pass and band-pass, **SVF** low, band, high and notch, and a **comb**) in series or side by side, with drive and key tracking; four envelopes with curves, two LFOs (free or synced, eight shapes, 16 drawn steps), eight **modulation slots** over 46 destinations, four **macros**; poly, mono and legato with glide; and a rack of **effects**: drive (soft, hard, tube, fuzz, fold, crush), chorus, phaser, a synced ping-pong delay and reverb. Stereo now.
- **Its own window**: rotary knobs with value arcs, drag (Shift for fine), double-click or Alt-click to reset, a typed value on right-click, values in their units; the wavetables drawn in depth with the frame playing in front, filter curves that move with modulation, envelopes with points to drag, LFOs with steps to draw. **Drag any source onto a knob to modulate it**: a coloured ring shows the depth (drag it to change it) and a dot where the knob is now. In the theme's colours, light or dark, and sharp at any size (it scales from 70 % to 160 %).
- **1,067 factory patches for dark techno, industrial and EBM**: basses, reeses, acid, leads, stabs, plucks, guitars, pads, drones, bells, sequences, risers, impacts, percussion, textures and voices, each with named macros. 97 are made by hand and come first in each category; the other 970 are a bank drawn from 24 kinds of sound (a reese, an acid line, a strummed guitar, a gated sequence…) with settings in the ranges that suit each, sorted by name, their levels set from a held chord like the rest. **Wailing Fuzz Lead** is a fuzz lead, one note at a time: strings that ring on over a sustained wave, long slides and a late, wavering vibrato; **Swarm Drone** is strings all tuned to one note, strummed, drifting and fuzzed; **Body Riff Power Chord**, **Palm Mute Chug** and **Barbed Riff** are the early-90s EBM synth guitar, a power chord from one key, clipped hard; **Target Lock Bass** is a sequenced bass matched by spectrum to an early EBM classic, a sawtooth through a resonant low-pass that rings on its 3rd harmonic (its first four harmonics within 2 dB of the record's). Guitar patches that hold their notes have strings that ring on (several seconds rather than a fraction of one), and the drones a sustained wave under the strings.
- **No clicks** ([#45](https://github.com/BobClawblaw/torqwave/issues/45)): a note struck again keeps its phase and filter and its envelopes go on from where they are; a stolen voice and All Sound Off fade over 4 ms; the amp never rises or falls in under 1.5 ms or releases in under 3 ms; a voice ends at exactly nothing.
- **Old sessions** open with their Basic Synth settings mapped to the nearest new ones (the wave as oscillator 1's position, oscillators 2 and 3, the filter as an SVF, the filter envelope and velocity to filter as modulation slots, glide as legato). **They sound close, not the same.** The mod wheel no longer adds vibrato by itself: it moves whatever a patch routes it to. The factory sequence presets play on their voices through this mapping until step 12 gives them patches.
- **Ask Torq** tells the model up to 256 of an instrument's parameters (64 before), so its automation advice reaches the new synth's filters, amp and effects (196 parameters, the filters past the 64th).
- **Fixed: a comb filter, a plucked string or the echo could read one sample past the end of its delay line**, once each trip round it, whenever its delay was not a whole number of samples: a read position a hair under zero rounded up to the line's length. What lay past the line was whatever the memory held, so a patch could come out louder in one run than another (Shadow Wreck, a comb, at +2.4 dB in a full test run and -18.2 dB alone). Found with the allocator filling memory with junk (`MallocPreScribble`: Shadow Wreck, Derelict Quake and Distant Crash at +12 dB, Cinder Wreck at +6) and Guard Malloc, which stopped in the comb; with the fix the synth's checks print the same levels with and without the junk, and Shadow Wreck, Wailing Fuzz Lead and Acid Corrosion play through under Guard Malloc.
- Tested (`--instruments`, 22 more checks): a bare sine at its velocity, 1.0004 for 1, starting from silence and silent 20 ms after its hold; velocity and level; a chord; sixteen voices and a steal; pitch bend, Volume, Pan, Cutoff (CC 74), the pedal and the mod wheel through a slot; state with the patch and macro names; a 0.19.2 state mapped to the new settings; the window at 1240 x 760, an LFO dropped on the cutoff knob routing it and showing its ring, every page drawn in dark and light at 2x; every patch's held chord between -24 and -1 dB (all 1,067); every table's aliasing at C7 under -60 dB (the most, -80.8 dB); unison mono at no spread (correlation 1.0000) and 0.494 at full spread with 8 voices; the ladder -12.0 dB at its cutoff and -49.8 dB two octaves up (-49.2 for four poles), its resonance peaking at 974 Hz for 1 kHz, 20.6 dB over its pass band at 90 %; the SVF -6.0 dB at its cutoff; a macro on each of the 46 destinations moving it by the slot's amount and changing the sound, and every source moving what it is routed to; six patches bit for bit the same in blocks of 512, 333, 64, 37 and 1 sample; sixteen voices in 8-voice unison through both filters and every effect take 5.9 % of a core on the Mac Studio (9.1 % with all three oscillators so); and the click test: Falling Note Ramp, Minor Bell Motif, Low Boom and a retrigger, steal and All Sound Off case move at most 0.081 between samples (limit 0.15), where 0.19.2's synth makes 0.29, 0.25 and 0.27. The whole suite passed (2291 checks).

**StringSwarm: every string on one note, through a fuzz, or a swarm of oscillators** (0.20.0)
- **StringSwarm** is a third built-in instrument, only in Torqwave: each note is a guitar of 1 to 12 modelled strings all tuned to it (Unison, Low Octave, Three Octaves or Fifths), a few cents apart, strummed one after another, through a fuzz or crunch and a cabinet. **Layers** plays up to four guitars at once, each through its own amp, spread left and right. **Strum Rate** strums again and again while a key is down, and **Strum Sync** sets it in note values (1/4 to 1/32, triplets too) at the session's tempo instead, counted from each note's start, so a part played on the grid strums on it.
- **Swarm**, the other **Source**, puts a sawtooth oscillator in place of each string: up to 12 a guitar, fanned evenly across **Spread** (a few cents to an octave) around the note, and tuned and laid out by octave as the strings are. **Converge** closes the fan to one pitch over its time, or, below 0, opens it from one; **Glide** slides every oscillator from the last note to the new one, each at its own speed, so the swarm smears; **Drift** lets each wander on its own slow course. A resonant low-pass after them is opened by an envelope (**Attack**, **Decay**, **Sustain Level**, **Release**) that Strum Rate fires again and again while a key is down, for pulsing, sequenced swarms; Strum staggers the oscillators' entries. The amps, layers and field are the strings'.
- **296 factory presets** in the preset menu, in sub-menus by category: for the strings, **Walls**, **Riffs**, **Strums**, **Clean** and **Drones**; for the swarm, **Swarm Sequences**, **Swarm Pads**, **Swarm Leads**, **Swarm Basses**, **Swarm Risers** and **Swarm Fuzz**. Each sets every setting and plays a held E2 and B2 at about -9 dB peak; the 42 that repeat at a note value are synced to the tempo. **Tremolo Swarm Wall** is matched by spectrum to the swarm-guitar wall of a 2017 industrial ballad: twelve strings over three octaves of one note, 30 cents apart, four guitars picked 14 times a second through the fuzz, its third-octave bands 5.1 dB (RMS) from the record's between 180 Hz and 4.6 kHz. Factory presets of any effect can now come in sub-menus by category.
- Nothing stops dead: a note let go is damped over Release, and a stolen note and All Sound Off fade over 20 ms.
- **Its own window**, with knobs in real units: the guitar's strings drawn across it, each with the note it is tuned to and how many cents off it sits, swinging as it rings, with a mark where the pick is; the amp with the curve it bends the signal through; the strum; the guitars across the stereo field; and the swarm and its envelope and filter, dimmed while it plays strings (and the strings' Pick and Sustain dimmed while it plays the swarm). Dark and light themes ([docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md), step 13).
- Tested (`--swarm`, 21 checks): the window is 900 by 690, its knobs read 70 %, 4.50 kHz, 6 strings, Once, 6.00 s and -12.0 dB as it comes, its strings are named D1, D2 and D3 once a D is played, a knob turned sets its parameter, and with the swarm chosen the Strings knob counts Oscillators; one string with no amp is in tune from A1 to A5 within 0.1 cents; as it comes a D and A peak at -7.2 dB; a part renders the same twice and in blocks of 512, 64 and 37 with Strum Rate on; All Sound Off in a fuzzed chord moves the output at most 0.100 a sample (0.111 while it played) and leaves silence 100 ms on; six strings up to 25 cents apart beat, their level climbing up to 1.71 times from one 50 ms to the next, where one string's only falls. The swarm: one oscillator in tune from A1 to A5 within 0.24 cents; a part with glide, converging, drift, Strum Rate and three fuzzed guitars the same twice and in blocks of 512, 64 and 37; a one-second Glide from A2 to A3 at 6 cycles in the first 50 ms and 44 in 0.2 s two seconds on; a 600-cent fan converging over 300 ms alike a period later 0.76 at first and 1.00 two seconds on, and opening from 1.09 to 0.50; Strum Rate pulsing the envelope (0.032 RMS as a pulse begins, nothing 100 ms on), and Strum Sync at 1/16 pulsing every 16th at a session's 90 BPM over Strum Rate's 3 a second; and in a fuzzed swarm the start, a steal, a release and All Sound Off moving the output no more a sample than playing does (0.106), then silence. 296 presets in 11 categories, each named once and setting every parameter, each playing its held E2 and B2 between -11.5 and -7.8 dB; and none of them clicks through a chord, sixteenths over it, restrikes, steals, releases and All Sound Off (no lone jump of 0.03 or more four times the largest step in the 30 ms either side; the nearest, Lucid Glimmer, 1.6 times).
- **Hear them first**: the [Sounds page](docs/sounds/index.html) plays 18 Torqwave Synth patches and 22 StringSwarm presets, each on a four-bar part at 120 BPM, rendered by Torqwave with nothing added after (`TorqwaveTests --sound-demos docs/sounds/audio` writes the 40 MP3s, 7 MB).

**Basic Drums: effects on every pad**
- **Each sound has its own effects**: a **filter** (low-pass, band-pass or high-pass, with Cutoff and Reso), **Crush** (16 bits down to 4, and a sample held for up to 20), and sends to the kit's **Reverb** and **Delay**, after its level and pan.
- **The space**: the kit's reverb (**Size**, a small room to a hall) and a **ping-pong delay** in beats of the session's tempo (1/16 to dotted 1/4; the first echo left, the next right, each darker) with **Feedback**. They go through the bus's drive and glue, and All Sound Off fades them with the hits.
- The kits use them: Dark Techno's clap in a big room and its rim in dotted eighths, Industrial's crushed snares, hats and rim, EBM Machine's big snare reverb, Electro's cowbell and rim echoes, Breaks' crunch, Industrial Rock's room on the snare and toms. Standard has none.
- The window is 1100 by 720: the top row is now Kit, Bus and Space, and the selected sound's effects sit under its knobs (Cutoff and Reso dim while the filter is off).
- Tested (`--drums`, 44 checks): each filter type, cutoff, resonance, crush and both sends change each of the 30 sounds; a snare's reverb rings on after it ends, longer in a bigger room; at 120 bpm an eighth's echo comes at 0.25 s on the left and 0.5 s on the right, a quarter's at 0.5 s; All Sound Off fades the reverb and delay over 3 ms and leaves silence; crush at 100 % holds samples; a pattern in the Industrial kit, effects on, renders bit for bit the same twice, in 37-sample blocks and sample by sample.

**Basic Drums sounds good, with its own window** (0.20.0, step 10 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- **New sounds for all 47 drums.** The kick has a two-stage pitch drop, a click layer, drive and three characters (Boom, Punch, Hard); the snare two tuned body modes, filtered noise and its own transient; the clap four bursts and a short stereo room; the hats six band-limited metallic oscillators through tuned filters; the cymbals ring-modulated metal and longer noise, a little apart side to side; toms and hand drums a pitch drop and a strike. Each note rings on its own voice, so a tom fill or two congas ring over each other. **Stereo out.**
- **Every sound has Tune, Decay (ms), Tone, Level (dB), Pan and Drive**; the kit has **Drive**, **Glue** (a bus compressor) and **Level**. Choke groups: the hats, the triangle and the cuica.
- **Seven kits**: Standard, Dark Techno, Industrial, EBM Machine, Electro, Breaks and Industrial Rock. Standard, where Basic Drums starts, plays at -10 dB (it was -6), so a groove's kick, clap, hat and crash together stay under 0 dB: its demo groove peaks at -4.5 dB, where -6 reached -0.5 dB.
- **Its own window**: a pad for each sound, lit as it plays and played by a click (louder towards the top); the selected sound's hit drawn with its level falling, its knobs with readouts in real units, the kit selector, presets, and the kit's knobs with the glue's gain reduction. In the theme, light or dark. Built on the shared knob kit (SoundControls).
- No clicks: every hit rises from silence and ends falling to nothing; a hit struck again or choked fades over 3 ms, and **All Sound Off now fades** rather than cutting.
- **Older sessions** load: the plug-in is the same, and the old kick, snare, hat and tom tune and decay (in percent) carry over to the nearest settings. They sound different, since the sounds are new.
- Tested (`--drums`, 39 checks): every note in every kit sounds without clipping (peaks 0.19 to 0.62 at full velocity); every note starts from silence (its second sample at most 5.9 % of its peak), ends falling to nothing, and fades when struck again 60 ms in; the choke groups and All Sound Off fade; each of the six settings changes each of the 30 sounds; pan, stereo cymbals and the clap's room; the kick's characters; velocity exact; drive, glue (8.9 dB of reduction at 80 % on a kick) and level; the seven kits and the state; a 0.19.2 state and the factory presets' Machine kit load; a pattern renders bit for bit the same twice, in 37-sample blocks and sample by sample; the window's pads, knobs, picture and kit name. The whole suite passed.

**Every General MIDI drum, no drum clicks, and levels that stand up** (0.20.0, step 8 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md), and fixes from use)
- **Basic Drums plays all 47 General MIDI drums** (35 to 81), adding Chinese cymbal, ride bell, tambourine, splash, vibraslap, high and low bongo, muted, open and low conga, high and low timbale, high and low agogo, short and long whistle, short and long guiro, claves, high and low wood block, muted and open cuica, and muted and open triangle. Before, the Sequencer's rows for them were silent. ([#43](https://github.com/BobClawblaw/torqwave/issues/43))
- A drum struck again while it rings, or an open hat choked, fades out over 3 ms instead of stopping dead, which clicked. ([#44](https://github.com/BobClawblaw/torqwave/issues/44))
- **The Levels panel stands up when docked at a side**: taller than wide, its bars stand side by side and fill from the bottom, with the scale beside them. ([#42](https://github.com/BobClawblaw/torqwave/issues/42))
- Tested (3 more checks): all 47 drums sound on both channels alike, peaking from 0.17 to 0.70 at full velocity, and notes outside them are silent; a conga struck again while it rings moves at most 0.038 a sample where it is struck (0.322 without the fade); a tall Levels panel fills a channel at -6 dB from the bottom and leaves it empty near the top.

**Styles in the guide, and sequences and kits open from Finder** (0.20.0, step 7 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- A **Styles** page in the guide: the techno and industrial styles the presets cover, what marks each, and what every preset keeps. The instruments page starts from a preset, covers Basic Drums and the Basic Synth's additions, and shows the Presets section and a style kit in the Sequencer.
- A built-in instrument's window grows to show all its parameters as far as the screen has room (the synth's twenty no longer scroll on a screen with 900 points under the menu bar); Basic Drums' stays as it was.
- The Sequencer scrolls to a sequence's notes when it opens one after another (the clip kept its id, so a bass opened after a lead was out of view) and when it opens one before it is laid out, as at start-up. ([#41](https://github.com/BobClawblaw/torqwave/issues/41))
- **Double-clicking a .tqseq or .tqkit in Finder** (or Open With, or a drop on the editor) opens it: a sequence in the Sequencer, a factory preset as a new copy; a kit into the session at the cursor, with the session brought in front. The Windows installer registers both kinds of file. ([#40](https://github.com/BobClawblaw/torqwave/issues/40))
- Tested (3 more checks): the synth's window shows all twenty rows where the screen has room, Basic Drums' stays 420 points; a bass preset opened before the Sequencer is laid out, and after a lead an octave and more above it, has all its notes in view (failing without the fix); the guide lists its fourteen pages. The whole suite: 2,271 checks.

**Preview sequences on your own instruments** (0.20.0, step 5 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- A **Preview On** button beside Stop in the Sequence Library: each sequence's own voice (as before; the built-in ones for presets), **the selected track's instrument** (with its effects, as set now), or **a plug-in for each role** (a drum plug-in for Drums, a bass synth for Bass), remembered with the plug-ins chosen. A role with none, a kit on the selected track, or no instrument track selected plays on its own voice. The list says what it plays on, and a new choice while one plays plays it again on that.
- Tested (`--sequences`, 4 more checks): with Basic Drums chosen for Bass (the built-in instruments standing in for plug-ins), a bass sequence renders bit for bit as on that voice and unlike on its own, while a pad and the Own Voice choice keep their own; the player says "on Basic Drums" and renders again on a new choice; on the selected track's instrument it follows the selection, and a kit and no selection keep their own voices; the choice and its plug-ins come back from the settings. The whole suite: 2,269 checks.

**The Sequence Library sorts, groups and filters by style, energy and character** (0.20.0, step 4 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- Sequences can carry a **style** (Techno/Dark), an **energy** (1 to 5), a **character** (Dark or Bright, Sparse or Dense, Straight or Swung, Clean or Distorted) and a one-line **description**, shown under the name; kits a style and description. All optional; older files read and save as they were.
- **Sort** by Tempo, Key and Energy too. **Group** by Role (as before), Style, Key or Energy, each then by role. The plan's "Group by Kit" became Group by Energy, since a kit holds copies of its parts rather than grouping sequences.
- **Filters**: Style (or a whole genre), Energy, Bars, Key, Character, and Presets, Mine or both. **Saved searches** keep a filter set by name, listed above the folders with what each finds now. **Similar** on a sequence's menu shows those of its role, style and key, nearest its energy first.
- **Transpose Drops to the Session's Key** (in Filters, off by default, remembered): a dropped sequence or kit moves the nearer way into the session's key; drums and keyless ones stay as they are.
- **Sequence Properties**: a dialog for all of these, on a sequence's or kit's menu (**Properties...**), as **Properties...** in the Sequencer (an undo step), and in place of the old Add to Sequence Library dialog (with the name and folder).
- Tested (`--sequences`, 10 more checks): each filter, sort and group on a fixed set of entries; a filter set back from its XML, and Similar; saved searches back from their file, replaced and removed; an older sequence read with none of these and written back the same, a newer one keeping them; Sequence Properties written into a sequence and a kit, read by the scan and refused for a preset; the dialog giving back what it was opened with and what was changed; the panel grouping by style, narrowing by filters, showing Similar and a saved search; transposing a drop up and down the nearer way, renaming the key in its own scale, and leaving drums and keyless ones alone. The whole suite: 2,265 checks.

**Factory presets: 160 sequences and 8 starter kits** (0.20.0, steps 3 and 6 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- A **Presets** section at the top of the Sequence Library, by role and sub-role: Drums 30, Bass 30, and Harmony, Pads, Arps, Melody and SFX 20 each, plus a starter kit (groove, bass, stab or chords, lead or arp, fill and build) for each of eight main styles. Weighted towards driving and peak-time techno, dark and industrial techno and EBM; smaller sets for hypnotic, minimal, melodic, acid and hard techno, electro-industrial, futurepop, aggrotech, synth-EBM, darkwave and industrial rock. Original patterns written to each style's conventions and named for the style, never a band or a song.
- All in A minor (Phrygian or harmonic minor where tagged), on the 16th grid, 1 to 8 bars, drums on the General MIDI map, bass mono but for 20-tick acid slides; each with a one-line description (the sequence's note) and a style.
- Each plays on a built-in voice (Basic Drums, or Basic Synth set up for the part); dropped on a track with an instrument it plays on that instrument, as any sequence does.
- Read-only: they ship inside the app and are unpacked into a folder of their own the first time a version runs, so a later version replaces them without touching the user's library. Rated and marked as favourites beside them. **Copy to My Library** and dragging onto a folder make the user's copy; **Edit in Sequencer** opens a new copy that Save puts in the user's folder of the same role. A **Presets** button in the Sequencer opens one the same way.
- Generated by `Scripts/make_presets.py` from compact pattern definitions (16-step grids, step lists, chords by bar) into `Data/Presets`, zipped into the app at build time.
- Basic Drums' hats, cymbals, cowbell and shaker are louder, nearer the kick.
- Tested (`--sequences`, 10 more checks): the app's presets unpack read-only, once, replacing an earlier set; every role has at least 20, and all 208 sequences in the presets and kits keep the rules above, each in its role's folder with a unique name, each kit's six parts at one key and tempo; the script run again writes the same files byte for byte; every preset rendered through its own voice at its tempo peaks between -24 and -1 dB; a preset's voice carries its synth settings (the acid line's resonance, glide and cutoff); the library lists Presets first with every preset, writes nothing to the user's folder, finds them by search and Favourites, and keeps their ratings beside them with the files unchanged; Copy to My Library copies into the user's role folder; Edit in Sequencer opens a new copy that saves into the user's library; the Presets button lists all 160; dropped on an instrument track a preset plays on its instrument, on empty space on its own voice. The whole suite: 2,255 checks.

**Basic Synth: three oscillators, a filter envelope, velocity to filter and glide** (0.20.0, step 2 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- Oscillators 2 and 3, each with its own wave, octave (-2 to +2), detune (±50 cents) and level, for detuned stacks and subs (asked for by the user).
- **Filter Env** (up to six octaves above the cutoff at the note's start) and **Filter Decay**, for plucks, stabs and acid.
- **Velocity to Filter**: softer notes darker, by up to four octaves; full velocity plays the cutoff as set (the 303's accent).
- **Glide**: a note played over a held one slides to it in the glide time without restarting its envelopes (the 303's slide); with it on, overlapping notes play one at a time.
- All four are off by default, so every session and sequence sounds exactly as before, and a synth saved before them loads with them off. Twenty parameters, from eight.
- Tested (`--instruments`, 6 more checks): the synth at its old settings (its defaults, a resonant square with a slow envelope, a triangle swept by CC 74, each playing a chord, a line, a bend, the mod wheel and the pedal) renders bit for bit as 0.19.2 did, checked by checksum in both the arm64 and the Intel halves; oscillator 2 an octave up at 50 % and 3 an octave and 50 cents down at 25 % sound at exactly those pitches and levels; with the filter envelope at 100 %, a sawtooth's 8th harmonic through a 100 Hz cutoff is 47 times as loud in the note's first 100 ms as half a second on, where it is as without it; with velocity to filter at 100 %, a note at velocity 0.25 has 0.026 of a loud note's 16th harmonic against its fundamental; a 100 ms glide slides A3 to A4 without restarting a 200 ms attack, where without glide both sound; an old synth's state loads with the additions off. The whole suite: 2,245 checks.

**Basic Drums, a built-in drum machine** (0.20.0, step 1 of [docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md](docs/PLAN-0.19.3.md))
- A second built-in instrument, offered after Basic Synth: simple synthesised drums on the General MIDI map (kick 35/36, rim 37, snare 38/40, clap 39, closed, pedal and open hat 42/44/46 with the closed and pedal hats choking the open one, toms 41 to 50, crash 49/57, ride 51/59, cowbell 56, shaker 69/70); other notes are silent. Each sound plays to its end whatever the note's length; velocity sets its level. Level, and Tune and Decay for the kick, snare, hats and toms. Kept basic on purpose: it is for hearing patterns; drum plug-ins play the same notes.
- Its noise is seeded per hit, so a pattern renders bit-identically twice and at any block size.
- Tested (`--instruments`, 8 more checks): made from its description as an instrument with no input and a stereo output; each of its 22 notes sounds, the same on both channels, and 9 unmapped notes are silent; velocity scales a kick exactly and a 10 ms note still sounds 100 ms on; a closed hat silences the open hat, which alone still rings 0.5 s on; a whole kit is silent within 6 s; a 16-step pattern renders bit-identically twice and in 37-sample blocks; Kick Tune changes the kick, and the parameters come back with the state; offered as "Basic Drums (built in)" right after Basic Synth. The whole suite: 2,239 checks.

**Ask Torq: a plan's undo steps said plainly**
- A plan with instrument loads, track effect changes, a rack change or renders is headed "The plan:" rather than "The plan, applied as one undo step:", since those are made after Apply, outside its undo step (the notes under the plan say which); after Apply it says "Applied; the notes above say what isn't in its undo step." A plan of edits alone reads as before. The steps name the track after the change ("Add Reverb to the effects on \"Pad\"", "Set Compressor on \"Drums\": ...") instead of a possessive after its quoted name.
- Tried by hand in the app (macOS 27, a local OpenAI-compatible server): on a copy of a session with two third-party instruments, one request added a Reverb to a track, froze another and bounced the first over bars 1 to 3.
