After Apply the Reverb was there, the freeze rendered (7 s, a 3 s clip and its tail), then the bounce (6 s) onto a new track with the original muted, and Undo History listed the plan, Freeze Track and Bounce Track as three steps.

## Released

**Release 0.19.2: crossfading clips laid over each other**
- `Torqwave-0.19.2.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.19.2-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.19.2-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.19.2-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each built by its workflow from the release's tag.
- Fixes 0.19.1's crossfades for clips laid over each other by hand (fix log 65): they popped and could not be crossfaded where they lay. Tried by hand on the session it was found in, after the fix: the overlap crossfades and plays without a pop.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app universal (arm64 and x86_64) at the release's code, signed it, and the whole suite (2,231 checks) passed in each half, the Intel one under Rosetta, with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker. Not run on an Intel Mac itself.
- Not checked on Windows or Linux by hand for this release: the workflows build both packages without running the tests.

**Crossfading clips laid over each other** (fix log 65, [#39](https://github.com/BobClawblaw/torqwave/issues/39))
- Two clips overlapping on a track without a crossfade: a double-click on their overlap makes the whole overlap a crossfade (S-curve both sides, one undo step, "Crossfade Clips"), selects both and opens it in the crossfade editor; before, it opened the clip on top in the editor. Their right-click menu starts with **Crossfade Clips** and **Crossfade and Edit...**.
- **Crossfade Selected Clips** (Shift-X) keeps the whole overlap of clips that overlap; only clips that just touch get 10 ms. Before, it made 10 ms in the middle of any overlap, cutting the clips back.
- Why it mattered: two clips laid over each other without a crossfade click where the upper one starts and pop where the lower one stops dead. Rendering the session it was found in, the stop was a step 184 times the largest the sound otherwise makes; crossfaded (the whole overlap or 10 ms, equal gain or equal power, mixed or layered, rendered at once or in 512-frame blocks), there was no step above the sound's own.
- Tested (`--crossfades`, 7 more checks, 65 in all): on two clips of one file overlapping by 1.68 s, as in the session it was found in, Shift-X crossfades the whole overlap with the clips where they were; a double-click on the overlap makes the crossfade, selects both and opens the editor, not the clip in place, and once crossfaded opens the crossfade, while off the overlap it opens the clip in place; Crossfade Clips does the same without the editor; one clip inside the other is refused with a reason; a 220 Hz sine laid over itself out of phase clicks (a step 844 times the sine's own), and crossfaded, equal gain or power, mixed or layered, rendered in 512-frame blocks, its largest step is 1.38 times the sine's own (equal power on two copies of one sound adds up louder). The right-click menu's two items were not driven by the tests (the menu opens asynchronously); they call the same code as the double-click.


**Release 0.19.1: crossfades in a session**
- `Torqwave-0.19.1.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.19.1-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.19.1-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.19.1-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each built by its workflow from the release's tag.
- Crossfades between overlapping clips on a track, as the first three steps of [docs/PLAN-CROSSFADES.md](docs/PLAN-CROSSFADES.md) built them: Crossfade Selected Clips (Shift-X), crossfades drawn, dragged and shaped on the lane, and the crossfade editor. Steps 4 (default crossfade settings) and 5 (Ask Torq) are still to come.
- Fades now meet exactly: a fade-in is one step further along than before, so a matched pair of fades adds up to full level at every sample. Sessions sound a hair different where a clip fades in (each fade-in sample is one step louder); session files are unchanged.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app universal (arm64 and x86_64) at the release's code, signed it, and the whole suite (2,224 checks) passed in each half, the Intel one under Rosetta, with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker. Not run on an Intel Mac itself. The crossfade editor was looked at in the app on a session; its auditions were not listened to by hand.
- Not checked on Windows or Linux by hand for this release: the workflows build both packages without running the tests.

**Crossfades in a session, step 3: the crossfade editor**
- **Edit Crossfade...** (a crossfade's right-click menu, Multitrack > Editing and Recording, or a double-click on a crossfade, which no longer opens the clip in place there) opens a window for one crossfade, or every crossfade whose clips are selected (else those in the time selection). It draws both clips' sound across the crossfade (out above, in below), both curves and their sum as a dashed line, and says the level at the middle in gain and in power.
- It sets the length (ms), each side's shape, Symmetric, Equal Gain and Equal Power, and presets: four factory ones (Short 10 ms equal gain, Dialogue 40 ms equal power, Music 200 ms equal power, Long 2 s S-curve) and the user's, saved like effect presets. Every change reaches every crossfade it holds at once, and all of them are one undo step (Edit Crossfade); Cancel puts them back, Done or the close button keeps them.
- **Play Out**, **Play In** and **Play Crossfade**, with a pre-roll and post-roll (1 s each to start).
- Unlike the plan, an audition is the clips as their track plays them (gain, fades, volume and pan) without the track's or the master's effects, rendered and played as a file is auditioned; so a change made while one plays is heard on the next press, not at once.
- Tested (`--crossfades`, 12 more checks, 58 in all): a length, Symmetric and one side's shape reach both crossfades of three clips, one undo step; S-curve both sides is 0.000 dB in gain and -3.01 dB in power at the middle, equal power +3.01 and 0.000; the Music factory preset and a saved one of the user's; Play Crossfade's sound is what the mixer plays over the pre-roll, crossfade and post-roll, sample for sample, and Play Out plus Play In add up to it within 1e-6; an audition reaches the player; Cancel puts both back and Done keeps one, two openings two undo steps; a double-click on a crossfade opens it and elsewhere opens the clip in place; Edit Crossfade... with the clips selected opens both. Not driven by the tests: the window opened in the app, its drawing (painted, not looked at by the tests), and an audition heard.

**Crossfades in a session, step 2: on the lane**
- A crossfade shows as a tinted area across the overlap, below the clips' title bars. Drag either edge to set its length (the other edge mirrors it about the join, as far as the clips' audio allows, and never under 2 samples), or its middle to move the join, keeping the length; the pointer is a resize arrow over the edges and a hand over the middle, and the length shows in ms while dragging. Each drag is one undo step (Crossfade Length, Move Crossfade). A crossfade with a locked clip is left alone, and the drag goes to the clip as before.
- Right-click a crossfade for **Equal Gain (S-Curve)**, **Equal Power** (the pair in use ticked), **Out Side** and **In Side** shapes, and **Remove Crossfade**.
- Unlike the plan, the menu has no presets or Edit Crossfade... yet, and a double-click on a crossfade still opens the clip in place: both come with the crossfade editor (step 3).
- Tested (`--crossfades`, 9 more checks, 46 in all), with the tests' mouse on the multitrack view: an edge dragged 20 pixels out moves both edges 20 pixels about the join; the middle dragged 30 pixels moves the join 30 pixels' worth, keeping the length; an edge dragged onto the join leaves a crossfade a pixel or two long; the pointers over the edges and middle; the menu's Equal Power, its tick, an Out Side shape alone and Remove Crossfade, each run as the menu would; with locked clips a drag changes nothing; and the lane painted with a crossfade on it. Not driven by the tests: the look of the area and its length label, and the menu opened by a real right-click.

**Crossfades in a session, step 1** ([docs/PLAN-CROSSFADES.md](docs/PLAN-CROSSFADES.md))
- **Crossfade Selected Clips** (Shift-X, Multitrack > Editing and Recording): selected clips that touch or overlap on a track are crossfaded, each with the next, over 10 ms centred where they meet, S-curve both sides, reaching into the audio past each clip's end, as one undo step. Two whole files that only touch have no audio past their ends to crossfade with, and it says so; clips with a gap, on two tracks, or one inside the other are refused with a reason.
- Underneath, for the lane and the crossfade editor to come: a crossfade is found from the clips (two clips on a track where the out clip's fade out and the in clip's fade in both cover exactly their overlap), and can be lengthened or shortened about its join, moved, given a shape for each side, or removed (the clips then meet at its join), each limited by the clips' audio and their own fades. Sessions store only the two fades, as before.
- **Fades meet exactly:** a session clip's fade in now starts one step above silence (the first frame of a 480-frame fade is at 1/480 of full level, not 0), as the editor's Crossfade Loop already did. The two sides of a crossfade used to sum to one step short of full level throughout, a dip of 0.02 dB for 10 ms at 48 kHz; matched pairs now sum to 1 (S-curve, linear, Fast Start out with Slow Start in) or to 1 in power (Equal Power). Every fade in plays one frame's step differently from before.
- Tested (`--crossfades`, 37 checks): a file split in two and crossfaded into itself over 480 frames plays the file to within 6e-8 with S-curve, linear and Fast/Slow Start pairs, and after the crossfade is removed the two clips play bit for bit as before it; the gains of S-curve and linear pairs sum to 1 within 1.2e-7 at every frame, and equal power within 2.4e-7 in power; two unrelated noises crossfaded with equal power over 2 s are 0.003 dB from either side's level. Lengths keep the join (an odd length too), a move keeps the length and stops where the clips' audio and their own fades allow, 4,800 frames asked for with 100 frames of audio past one clip's end give 200 frames centred on the join, locked clips are left alone, and a session file reads back the crossfade's frames and shapes with nothing new in it. Crossfade Selected Clips was run on the multitrack view with three clips selected: two crossfades, one undo.
- Not tried by hand: Shift-X in the running app.

**Release 0.19.0: sound design in the session, working to picture, the Sequencer and the Idea Board**
- `Torqwave-0.19.0.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.19.0-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.19.0-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.19.0-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each built by its workflow from the release's tag.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app universal (arm64 and x86_64) at the release's code, signed it, and the whole suite (2,166 checks) passed in each half, the Intel one under Rosetta, with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker. Not run on an Intel Mac itself.
- Not checked on Windows or Linux by hand for this release: the workflows build both packages without running the tests (the runners have no audio device). The Windows and Linux builds of the work pushed before the release had failed on a missing `#include <bitset>` in `MidiOut.h`, which the Mac's compiler had let pass; it is fixed in the release.
- The screenshots in the README and the guide were all retaken on this build.

**Sound design in the session**
- **Edit in Place** (double-click a clip, or Option-E): the part of a clip's audio it plays opens in the editor as a file of its own, named "<clip> (Clip)", where every editor tool works. **Save to Clip** (Cmd-S) writes the edit to a new file in the session's recordings folder and makes it a new take of the clip, in use, from the clip's start, as one undo step; what it played before stays a take. Other clips of the same file are untouched. **Back to Clip** (Option-B, the File menu, or a button on the toolbar) returns to the clip in the session; closing the edit with changes offers to put them into the clip first. **Open Source File**, in a clip's menu, opens the whole file as double-clicking used to.
- **Clip Effects...** (a clip's menu, or Multitrack > Clips): a pitch shift of up to an octave either way, keeping the length, and a chain of effects (any effect plug-in or built-in one) on one clip alone, each change an undo step, saved with the session. Sessions without them are written and read as before. The clip's title shows them.
- **Combine to New Sound** renders the selected clips alone, as they play together (their pitch shifts and effects, fades and gain, their tracks' volume, pan, automation and effects), into a new file on a new track under them, muting the layers, as one undo step. **Add to Library...** files a clip's sound, as it plays, in the Sound Library as a 24-bit WAV with its UCS category (guessed from the clip's name) and tags, in the library and in the file's iXML, adding the folder to the library when no watched folder holds it.
- Ask Torq gains `set_clip_effects` (a clip's pitch shift, an effect added to its chain, or none), and `list_clips` shows a clip's effects.
- Unlike the plan, clip effects are not played live on the audio thread: as with audio that follows the tempo, each clip with effects is rendered in the background (pitch first, then the chain, as long as the clip) whenever its audio or effects change, and playback reads the render as clip audio, so it stays allocation-free and the same at any block size. A clip plays dry until its render is ready ("rendering..." on its title); Mix Down, Bounce, Freeze, the stem and region exports, Combine and Add to Library wait for renders. The effects come before the clip's fades, gain line and gain, and a tail stops at the clip's end. A chain that can't be loaded is reported once and the clip plays dry. Recent renders are kept, so an undo is in place at once.
- Also unlike the plan, saving an edit does not use `replaceActiveTake` (which would lose what the clip played): a new edit function adds the edit as a take. The edit may change the clip's length, and a comped clip's comp ends (its takes stay). A clip that follows the tempo opens the part of its original it plays, before stretching, and its edit goes back into that original, which is stretched again; the edit must keep its length (its warp markers are frames of that audio), and saving one of another length is refused with a message saying to play the clip at its own speed first. A locked clip, or one on a frozen track, is refused. Combine leaves out buses and sends, and "pitch" for layers is the clip effects' pitch shift. Ask Torq doesn't edit in place, combine or file sounds, since those write files.
- The editor's start-up reopening of the last file no longer replaces a clip opened in place meanwhile.
- Tested (`--sounddesign`, 28 checks, and Ask Torq's tools under `--ai`):
  - An edit in place of a trimmed clip opens exactly the part it plays; saved shorter and quieter, it becomes take 2 in use from the same start, plays the edit sample for sample, and take 1 plays what it played before from the same frame; another clip of the same file keeps its audio, trim and takes; one undo puts the clip back, redo brings the edit back. A comped clip opens as it plays and its edit becomes take 3. A following clip opens the part of its original it plays (within a frame of 0.5 s in), an edit of another length is refused, one as long goes back into the original with the rest of it bit for bit as it was, and is stretched again to the same length; an edit whose original changed since is refused.
  - A clip shifted -3 semitones through the built-in reverb and compressor plays identically, sample for sample, in blocks of 1, 7, 64, 333 and 4096 frames and in a mixdown, and exactly as a plain clip of its render with its fades and gain. With every effect bypassed and no shift the render is the clip's audio bit for bit. A trim drops the render; undo brings it back.
  - The background renderer put a 1.25 s clip's render in place in 22 to 24 ms on the development Mac (three runs), bit for bit what a render on its own gives, with no undo step and nothing to save; a changed chain is rendered again and an undo has its render back at once; a missing plug-in is reported once and the clip plays dry.
  - Two layers (one shifted +5 semitones, at -6 dB and panned) combine to exactly what the stack plays, other clips left out; landed in the session, it plays the same (worst difference 0).
  - Add to Library: the sound is in the index with its category and three tags, found by tag and category, the iXML holds them, and the file reads back within 1e-6 at 24 bits; the same name again makes a second file; a category not in the UCS list and an empty name are refused.
  - Clip effects (pitch, two slots, one bypassed) round trip through the session file, and a session without them has no trace of them.
  - Double-clicking a clip in the multitrack view was driven by the tests' mouse; the Clip Effects panel's On, Up and Remove buttons were clicked by the tests, each an undo step.
- Not tried by hand, and not driven by the tests: the whole flow in the running app (double-click, the editor's linked file, Save to Clip, Back to Clip and the close prompt in MainComponent), the Clip Effects window with third-party plug-ins and their editor windows, the Add Effect menu, the Add to Library dialog, Combine with plug-in track effects, and Save As on a clip opened in place (it writes a copy; the editor stays linked to the clip).

**Working to picture**
- **Session timecode** (**Multitrack > Picture > Session Timecode...**): a frame rate (23.976, 24, 25, 29.97 drop frame and non-drop, or 30) and the timecode at the session's start, such as 01:00:00:00. **From Video** reads both from the session's video through ffmpeg: the rate from its picture (50, 59.94 and 60 counted at half), drop frame when the video's own timecode is counted so, and the start that has its first frame show its own timecode. A session with no timecode takes its video's when one is attached. With a timecode, the session's clock, time ruler, marker list, clip list, selection times and Go to Time show and take timecode, through midnight, in place of View > Time Format (bars and beats still win where they are shown). It is saved with the session and is an undo step; older sessions open unchanged. View > Time Format gains SMPTE 23.976.
- **Find Cuts** runs ffmpeg's scene detection (score over 0.3) on the session's video and adds a marker, Cut 01 on, at the first frame of each new shot, where the video starts in the session; running it again adds only cuts with no marker yet. **Mark Hit** (Shift-M) adds a marker, Hit 01 on, where the session plays, or at the cursor while stopped.
- **Fit Tempo to Hits...** lists the point markers (those in the time range chosen to start with, or all), and finds either one tempo, between two tempos, that lands the most chosen hits within a frame of a beat or of a bar's first beat with the farthest as near as it can be, or a tempo map with each hit on a bar line. Each hit shows how far off it lands, in ms and frames, and the dialog sums the fit up; Apply is one undo step, and clips on musical tracks keep their beats. Ask Torq gains `fit_tempo_to_hits`, and `get_session` shows the timecode.
- **Spotting:** a sound dropped on a marker (within 6 pixels of its line), from the Sound Library or the Finder, lands with its sync point on the marker: its cue named Sync, else its first cue, else its loudest sample. A sync point further in than the marker leaves the sound's head out at the session's start.
- Unlike the plan, or where it left things open:
  - The tempo map's changes sit at bar starts, so a fitted map puts each hit on a bar line (not on any beat), with the beats between two hits as near the one-tempo fit as whole beats allow; beats left over from whole bars go into a shorter bar before the hit (a pickup when the first hit comes early). A one-tempo fit runs from the session's start, as bar 1 always does, and replaces any tempo changes.
  - Torqwave had no sync point, so it is defined as above. The marker lane still shows names; the marker list shows the timecode.
  - The cut threshold is fixed at 0.3; there is no setting for it.
- Tested:
  - Timecode: 14,125 frame counts through a day at the six rates read back from their text; 7,200 session times from starts of 00:00:00:00, 01:00:00:00 and 23:59:58:00 read back to the first sample of their frame at every rate; an hour from 01:00:00;00 at 29.97 drop frame is 02:00:00;00, and 23.976 is 00:59:56:09 after an hour; frame rates map to their timecode (23.98, 29.97 drop, 50, 59.94; none for 12 or 27). The timecode and hit markers come back from a session file; a session without one writes none and opens as before; setting it undoes.
  - Cuts: in a generated video (2 s of a moving test picture, 1.6 s of colour bars, 2 s of another moving picture), ffmpeg found exactly the two cuts, at 2.000 and 3.600 s, in 80 to 98 ms over the runs, none in the moving shots; the markers land at the video's start plus each cut, and a second run adds none. Generated 25 fps (01:00:00:00), 29.97 drop-frame and 23.976 videos give the session's rate and start.
  - Fit Tempo: six hits tapped up to 6 ms off beats of 97.3 BPM gave 97.304 BPM, the farthest 5.8 ms off (a frame is 40 ms); five on bars of 123.7 gave 123.694, 5.1 ms; six hits at odd times got a tempo map with each on a bar line to the sample (8 changes, 100.0 to 141.5 BPM), and so did the session after Apply, a musical clip keeping its beat and undo giving 120 BPM back; `fit_tempo_to_hits` gave 97.304. The Fit Tempo panel was driven by the tests' mouse: clicking a stray marker out left the five hits fitting at 97.296 BPM, all within a frame.
  - Spotting: a cue named Sync, a first cue and a loudest sample each landed on the marker to the sample, a 44.1 kHz sound converted; one spotted earlier than its sync point started at 0 with its head left out. In the view, files dropped 3 pixels from a marker's line were spotted on it and 40 pixels away dropped where they fell (the drop called as the system's would, not dragged by hand).
- Not tried by hand: none of this has been used on a real film or with a real video's timecode; the Session Timecode and Fit Tempo dialogs were not opened in the app; Find Cuts and Mark Hit were not run from the menus; no sound was dragged from the Sound Library window onto a marker; the ruler's timecode was not looked at.

**Mixer scenes, channel-strip presets and hardware inserts**
- **Mixer scenes:** **Store Scene** in a column on the mixer's left (or **Multitrack > Mixer Scenes and Channel Strips > Store Mixer Scene**) keeps the whole mix as a scene, and its button recalls it, as one undo step; right-click a scene to update, rename or delete it. A scene holds every track's, bus's, folder's and VCA fader's volume, pan, surround pan, mute, solo and polarity, and each track's sends. It holds no effects, automation, routing, groups or automation modes: unlike the plan's "plug-in states", effect settings live outside the undo steps, so they are left to channel-strip presets. Scenes are saved with the session; older sessions open unchanged. Ask Torq gains `store_scene` and `recall_scene`, and `get_session` lists the scenes.
- **Channel-strip presets:** **Save Channel Strip...** and **Apply Channel Strip** (a mixer strip's right-click menu, or the Multitrack menu for the selected track) save a track's effects, sends, volume, pan, polarity and output by name and put them onto a track that is already there, building on track templates (any `.sbtrack` file applies too; templates now keep the polarity). The preset's effects replace the track's; an instrument track without an instrument in the preset keeps its own. Deviation: the settings, sends and output are one undo step, but the effects are not, as no change of effects is an undo step in Torqwave.
- **Hardware inserts:** **Hardware Insert**, a new built-in effect, sends a track's audio out of an interface output pair and back in from an input pair. **Ping** times a click's round trip through the interface and the gear; the insert reports it (plus a guard of 64 frames for the player reading ahead) as its latency, so delay compensation lines it up. The insert counts its frames on the device's clock inside each device cycle, so the round trip stays exact however its audio is split into blocks. Away from the device (mixdowns, bounces, freezes, stems, or the device at another rate) it passes the audio through, delayed by its latency. Nothing on the audio thread allocates or locks: the rings are made when the app starts.
- Retrospective audio recording was not built, as planned.
- The Sound Library guide said Return inserts a sound into the editor; it inserts it into the session, on the selected track at the cursor. The guide now says so.
- Tested:
  - A stored scene recalls every volume, pan, surround pan, mute, solo, polarity and send exactly, and recalling it again changes nothing; tracks added since keep their mix, and sends to deleted buses are left out. A recall is one undo step, undone and redone exactly. Scenes come back from a session file, naming the loaded tracks, and recall exactly.
  - In the mixer, Store Scene, a scene's button, and its menu's Update, Rename and Delete, each an undo step (buttons clicked with the tests' `triggerClick`, menu items run as the menu would). Ask Torq stores and recalls a scene by name and refuses an unknown one.
  - A preset applied to a track with an EQ and a send of its own gives it the preset's compressor (its threshold of -30 dB kept) and reverb, its send and output by bus name, its volume, pan and polarity, keeping its name and colour; an undo gives the settings back. An instrument track keeps its Basic Synth first. Saved and applied from a mixer strip's menu.
  - Through a simulated interface with outputs 3/4 looped back into inputs 3/4 (driven through the same wrapper the engine runs the player in), the ping measures round trips of 300, 1,013, 4,000 and 64 frames exactly at blocks of 128, 441, 1,000 and 64 frames; with nothing coming back the ping fails. With the insert on one track, the mix of two noise tracks is identical, sample for sample, to the same session without the insert, at blocks of 64, 100, 256, 441, 512 and 1,000 frames and at random sizes from 32 to 1,000. Reading the return one frame off makes that test fail. Away from the device the audio passes through delayed by exactly the latency. The round trip is saved with its sample rate.
  - Not tested: the audio thread allocating nothing (by construction, not measured); the engine turning the inserts' channels on in a real device.
  - Not tried by hand: hardware inserts with a real interface and outboard gear (so not the real round trip or the ping's click through real converters), scenes and channel strips in the app on a real mix, and the menus' dialogs (naming a preset, renaming a scene).

**Audio that follows the tempo**
- An audio clip can **follow the tempo** (a clip's menu, **Tempo > Follow Tempo...**): it is given its own tempo, guessed from its name ("loop 120bpm"), then from its attacks, and is stretched so each of its beats plays on the session's beats, through tempo changes in the tempo map, a moved clip, or a session tempo changed later. **Warp markers** pin frames of the clip to beats: **Warp Markers at Transients** puts them on its attacks, **Quantize Warp Markers** moves them to the grid, and a marker drags along the clip (snapping when snap is on), Option-click removes one. Between markers the audio is stretched in proportion; past the last it plays at its own tempo. **Slice at Transients** cuts a clip at its attacks. **Play at Its Own Speed** turns following off and gives back the original. Tempo, markers and the clip's place are saved with the session; older sessions open unchanged.
- Unlike the plan, the audio is not stretched in real time on the audio thread: each following clip is stretched in the background (Signalsmith Stretch, each stretch between two points of its map at one ratio, crossfaded over 5 ms before each point) whenever its tempo, its markers, its place or the tempo map under it changes, and plays its last stretch meanwhile ("stretching..." shows on the clip). Playback reads a stretched clip as any other clip, so it stays sample for sample at any block size, and mixdowns, bounces, freezes and stem exports wait until every clip is stretched. Recent stretches are kept, so an undo is in place at once.
- Tested: a 100 BPM click track in a 120 BPM session plays each click within 0.29 ms of the session's beats; at 90 BPM within 0.33 ms; through a change from 120 to 90 BPM inside the clip within 0.38 ms; moved to bar 2 within 0.56 ms; a click 30 ms late pinned by a warp marker within 0.48 ms, with its neighbours on theirs. The tempo comes back from a session file and is stretched again to the same samples; tempos are read from names and detected from click tracks (100.0 and 132.0); slices start within 5 ms of each click; the background stretcher puts a clip in place without an undo step or a change to save. Not yet tried by hand on real loops, and dragging warp markers was not driven by the tests' mouse.

**Comping**
- **Comping** takes, as in Cubase and Logic: **Show Takes** (Multitrack > Clips, or a clip's menu) opens a lane per take under a track, and a drag across a take's lane makes the clip play that take over that range, as one undo step. The clip plays a different take in each part, joined by an equal-power crossfade, 10 ms by default (shortened where joins are closer). A take's name plays it throughout. The comp stays with the sound through moving, trimming, splitting, reversing, stretching, pitch shifting and choosing another take, and is saved with the session; older sessions open unchanged. **Flatten Comp** renders it into a new take, from a new file. Ask Torq gains a `comp_takes` tool. Tested: a comp of three takes plays each take sample for sample over its part, at any block size, and the crossfades are the equal-power mix to within 1e-6; flattening plays exactly what the comp did, fades and gain line included; the comp comes back from a session file and undoes as one step; Normalize goes by the loudest part the comp plays; in the view, a drag across a take lane and a click on a take's name were driven by the tests' mouse. Not yet tried by hand with a loop recording.

**Arranger**
- An **Arranger** strip in the multitrack view, under the marker lane: named, coloured sections (Intro, Verse, Chorus...) across every track. Drag on the strip to draw one, double-click it to rename it. Dragging a section moves everything in its time on every track (clips, notes, automation, markers, and its tempo and time-signature changes when it is whole bars): the gap closes and room opens where it lands. Option-drag or **Duplicate** copies it; **Insert Bars Before...** puts empty bars in at the tempo there; **Delete Section and Its Time** closes the gap. An **arrangement chain** sets a play order in which sections can repeat; **Play Arrangement** makes playback follow it, and **Flatten Arrangement** lays the session out in that order. Sections and the chain are saved with the session; older sessions open unchanged.
- Delete Time, Insert Time, Copy Time and Paste Time carry tempo and time-signature changes when their edges are on bar lines, and carry sections, as they do markers. Unlike the plan, they leave the session's loop where it is: the loop is kept outside undo, so carrying it would leave it moved after an undo.
- Ask Torq gains five session tools: `add_section`, `move_section` (with `copy`, "double the chorus"), `delete_section`, `change_section_bars` ("make the intro 4 bars shorter") and `set_arrangement`; `get_session` lists the sections, the chain and the tempo changes.
- Tested: moving, copying and deleting a section carries clips, notes, envelopes, markers and tempo changes exactly, with undo, and locked clips stay; a chain plays the right frames of each section at any block size, jumping inside a block; flattening gives the chain's order with each section's tempo; sections and the chain come back from a session file; in the view, drawing, moving, copying and sizing sections were driven by the tests' mouse. Not yet tried by hand on a song.

**Automation modes and VCA faders**
- **Automation modes** for each track and bus, as in Cubase: Off, Read, Touch, Latch and Write, chosen from the track's **A** button (which shows the mode) or for every track at once from **Multitrack > Automation**. Off ignores the envelopes; Read plays them; Touch writes while a control is held; Latch goes on writing the last value until playback stops; Write writes the volume and pan from play to stop, moved or not. Several tracks write at once, each pass an undo step. The modes are saved with the session and, like the loop, are not undo steps. **Write Automation** (W) now puts every track in Touch, or back to Read.
- The **mixer's** faders and pan knobs write automation too, as the track headers do.
- **VCA faders:** a track kind with no audio that tracks and buses join (**Multitrack > Tracks > Add VCA Fader**, or a header's **VCA** menu). Its volume, and its volume envelope, are added to each member's in dB as they are mixed, without summing them; its mute and solo act on its members; bounces, freezes and stems keep its level. Ask Torq's `get_session` shows a track's VCA fader and mode.
- A value written where a held one ends now steps there, rather than ramping from where the hold began.
- Tested: Off plays the setting, sample for sample; Touch, Latch and Write each write what they should over a scripted pass, Write from where playback began; two tracks write at once, and each pass undoes on its own; an undo, or an older state applied, leaves the modes; the mixer's fader writes while playing in Touch; a member of a VCA fader at -3 dB plays sample for sample as at -9 dB, its envelope moves its members within 1e-6, a bus joins one, mute and solo follow it, and membership and modes come back from a session file. Not yet tried by hand on a mix.

**Drum grid, swing, Capture MIDI and MIDI channels**
- A **drum grid** in the Piano Roll (the **Drums** button): a row for each drum sound and a column for each grid step. Click a step to set or clear it, and drag up or down from it for velocity. The rows come from the track's drum map: General MIDI's, or the track's own, edited by right-clicking a name and saved with the session. Notes outside the map get rows of their own.
- **Swing**, 50 to 75 %, on a 1/8 or 1/16 grid: in the Piano Roll (its lines, snapping and the drum grid's steps) and in **Quantize Notes...**, which also gains **Strength** and **Starts and ends**.
- **Capture MIDI** (Option-Shift-R): the last 60 seconds of MIDI played in, while playing or stopped, become a note clip. Notes played while the session played land where they were played. Notes played while stopped start at the cursor.
- **MIDI channels:** a note clip's notes and controller lanes go out on its channel, 1 to 16, set by the Piano Roll's **Ch** choice and saved with the session.
- Tested:
  - The grid puts in exactly the notes a clicked pattern means; clicking, Option-clicking and dragging change steps as they should.
  - Swing at 66 % puts the off-beat line at 66 % of its pair, in the drum grid, in the Piano Roll's snapping and in Quantize Notes; strength and lengths move notes by what they should.
  - A capture of notes played while stopped keeps their times from the cursor, at the session's rate; one played while playing keeps their session frames.
  - A clip's notes and lane values go out on its channel.
  - Drum maps, the drum grid choice and channels come back from a session file.
  - The grid was driven by the tests' mouse.
  - Not yet tried by hand with a drum instrument or a keyboard.

**MIDI out**
- **External instruments:** a track's **MIDI Out** (in its instrument button's menu) plays its note clips, controller lanes and live input on a hardware synth through a MIDI output port. Each clip goes on its own channel, or everything on one. An offset of up to 500 ms either way lines the synth up. The messages are timed to leave when their audio is heard, allowing for the rack's and the interface's output latency, from a thread of their own so the audio thread never waits on a port. Stopping, or a loop going round, ends the notes left on.
- **MIDI from plug-ins:** **MIDI From** has a track's instrument play the MIDI another track's plug-in puts out (an arpeggiator, chord or MIDI effect plug-in), as well as its own notes. MIDI effect plug-ins now count as instruments.
- **MIDI clock and transport out** (**Multitrack > MIDI Clock Out**): 24 clocks a beat on the tempo map, with Song Position, Start, Continue and Stop.
- Tested:
  - Through a virtual MIDI port, the port receives exactly a clip's messages on its channel, each within 5 ms of its time (1.4 ms on the development Mac).
  - A track's channel and offset apply.
  - Stopping and a loop jump end held notes; live input goes out while stopped; nothing goes out while the session is resampled.
  - Clock: Song Position 0 and Start, then 24 clocks a beat within 5 ms (1.5 ms measured); a jump sends Stop, Song Position and Continue; stopping sends Stop.
  - A chord plug-in's notes reach the track taking its MIDI at their frames, or a run late when that track is above its source.
  - Ports, channels, offsets and MIDI From come back from a session file.
  - Not yet tried by hand with a hardware synth or a real arpeggiator plug-in. The virtual-port tests run on macOS and Linux only.

**Control surfaces and MIDI learn**
- **Mackie Control** (**Multitrack > Controllers > Control Surface...**): a Mackie Control Universal, or a surface in its MCU mode, works the session's tracks and buses in banks of eight.
  - Its motorised faders set and follow the volume, and its envelope as the session plays. Its V-pots pan.
  - Its mute, solo, select and record-arm buttons act on their strips, and their lights stay in step.
  - Bank and channel buttons move the strips. The transport buttons play, stop and record; rewind and fast forward go to the start and end.
  - The scribble strips show each track's name and its volume, or its pan while a V-pot turns.
  - The surface's input no longer plays the instruments: it is turned off among the live MIDI inputs, and stays off if the surface is later dropped (Settings turns it back on).
  - Unlike the plan, HUI is not supported, and the master fader does nothing, as the session has no master volume.
- **MIDI learn:** any controller's CC can move a track's volume, pan, mute or solo (**MIDI Learn** in a track header's or mixer strip's menu), or play, stop and record (**Multitrack > Controllers**). Each control has one CC and each CC one control. Bindings are saved with the session, each learned one is an undo step, and a learned CC no longer reaches the instruments. Relative (endless-encoder) knobs are not supported.
- Both write automation as the mixer's controls do: in Touch, Latch or Write, a held fader writes; a learned knob ends its pass half a second after it last moved.
- Tested:
  - The Mackie messages, byte for byte: 14-bit faders on the mixer's taper (there and back within 0.001 dB), buttons and LEDs, V-pots and their rings, the scribble SysEx.
  - A simulated surface on virtual MIDI ports, both ways: what it shows on opening; a fader moving the volume as one undo step; mute, solo, select and arm, and their LEDs; banks; the transport; a V-pot panning, shown on its ring and strip, and centred when pushed; a motorised fader following an envelope but not while touched; writing a volume envelope in Touch; and everything left dark and down on closing.
  - MIDI learn: binding, volume and pan scales, buttons toggling at each press, the transport, which CCs are taken, one CC per control, the session file, and removing a track dropping its bindings.
  - Not tested: that the surface's input stops playing the instruments. Not yet tried by hand with a hardware surface or controller. The surface tests run on macOS and Linux only.

**Ask Torq: a list of servers**
- **Settings > AI** keeps a list of endpoints, any servers that speak the OpenAI-compatible API, each with its own name, address, model and key. Choose one under **Endpoint** to use it, **Add** another, or **Delete** the one shown (its saved key goes with it unless another endpoint shares its address). The server set in an earlier version comes back as the first endpoint, "Server 1".
- Tested: the earlier setting comes back as a list of one; added servers keep their own address and model; choosing one uses its address, model and key; the list and the one in use come back from the settings; deleting before, at and after the one in use, down to none, which turns AI off; on the settings page, Add, typing a name and address, choosing and Delete, driven by the tests. Not yet tried by hand against two real servers.

**Ask Torq: track chains, effect automation, freeze and bounce**
- Ask Torq on a session works a track's whole chain and renders: `remove_track_effect`, `move_track_effect` and `add_track_effect` at a place among the effects; `list_effect_parameters` reads an effect's or the instrument's parameters (id, name, value, how it reads, its range, a stepped parameter's choices) and `set_track_effect` switches an effect off or sets parameters, by a value from 0 to 1 or by text as the plug-in reads it; `set_automation` also draws an effect's or instrument's parameter; `freeze_track` freezes or unfreezes a track and `bounce_track` bounces one, over a range or whole. Chain changes and settings are made once the plan is applied, outside its undo step, as instruments are; automation and unfreezing are in the undo step; freezes and bounces render after everything else in the plan, one after another, each its own undo step, as the menu commands do. Each chain is read with its parameters when the request starts, as the tools run off the message thread, so an effect the plan adds can be set or automated only in a later request. Taking an effect off takes its automation with it. `get_session` numbers each track's effects and names automated parameters.
  - Tested (`--ai`): the tools against a made-up chain (settings by value and by a stepped parameter's choice, refusals that leave the plan as it was, a place past the end, an effect added in the plan refused settings, automation drawn and removed with its effect, a freeze planned and taken back, an unfreeze, a bounce range); and on the engine's live chain with the built-in effects: a chain read with its parameters, then a plan made on it (a Limiter added first, the Reverb taken off, the Compressor switched off and its threshold set from "-24 dB"), and on an instrument track a Reverb put first among the effects after Basic Synth, a synth parameter set, the instrument refused removal. Not driven by the tests or tried by hand: the freezes and bounces queued after Apply in the running app (they call the same code as Bounce Track and Freeze Track, now by track id), and third-party plug-ins' parameters.

**The Sequence Library** (0.20 plan, step 1: [docs/PLAN-0.20.md](docs/PLAN-0.20.md))
- **Sequences** (`.tqseq`): a MIDI part kept with its voice, meaning the instrument (with its settings), effects, volume, pan and colour of the track it came from. Its notes and controller lanes are in beats (960 ticks a beat), so it lands on the same beats at any session tempo, through tempo changes too, with nothing stretched. Its loop, its offset and its MIDI channel go with it. The voice leaves out what belongs to a session: sends, output, inputs and automation.
- **Add to Sequence Library...** (a note clip's menu, or Multitrack > Clips) saves the clip with its track's voice, a name, a folder, a key and tags.
- **Window > Sequence Library:** the library's folders as a tree. The first two levels are fixed roles (Drums, Bass, Harmony, Pads, Arps, Melody, SFX, Kits and Inbox) and their sub-roles; below them, folders of your own. It shows each sequence's bars and key and whether it has an instrument, and it has a search box. The files live under Music > Torqwave > Sequences, and the panel follows changes made in the Finder.
- **Drop a sequence** into the multitrack view, from the panel or the Finder: on empty space or an audio track it makes a new instrument track from its voice, and its plug-ins load in the background; on an instrument track it adds the notes only, or with Option a new track. A double-click puts it on the selected track at the cursor. Each drop is one undo step.
- **New Sequence:** **New...** in the Sequence Library asks for a name, folder (the one selected), instrument, length in bars and key, then makes an instrument track with that instrument and an empty clip of that many bars from the cursor's bar, and opens it in the Piano Roll. **Add to Sequence Library...** on that clip comes filled in, and saving a clip again with the same name and folder replaces the file it saved before (remembered until you quit). The Multitrack workspace now has the Sequence Library as a tab beside the Track Inspector. Tested: the new track is an instrument track named for it with an empty clip of whole bars from the start of the cursor's bar, and through a tempo change the length follows the map. Not tried by hand: the dialog, the instrument loading on the new track, the Piano Roll opening on it, and saving over the file.
- **Folders in the Sequence Library:** every role's sub-role folders show, empty ones too (before, a folder showed only once a sequence was in it). Right-click a folder for **New Folder...** and **New Sequence Here...**, Show in Finder, and Rename and Move to Trash for your own folders (the fixed roles and sub-roles stay). Your folders, at any depth, are in the dialogs' Folder lists and sequences save into them. Tested: the folder list holds every role and sub-role with the user's folders right under their parents; the panel shows the empty sub-roles and the user's folders; a folder is made once and then refused; the fixed folders are told from the user's. Not tried by hand: the right-click menu, renaming and trashing a folder.
- **Drag sequences between folders** in the Sequence Library: dropped on a folder, a sequence moves there (Option copies it) and takes that folder's role and sub-role; `.tqseq` files from the Finder are copied in. A name already used there gets a number. Tested: a move leaves the old folder, sets the role and sub-role, picks a free name and keeps the notes; a copy keeps the original; a move to the same folder changes nothing. Not tried by hand: the drag itself, in the panel and from the Finder.
- **Hear a sequence before using it** (step 2): a click in the Sequence Library plays it through its own instrument and effects at the session's tempo, looping, in time with the session's next bar while it plays. It is rendered on a thread of its own as a drop would play it (the pass after a first one, so what rings past the end comes in at the start of the loop), and the last eight renders are kept, so going back to one plays at once. Up and Down move through the list, playing each; a second click or Stop stops it, and so does hiding the panel. One with no instrument says why it can't be heard. Tested: frames to the next bar, through a change to 3/4; two bars through the built-in synth render to the right length at 120 and 60 BPM and sound from the start; the player renders, plays and is heard, stops to silence, plays a second time from what was rendered, and says why one with no instrument is silent. Not tried by hand: the preview with third-party instruments, and its timing against a playing session.
- **The Sequencer window** (step 3; **Window > Sequencer**, **Edit in Sequencer** on a sequence's menu in the library, and a **Sequencer** workspace with the library on its left): one sequence open by itself. The Piano Roll, drum grid and controller lanes edit the sequence file, with undo steps of the window's own. It plays through its voice beside the session, with **Play**, **Loop**, a **Click** on each beat, and a tempo of its own (from the one it was written at) or the session's. The voice is down the right: change the instrument, add and remove effects, open their windows. **New**, **Open**, **Save**, **Save As**, **Duplicate** and **Make Variation** work on library files; a variation names the sequence it came from (a new `variationOf` attribute in the file), and asking about unsaved changes comes before opening another. Changes to the voice are kept outside the undo steps, as track effects are; a voice that didn't all load is saved as the file had it. Tested: a note added at 128 BPM (23.4375 frames a tick) and saved reads back exactly the sequence with that note in ticks, its other notes, lanes and voice untouched, and undo and redo step through it; Make Variation saves "Groove Variation" beside it naming Groove, with its notes and voice, and Duplicate of that saves "Groove Variation 2"; through the built-in synth, a note on beat 2 is first heard one frame after the beat at 90 and at 140 BPM (the synth's sine starts at 0), a loop is back at its start after a bar, and the click sounds from the first beat. Not tried by hand: none of the window was used in the running app (its buttons, dialogs, the instrument and effect menus, plug-in windows, Session tempo, Space and Cmd-Z, the playhead clock), it was not heard through the audio device, and a third-party instrument was not loaded in it. Drum maps edited in the window are not saved (a voice has no drum map), so the drum grid's map can't be changed there.
- **The writing tools** (step 4), in the Piano Roll's new **Tools** menu (in the session and in the Sequencer), each one undo step on the selected notes or all of them: **Chords...** from Roman numerals in a key (case for major and minor; 7, maj7, dim or °, ø or m7b5, aug or +, sus2, sus4; b or # for borrowed roots; a beat to two bars each, in place of the notes or added); **Arpeggiate...** (up, down, up and down, random; one to four octaves; 1/2 to 1/8 beat; note length 50 to 100 %); **Humanize...** (timing up to 40 ms, velocity up to 30 %); **Fit to the Key**; **Scale Lock** (clicks, drags and the arrow keys stay in the key, rows outside it shaded); **Key...** (twelve scales). The key is the part's when it has one, or guessed from the notes. **Note probability:** right-click a note or a drum step for **Probability** (100 % to 10 %) and **Velocity**; a note plays on a pass by a draw fixed by the clip, the pass and the note, so playback, Mix Down, bounces and MIDI out agree at any block size. It is saved in sessions (a `probability` attribute, only below 1) and sequences (`pr`); older files open unchanged. With swing and velocity, which the drum grid had, that makes it the step sequencer. **Make Variation...** in the Sequencer now asks for changes: velocity, notes moved within the key, and density thinner or denser, by chance. Tested (`--sequences`): keys read and refused, snapping and steps in C major; keys guessed from notes; i–VI–III–VII in A minor and ii7 V7 Imaj7 vii° bVII Vsus4 viiø7 viidim7 vii°7 in C give the right pitches, and VIII, an unknown suffix and a pentatonic key are refused with the reason; the arpeggiator's four orders and two octaves, note for note, with random repeatable for a seed and never repeating a pitch; humanize within its amounts on the chosen notes only; a variation's velocity and pitch within their amounts, density filling every empty step at +1 and keeping one note at -1; a 50 % note played 200 times in 400 passes, on the same passes in blocks of 4096 and 333 frames, 100 % all and 0 % none, and probability kept by session and sequence files; the Piano Roll's Chords, Probability and Arpeggiate through its callbacks, each one undo step; and a Sequencer variation at density +1 filling every sixteenth of a bar of hats. Unlike the plan, the arpeggiator works on drawn (or recorded) chords only, not on chords held live on a keyboard, and the step sequencer is the drum grid with probability added rather than a new view. Not tried by hand: none of the Tools menu, its dialogs, the right-click menus, Scale Lock's dragging and shading, or the Make Variation dialog was used in the running app, and probability was not heard through an instrument.
- **The Idea Board** (step 5; **Window > Idea Board**, and under the Sequencer in the **Sequencer** workspace): a grid of up to eight parts (rows) and eight scenes (columns), each cell a sequence file from the library, dropped from the library or the Finder or chosen. A click launches a cell, at once when nothing plays, else on the next bar in place of its part's other cell; a click on a playing cell stops it on the bar; a scene's name launches the scene, a part's name stops the part; **Stop All**. Cells play through their own voices on a free-running player beside the session, at the session's tempo (taken where its playhead is when the board starts) or the board's own. **Send to Session** lays the scenes out from the cursor's bar as arranger sections named for them (each as many whole bars as its longest sequence, empty scenes left out), a track per part from its first sequence's voice (another beside it for another voice), each sequence a repeating clip filling its section, as one undo step. Boards are a new file type (`.tqboard`, XML naming the sequence files, relative to the library when in it) kept in a Boards folder beside the library, saved as they change; the last one opens on start. A sequence without a loop repeats whole from its start. Tested (`--sequences`): the next bar at 120 BPM (96000 from 30000 with the launch margin); a board saved and read back, cells relative to the library; removing a part moves the cells under it; launches while stopped and playing, the part's other cell stopping on the bar, queued, playing, stopping and idle states, a scene launch and a part stop on the next bar; through the built-in synth, a bass launched at frame 30000 while playing is first heard at frame 96001 (the bar is 96000) and the clock runs on past the sequences' ends; Send to Session from frame 30000 makes the Verse and Chorus sections (two bars each, from bar 2), the empty scene left out, three tracks named for the parts and five looping clips filling their sections, and one undo takes it all away. Not tried by hand: the Idea Board panel was not used in the running app (clicks, menus, drops from the library and the Finder, New, Open, the tempo controls, the playing and queued colours), nothing was heard through the audio device, Send to Session was not run from its button, and a third-party instrument was not loaded on the board. The panel itself is not driven by the tests (it opens the user's Boards folder).
- **Kits, MIDI files and packs** (step 6), in the Sequence Library. **Kits** (`.tqkit`): select sequences and choose **Make Kit from Selected...**, or **Save as Kit...** on a folder (its sequences and those below it); the kit is one file in Kits holding every part whole with its notes and voice, its key the one its parts agree on. Dropped into the session (from the library or the Finder) or double-clicked, it makes a folder track named for it and a track for each part inside, each with its instrument and effects, as one undo step; a click in the library plays all its parts together. **MIDI files:** **Export MIDI File...** on a sequence (or a kit, a track a part) writes a type 1 Standard MIDI File at 960 ticks a beat with the notes as they play (a loop written out), the controller lanes, channel, name, written tempo, time signature and key; **Import... > MIDI Files...** (or **Import MIDI Files Here...** on a folder) makes a sequence of each track with notes, in the folder chosen, playing on a known instrument at its default settings or on none. **Packs** (`.tqpack`, a zip): **Save as Pack...** on a folder; **Import... > Pack...**, or a pack dropped on a folder, opens it with each sequence going to the folder of its role and sub-role (the Inbox for none or an unknown one), kits to Kits, a clashing name numbered and anything else skipped. **Ratings** (0 to 5; keys 0 to 5) and **favourites** (F), kept as attributes in each sequence's or kit's own file (with the time it was added) rather than in an index, so they travel with the file through the Finder, moves and packs; older files open unchanged. **Sorting** by name, bars, rating or newest, a **Favourites** filter and **Fits the Session**: the sequences in the session's key, and those with no key. **The session's key** is new: set in Multitrack > Tempo > Tempo..., an undo step, saved in the session file as a `key` attribute only when set, so older sessions open unchanged with none. **Settings > Sequence Library** chooses the library's folder. Tested (`--sequences`, 16 new checks): a kit of three parts saved and scanned (3 parts, 4 bars, key E minor from E minor and Em, none from E minor and G major); dropped into the engine's session it makes a folder and 3 tracks in it at bar 3, 4 tracks added and taken away by one undo ("Add Kit Night Drive") and brought back by redo, each part's basic synth loaded; the kit's preview renders its three parts together for 4 bars (384000 frames at 120 BPM); a sequence with a loop and an offset exported and imported again keeps its 17 played notes to the tick (velocity within 1/127), its name, channel 3, 96 BPM, 3/4, Bb major and mod wheel lane; a repeated note stays two notes; a kit's three parts imported into Bass > Sub with the synth, a clash numbered and an unreadable file reported; a Drums pack of 2 sequences and a Kits pack opened into another library by role and sub-role (Kick 2 on a clash), an unknown role to the Inbox and a text file skipped, an empty folder refused; ratings and favourites written without changing the notes or the added time; the four orders; the key filter (Am, no key, A dorian); the panel's Fits the Session, rating order, Favourites, and the 1 and F keys keeping the selection; the chosen folder; and the session key read, normalised, refused, saved, loaded, absent from older files, and undone and redone. Unlike the plan, the key is set in the Tempo dialog, not the Track Inspector; Fits the Session also shows sequences with no key; and a pack opens by role and sub-role, so the user's own folders inside a sub-role are not recreated. Not tried by hand: nothing of this was used in the running app: the panel's new row, menus and keys, the Make Kit, Import MIDI, Export MIDI, Save as Pack and Open Pack dialogs and file choosers, dragging a kit or a pack from the Finder, hearing a kit's preview, the Settings row, and the Tempo dialog's Key box.
- **Ask Torq writes and reviews sequences** (step 7, experimental): an **Ask Torq** button in the Sequencer opens Ask Torq on the sequence there, with three new tools that take and give the sequence as JSON in ticks (pitch, start, length, velocity, probability; lanes as a controller and points), with its role, key, beats in a bar, length, the voice's instrument (and what the plug-in catalogue says it is), its parameters and effects, the Sequencer's tempo and, with a session open, the session's tempo and key. **get_sequence** reads it and lists the notes outside its key. **write_sequence** writes notes and lanes (and, if asked, the length, loop and key), checked (pitches 0 to 127, notes starting and ending inside the length or loop, velocities 1 to 127, controllers 0 to 127 or pitch bend, lane values 0 to 1, a key that parses) and refused with the reason otherwise; the plan's **Apply** puts it into the Sequencer as one undo step of its own history, exactly as written. **suggest_for_sequence** keeps a short review and up to 12 suggestions, shown in the Ask Torq window one a row with **Apply**, and **Apply All**: note edits (each note found by its pitch and start), controller lanes, and an effect for the voice; each Apply, and Apply All, is one undo step in the Sequencer. Under every review, Torqwave itself names the notes outside the key with where they are ("Outside A minor: F#3 at bar 1, beat 3"). An effect added by a suggestion is an undo step (the voice before it is loaded again on Undo), unlike one added with **+ Effect**. **Not built as planned:** automation of the instrument's parameters can't be kept in a `.tqseq` (the voice is a track template without automation, and an envelope names its plug-in by a slot id known only once the voice is loaded), so an automation suggestion is shown as advice, marked **Advice**, to draw on the track once the sequence is in a session, and the model is told to suggest a controller lane instead where the instrument answers one. The AI eval set has four sequence requests (`--ai-eval-set sequence`: broken-beat hats, an i-VI-III-VII pad progression, a review of a bass line that should name its F#, and an effect for an arp). How good the music is depends on the model, and the guide says so. Tested (`--ai`, 12 checks against the mock server and the tools): the five tools offered and the system message naming the part's role, key, instrument and both tempos; get_sequence naming F#3 and G#2 outside A minor; nine bad writes refused with their reasons, nothing written; six notes and a CC 74 lane written exactly (velocity n / 127, probability 0.5 kept); four bad suggestions refused; a review of four suggestions kept apart from the plan; a suggestion applied by itself (its velocity kept exactly), all four together (Chorus in the voice), and refused when its note is gone; then through the Ask window and a real Sequencer: nothing changing before Apply, the written notes and lane landing as one undo step ("Ask Torq: Two bars of bass in A minor") that Undo takes back whole and Redo restores; a review showing "Outside A minor: F#3 at bar 1, beat 3." and four rows with Apply All; three suggestions applied one at a time as three undo steps (the note, a mod wheel lane, Chorus loaded into the voice after the Basic Synth), the automation one staying advice, and each undone, Chorus taken out of the loaded chain; and Apply All as one step ("Ask Torq: 3 suggestions"), undone and redone whole. **Not tried by hand:** the Ask Torq button in the running app's Sequencer, the suggestion rows and their layout, the window's prompt when opened from the Sequencer, and an effect suggestion's Undo with its plug-in window open were not used; no real local model was run on the new tools, and the four eval requests were not run against a model.
- **From the first try of steps 3 to 7:**
  - **The first note of a sequence was never heard** when the Sequencer started playing, nor the first note of each pass of its loop (nor a cell launched on a stopped Idea Board): starting and looping told the instrument the play had ended, which leaves out the next block's notes and a lane's first value. Starting now resets the instrument and plays the first block; a loop's end turns the held notes off and plays the new pass from its start. The session's transport was not affected.
  - **Basic Synth answers controllers:** pitch bend (two semitones), the mod wheel (vibrato up to half a semitone at 5.5 Hz), Volume and Expression (on MIDI's squared curve), Pan, Sustain, Resonance (CC 71) and Cutoff (CC 74, as far as eight octaves down); before, it ignored every lane. Without controllers it plays exactly as before.
  - **The Sequencer:** a **Length** box (bars, by the beat, an undo step; a loop as long as the sequence follows it, notes past the end kept); **Instrument...** lists the plug-ins under Audio Units and VST3 by maker, as the session's menus do, ticks the one in use and opens the chosen instrument's window. The session's instrument menus now group by format whenever the instruments come in more than one, not only past twelve.
  - **The Piano Roll shows a playhead** while playing, in the Sequencer and in the session, on each pass of a loop, and **its bars**: a bold line and the bar's number at each bar.
  - **A loop's end no longer cuts the sound off:** it ended the held notes with All Sound Off too, which silences an instrument at once; now each held note gets its off and its release rings into the next pass.
  - **Drag a sequence onto the Sequencer** from the Sequence Library or the Finder to open it there (asking about unsaved changes first).
  - **Velocity changes were lost:** the Piano Roll kept a velocity slider change only when a drag of it ended, and read the clip again every tenth of a second meanwhile, putting the old velocity back; a typed, stepped or scrolled value was never kept. Now a typed or stepped value is kept at once, and the clip isn't read again while the slider is dragged.
  - **Notes on one another merge:** a saved sequence had one note five times over at one start, which an instrument plays five times over (Predator3 distorted there). Notes of one pitch starting together are now one note (the longest, loudest) whenever the Piano Roll keeps an edit and whenever a sequence becomes a clip, so such a file plays right as it is and is mended when saved again. How the copies were made was not found.
  - **The Piano Roll's mouse:** a drag on empty grid boxes notes in (as the guide said; it took Shift), a click adds a note, Cmd-drag draws one with its length. A note's end is easier to grab: the last 10 pixels (a third of a short note) and 3 past it, the pointer a left-right arrow there, a grip on selected notes. **Option-Left and -Right** shorten and lengthen the chosen notes a grid step (Shift: a quarter). A trackpad's small scroll steps now add up (they rounded to nothing).
  - **Notes cross the Sequencer's loop:** with **Loop** on, a note running past the loop's end keeps sounding into the next pass until its own end, as the notes are what loops; the Piano Roll lets a note's end go up to a pass past the loop's end and shows the carried part faintly at the start. A sequence starting partway through its notes (an offset), or with a loop inside it that doesn't divide its length, still loops as before, cut at the end. The session and the Idea Board are unchanged.
  - Tested (fourth round): a note from beat 4 of a one-bar loop, two beats long, is at 1.00 of its level a quarter beat into the next pass and silent a beat after its end; a sequence with an offset keeps the old loop. Not driven by the tests: the faint carried part and the longer end drag in the running app.
  - Tested (third round): a typed velocity is kept and stays; a dragged one stays while the timer runs and is kept at the end; the drag box, click, Cmd-drag, Option-arrows, grabbing past a note's end and merging, through the Piano Roll's mouse and keys; a trackpad's 0.03 steps scroll; the stacked sequence plays 4 notes of 8. Not driven by the tests: the cursor and the grip's look.
  - Tested (second round): a note ending on the loop's end is still at 0.98 of its level in the first 10 ms of the next pass; a sequence dropped from the library and one from the Finder open in the Sequencer, a kit and a folder are refused. Not driven by the tests: the bar lines and numbers (painting) and the drop's outline.
  - Tested: through the Sequencer's own player and Basic Synth, a note at velocity 0.25 on frame 0 plays at 0.252 of one at 1, a Pan lane at 0 leaves the right channel silent, and looping, the first note plays again on the second pass at the same level; full pitch bend plays B4 for A4, Volume 64 at (64/127) squared, Pan 0 on the left alone, Cutoff 0 lowers a sawtooth's 8th harmonic by more than 20 dB, Sustain holds a note past its key going up, and the mod wheel leaves under half of a steady A4 at 440 Hz; Length to 2 bars makes 8 beats (192000 frames at 120 BPM) and undoes, 1.5 beats rounds to 2, notes kept; the playhead's place on a looping clip's timeline; an instrument chosen in the Sequencer opens its window; the instrument menu groups two formats under submenus. Not tried by hand: the Length box, the menu and the playhead line in the running app, and controllers on third-party instruments.
- Tested: a note clip read into beats and put back where it came from is the same clip, frame for frame (notes, lanes, loop and offset). Written at 90 BPM and dropped into a 140 BPM session, or across a change from 120 to 90, every note starts and ends on its beat within a frame. The voice keeps the instrument, effects and volume, without sends, output or automation. The three kinds of drop each do what is described. The library makes its folders, numbers a second name, scans the user's own folders and skips files that aren't sequences, and its search matches every word. Not tried by hand: saving and dropping sequences with real instrument plug-ins, and the panel itself.

**Other**
- **Screenshots retaken** for 0.19.0: every image in the README and the guide was taken again on this build (the masked status line keeps an interface's name out), the music ones counted in bars and beats with every clip, note, section and automation point on the grid, and a new one of the Sequencer workspace (the Sequence Library, the Sequencer and the Idea Board with a scene playing) in the guide's Sequencer section and the README.
- The Idea Board called a one-bar sequence "1 bars"; it now says "1 bar". Tested: the cell text for 1, 2 and 1.5 bars.
- **The master track** (0.20 plan, step 2b): every session has a **Master** track, always last, that the whole mix plays through: its effects, then its volume.
  - **Automation:** its volume envelope and its effects' parameters are automated on its lane with its A button, in its automation modes, like any track's.
  - **Its effects** are its FX chain, saved in the session file. The **Master Bus Effects** panel shows and edits the same chain. The editor's effects rack is no longer played on a session.
  - **Its fader and meter** are in its header and on the mixer's last strip, and the control surface's last strip moves it. It has no pan, solo, sends or output. It can't be moved, hidden, deleted, or put in a folder or VCA. The Output menu's first choice, "Master", is it.
  - **Latency:** its effects' latency is made up, so playback and mixdowns stay in time. Bus volume and pan envelopes are now read at the time their audio plays, after the latency of the buses they feed; before, they were early by that much.
  - **Mix Down goes through it** (its effects and volume), and so do Export Regions, a stems export's loudness spec and a video's sound, which before left the effects rack out. Stems, Bounce and Freeze stay each track's own sound.
  - **Older sessions** open with a master at 0 dB carrying the effects rack's effects, which played on their whole mix until then, so they sound as they did. A project's old sessions get the project's rack.
  - **Tested:**
    - at 0 dB with no effects, the mix is bit for bit as it was; at -6 dB it is the mix times that gain, in stereo and on all six channels of a 5.1 session;
    - a volume envelope on it plays and mixes down sample for sample alike, 30 dB down in its dip;
    - a limiter at -12 dB on it with the volume at -6 dB brings a mix's peak from -5.0 to -18.0 dB, so the volume comes after the effects;
    - 700 frames of latency on it, with 300 on a bus, are made up in playback and mixdown, from the start and part way in;
    - an automated effect parameter on it plays and mixes down alike;
    - a session without one is given one in every undo step, so undo keeps it;
    - it stays last, nothing routes into it, and it can't be deleted, moved or put in a folder; a step that misplaces, solos or pans it is tidied;
    - its volume and envelope survive the session file and undo.
  - **Tried in the app:** Mix Down and stems export work.
  - **Not tried by hand:** the master's header, mixer strip, A menu and Master Bus Effects panel; opening an older session or project with rack effects; and a control surface.
- **Automation follows the clips:** moving clips in time on their track (dragging, nudging, or typing a start in Clip Properties) takes the points of that track's envelopes under them along (volume, pan, surround pan, effect and instrument parameters), with the line across the clip as it was; where it went, they replace what was there, and where it was, a straight line joins the two sides. The line outside both places doesn't change. An envelope with no point under a clip, a clip moved to another track, and a copy made with Option leave the automation where it was. One undo step with the move. Tested: a clip moved 8000 frames earlier carries a volume dip exactly and leaves no points behind, an effect's ramp with no point under it is left alone, a move to another track leaves the automation, and two note clips moved together carry the pan under each. Not tried by hand: dragging clips with automation in the app.
- **Fixed: instrument and effect plug-ins crackling while the window was busy** (since 0.16.0, heard in 0.18.0 too). A track's plug-ins ran only when the audio thread could take the effect chains' lock at once, and played that block silent otherwise; the window took the same lock for every look at the chains (repainting the tracks, the inspector, the mixer), so blocks went silent many times a second while playing, with no dropout counted. The window's looks now take a lock of their own; only a change to a chain, or saving a plug-in's state, still holds the audio thread off, and each block that does is now counted with the dropouts. Tested: with another thread asking about a chain over 2 million times while 747,160 blocks played, none was silent (with the old locking, the same test has silent blocks); a block during a change to the chain is silent and counted. Not yet heard by hand after the fix. Heard fixed on the session that crackled (Ripple Test2).
- The toolbar's **Audio Settings...** button is now **Settings...**: it opens the whole Settings window (on the page last shown), as it always did, but its name read as if it only set up the audio device. The Window menu's item for the audio page is now **Audio Interface...**, and messages and the guide say "Settings > Audio". Screenshots in the guide still show the old name.
- The **Piano Roll zooms**: Cmd-scroll or a pinch zooms the time around the mouse, **-**, **+** and **Fit** at its top right zoom or show the whole clip, a scroll bar under the notes (or a sideways or Shift-scroll) moves along, and Option-scroll makes the rows taller or shorter. Until now it always fitted the clip's whole length, so a one-bar pattern in a long clip was a sliver; a clip that repeats now opens on its first repeat. Tested: the view opens on the pattern, Cmd-scroll keeps the frame under the mouse in place, a note clicked into the zoomed grid lands on its line, scrolling stops at the end, Fit shows all and the zoom stops at a twentieth of a second across; the suite's other piano roll and drum grid tests pass with it. The pinch and the scroll bar were not tried by hand.
- **Settings > AI** saves an endpoint's name and address as they are typed (before, only on Return or leaving the box), the Endpoint list shows a new name at once, and a line at the bottom of the page says what was saved and when ("Saved at 07:53:12: the name Qwen 3.8 27B."), also for the model, a new, chosen or deleted endpoint. Tested on the page by the tests (typed name and address, the list and the line); not yet looked at in the app.
- The status line at the bottom of the window (the file, the selection and the device) no longer squeezes long text narrower to fit: it keeps the font's own width and ends with an ellipsis.

**Release 0.18.0: Cubase-style sessions, Ask Torq on sessions, ASIO and Intel Macs**
- `Torqwave-0.18.0.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.18.0-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.18.0-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.18.0-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its own, built from the tag by the workflows. The first release since Torqwave set out to be an editor and a DAW in one, for sound design, writing music and working to picture (see the [roadmap](ROADMAP.md)); releases are no longer called dogfooding releases, and the dogfooding log goes on as the [fix log](docs/DOGFOODING.md).
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app universal (arm64 and x86_64) at the release's tag, signed it, and the whole suite (1,818 checks) passed in each half, the Intel one under Rosetta, with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker. Not run on an Intel Mac itself.
- Checked on Windows 11 with ASIO4ALL v2 over a USB audio codec: ASIO opened, played, opened its control panel and came back after a restart, and switching device types kept the outputs on; the whole suite (1,781 checks) passed on that machine with the ASIO build. Not tried: recording through ASIO, and a manufacturer's own ASIO driver.
- Checked on Linux (Ubuntu 24.04 in a container, x86_64, a virtual display with a window manager, no audio device): 1,782 checks passed, the new ones among them; the two that failed (ffmpeg reading a WAV's broadcast details, and the rack's device-list announcements) are the two that have failed there since 0.16.2, from that setup.

**ASIO on Windows**
- **ASIO** on Windows: ASIO interfaces are listed under Settings > Audio beside Windows Audio and DirectSound, with each device's own Control Panel button for its buffer size and routing. Built from the ASIO SDK headers JUCE bundles, which Steinberg has licensed under GPLv3 since October 2025; the Windows packages carry its licence as `ASIO-SDK-LICENSE.txt`. Torqwave no longer asks for an open ASIO device's inputs with a second device object, which would load the driver twice. Not yet tried with an ASIO interface: the Windows workflow builds it, and the checks need a Windows machine with an ASIO driver.
- Choosing another device in Settings > Audio (an ASIO driver such as ASIO4ALL, say) no longer leaves it with no outputs on and plays nothing: the output pair, and the outputs a session or file uses, are turned on again whenever the device in use changes, and an empty setup is no longer kept for the next launch.

**Universal Mac app**
- The Mac app is **universal** again, for Apple silicon and Intel Macs (macOS 13 or later). Each half passes the whole suite (1,818 checks each), the Intel half under Rosetta on the development Mac; it has not yet run on an Intel Mac itself. The plug-in scan asks for code for the processor it runs on, so on an Intel Mac a plug-in built only for Apple silicon is listed as not working, with the reason, as an Intel-only one is on Apple silicon. `Scripts/package.sh` builds both halves and tests each.

**Cubase-style sessions**
- A session's loop, as Cubase's cycle: a band along the top of the multitrack ruler, apart from the selection, that **Loop Playback** goes round.
