Drag along the top half of the time ruler to draw it, inside it to move it, at an end to size it. **Transport > Loop to Selection** (Option-P) sets it from the time range or the selected clips, and **Clear Loop** removes it. Play from before it runs into the loop and goes round; play from after it plays on. The session keeps it, and **Bars and Beats** too, so a session opens counting bars if it was saved so. Neither is an undo step, and an undo leaves both as they are. In bars and beats, with snapping on, a time range and the loop go onto the grid as you drag (the nearest line, however far, or a clip edge or marker close by), and **Bars and Beats** is in **View > Time Format** too.
- Cubase-style timing in the multitrack view: a **Grid** box beside a **Snap** switch in the toolbar (and **Multitrack > Grid**): Auto, Bar, 1/4, 1/8, 1/16, 1/32, 1/8 Triplet or 1/16 Triplet, held at any zoom and drawn in the lanes, bar lines strongest. The loop's start and end as locator fields in the toolbar, typed in bars (`9.1.1.0`) or the time format; the transport clock in bars, beats, sixteenths and ticks while counting bars; a **Tap** button beside the tempo; and the loop lit down every lane.
- An info line over the multitrack view's tracks, as Cubase's: the selected clip's name, start, length, gain, fades and note in one row, typed into to change them (Window > Info Line hides it). Counting bars, it and Clip Properties show and take the start, length and a note clip's loop in bars, beats, sixteenths and ticks.
- A **Track Inspector** panel (Window > Track Inspector; on the left in the Multitrack workspace), as Cubase's: the selected track's name, kind, volume, pan, mute and solo, output, instrument, inserts (a click opens one's window) and sends with their levels and pre-fader switches, each change an undo step.
- A tenth theme, **Studio**: a mid-grey dark theme with lighter panels and buttons over darker lanes and a silver waveform, the look of many recording programs (it goes well with the Violet accent). View > Theme.

**Ask Torq 🐶 on sessions, and the Effects menu (experimental)**
- Ask Torq works in the multitrack view (experimental). The model gets a copy of the session and 19 tools for it: tracks, buses and folders, volume, pan, mute, solo, outputs and sends; moving, trimming, splitting and fading clips and setting their gain; note clips with their notes, looping if asked; an instrument track's instrument, from the scanned instruments, and its saved presets; markers and regions; and the tempo. Apply makes the plan one undo step, and only when the session hasn't changed since the request; the instruments it chose are loaded after that, outside the undo step. An instrument's own settings, replacing an instrument, importing and bouncing aren't in its tools yet; it says so and plans nothing.
- Ask Torq on a session can draw a track's or bus's volume and pan automation (`set_automation`), and add effects to the end of a track's or bus's chain (`add_track_effect`, added once the plan is applied, outside its undo step, with the plug-in's default settings); `get_session` lists each track's effects and automation. Effect settings, removing or reordering effects, effect automation, bouncing and freezing aren't in its tools yet.
- Ask Torq can use nearly the whole Effects menu on a file: 43 more edits, 72 in all. Among them: one channel's polarity, the transient shaper and exciter, every repair tool (hum, clipping, clicks, noise from a print, adaptive noise, dialogue isolation, wind and bleed, ambience, wow and flutter, azimuth, phase, spectral recovery), rebalance, centre, pitch correction, the graphic EQ and multiband compressor, spectral attenuate and remove, delivery specs, dither, gates, gain and pan ramps, pauses, each region's level, fades or direction, and generated tones and noise. Each is a step kind in the shared engine, calling the same function as its menu command. Dynamic EQ, Match EQ, convolution, DTMF, Generate Instrument, Codec Preview and Spectral Heal are left out.
- Instrument presets: **Multitrack > Tracks > Save Instrument Preset...** saves the selected track's instrument as it sounds now, its whole state, under a name. Many instruments don't show a host their own sounds: Battery 4 offers its host only "New (default)", and its kits are only in its own browser. A kit saved this way can be chosen again by name, and Ask Torq can choose it: a new drum track on Battery with a saved kit, or another saved kit for a track that already plays it. Kept as one `.tqinstrument` file each, by plug-in and format, in `~/Library/Torqwave/Instrument Presets`.
- Measured: with Qwen3.8-27B, 14 session requests (`--ai-eval-set session`, among them a four-on-the-floor kick on a new drum track and a bass line in note clips) were all right in three runs out of three. Before the note tool named its octaves (middle C is C4), 2 of the 42 answers were wrong: one bass line, and one kick looped once a bar instead of on every beat. With two requests for saved kits added (a drum track on a named kit with a backbeat, and a kit that isn't saved, where it named the three that are and planned nothing), all 16 were right in two runs; a third was cut off when the model's server restarted. On 25 file requests written after its instructions were last changed and never used to tune them (`--ai-eval-set unseen`), it was right 66 times in 75 over three runs; one request (flipping one channel's polarity) has no tool to do it, and the model said so each time.
- The evaluation measures a tone's level in 0.1 s blocks, so a pitch shifter's slowly drifting phase no longer reads as a quieter tone.

**Other**
- `Scripts/package.sh` signs with the identity in `TORQWAVE_SIGN_IDENTITY` when it is set (a self-signed code-signing certificate will do), so macOS keeps the app's permissions from one build to the next.

**Release 0.17.0: Ask Torq, the plug-in catalogue and folders**
- `Torqwave-0.17.0.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.17.0-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.17.0-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.17.0-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its SHA-256 file, built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows and Linux workflows: the changes below on top of 0.16.4. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows and Linux packages are built on runners without an audio device, so untested there. Ask Torq is experimental and off by default.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 at the release's tag, signed it, and the whole suite (1,790 checks) passed with the signed app from the disk image as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.17.0 with the bundle ID `org.torqwave.editor` and holds arm64 alone, and it is stamped as a release build.
- Checked on Linux (Ubuntu 24.04 in a container, x86_64, a virtual display with a window manager, no audio device): 1,754 checks passed, the new ones among them; the two that failed (ffmpeg reading a WAV's broadcast details, and the rack's device-list announcements) are the two that failed there for 0.16.2 to 0.16.4, from that setup.

**Folders in sessions**
- **Right-click menus:** an empty part of a lane and a track's header now offer Add Folder (around the track right-clicked), beside Add Track, Add Instrument Track and Add Bus. A track's header adds Take Out of Folder and Delete Track (or Delete Bus); a folder's header, Delete Folder, Keep Its Tracks and Delete Folder and Its Tracks. The Folder submenu gains New Folder.
- **Dragging out of a folder:** where a dragged track lands now decides its folder. Dropped on a track it takes that track's folder, or none, so a track is dragged out of a folder by dropping it among tracks outside, or on its own folder's header (it lands just above the folder). Before, only dropping on a folder's header changed a track's folder, so a track could be dragged in but not out.
- **Multitrack > Tracks > Take Out of Folder** takes the selected track out of its folder. Delete Track says it deletes a folder too; a folder's tracks stay, out of it.

**Ask Torq 🐶 (experimental)**
- **Ask Torq 🐶** (a button at the top of the editor while it is turned on, or the Edit menu; not in the multitrack view yet) turns a sentence into a plan of edits on the open audio file ("trim the silence at both ends, bring it to -16 LUFS and fade out the last second"), made by a language model you run yourself. Experimental and off by default: it can misread a request or choose settings you wouldn't, so every plan is shown before anything changes, and Apply makes it one undo step ("Ask Torq: ..."). Discard leaves the file as it was. It works on audio files, not sessions yet. See the guide's new page, [Ask Torq](docs/guide/13-ask-torq.md).
- **Any server that speaks the OpenAI-compatible API** (Chat Completions with tool calls): Ollama, LM Studio, llama.cpp's server, vLLM and the like, on your computer or your network. **Settings > AI** takes the server's address, a key only if the server asks for one (kept in the Mac's keychain or Windows' Credential Manager, never in Torqwave's settings; on Linux read from `TORQWAVE_AI_KEY`), and the model, chosen from the server's own list, with a Test button. Torqwave ships no model and never sends audio: the model gets your sentence, the file's name and Torqwave's measurements.
- **What the model works with:** 26 tools on a copy of the file. It looks (length, selection, markers, loudness, true peak, each channel's levels, the spectrum in octave bands, where the sounds, silences and clipping are), and it edits through Torqwave's own code: 30 of the Effects menu's edits run as recorded sequences run them, each setting checked against its dialog's range; fades, trimming silence, silence, delete, crop and insert silence; markers and regions. Plug-ins are set through their parameters, so any AU, VST3 or LV2 effect works as the built-in ones do: the model adds them to the effects rack, reads and sets their parameters (each value read back from the plug-in, so one it clamps or refuses is reported), bypasses, moves and removes them, and renders the rack into the audio. A rack rendered by a plan comes back when the step is undone, as after Apply Effects Rack.
- A request may take up to 60 rounds of tool calls. When they run out, the model is asked to sum up and the plan it made so far is still offered, marked as possibly incomplete. The same call made a third time with nothing changed in between is refused, which breaks most loops.
- **The plug-in catalogue** (Settings > AI > Plug-in Catalogue...): what Ask Torq knows of each effect, kept between runs. **Learn All** reads every effect's parameters (names, units, ranges, choices, defaults) in the plug-in scan's separate process, so one that crashes or hangs is marked and the rest go on, then has the model describe each one from them: what it does, what it's good for, which settings matter. An AU and a VST3 of one plug-in share a description. It runs in the background, resumes where it stopped, and stops at once when the server can't be reached. Each entry can be read, edited (and then kept through later runs) or forgotten. Ask Torq's plug-in list shows each description's first sentence and searches them, a new tool (describe_plugin) gives a whole entry without opening the plug-in, and a plug-in Ask Torq opens has its parameters kept. Effects only for now; instruments come with sessions. A plug-in's facts include its presets' names, so the model can suggest one. A plug-in the model doesn't know is written up as "Not known:" with no settings, instead of a guess, and shows as not known.
- **Shipped descriptions:** Torqwave carries reviewed descriptions of 406 effects it has been learnt on (`Data/plugin-knowledge.json`, made by `Scripts/export_plugin_knowledge.py`, which ships only reviewed entries and refuses text that looks like a path, address, email or key). Where this computer has no description of its own, the shipped one is used, and Learn All asks the model only about the rest.
- The window shows the model at work: its thinking (for a model that shows it), each tool it calls, each step it adds, and its answer. Stop cancels a request. A plan made while the file changed is not applied.
- Linux builds use libcurl, loaded when a server is first asked, so HTTPS servers work there; the app still starts without it. The Mac app may now load plain HTTP from the server you name (local model servers rarely use HTTPS).
- Measured with an evaluation of ten requests on a generated test file (`TorqwaveTests --ai-eval <server> <model>`: trimming, loudness, a fade at the end of the sound, a region over the sound, a compressor set up in the rack, gain then limiting, reversing half the file, normalizing each channel, a concert-hall reverb, and a request it can't do, transcription, which must change nothing), each plan checked by measuring the result. With Qwen3.8-27B (4 bits a weight) on a local server: 9 of 10 at first, the fade put at the file's silent end instead of the sound's; after one line was added to the model's instructions (find the sound first when a request speaks of it), 30 of 30 over three runs, 60 to 69 s for the ten. That line was written against these same requests, so the 30 of 30 overstates what to expect from requests it hasn't seen. No other model has been measured.
- Measured by the tests (60 new checks): the client against a mock server on this computer (models listed, requests carrying the model, messages and tools, the key only when there is one, answers streamed or whole, tool calls put together from pieces, the server's errors, cancelling a request the server is slow to answer (it returned within 20 ms of Stop), a server that isn't there); every tool on generated audio, settings out of range or unknown refused with nothing changed; plug-in parameters set from text, a value the plug-in can't read found by searching its range; the built-in limiter added, set and rendered (a tone 12 dB up held at -6.00 dBFS); the Ask window from request to Apply; and the name's puppy drawn in colour. The whole suite passed on the Mac (1,767 checks; 341 commands, 107 default keys). On Linux (Ubuntu 24.04 in a container) 1,731 checks passed, the AI ones among them, and the two that fail there failed the same way for 0.16.4, from that setup.

**Keys in Settings**
- **Settings > Keys** now holds the keyboard shortcut list itself, filling the page, instead of a button that opened it in a window of its own. **Window > Keyboard Shortcuts...** opens that page. The Settings window opens larger (860 by 660) to fit it.
- The Keys, Plug-ins and AI pages no longer leave an empty line's height above their panel, and a page that fits its window no longer shows a scroll bar.

**Release 0.16.4: Automation on instrument tracks**
- `Torqwave-0.16.4.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.16.4-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.16.4-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.16.4-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its SHA-256 file, built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows and Linux workflows: the changes below on top of 0.16.3. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows and Linux packages are built on runners without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 at the release's tag, signed it, and the whole suite (1,707 checks) passed with the signed app from the disk image as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.16.4 with the bundle ID `org.torqwave.editor` and holds arm64 alone, and it is stamped as a release build.
- Checked on Linux (Ubuntu 24.04 in a container, x86_64, a virtual display with a window manager, no audio device): 1,672 checks passed, the new ones among them; the two that failed (ffmpeg reading a WAV's broadcast details, and the rack's device-list announcements) are the two that failed there for 0.16.2 and 0.16.3, from that setup.

**Envelopes on instrument tracks**
- A click on an instrument track's envelope line over a note clip adds a point, as it does over an audio clip. Before, the note clip under the line took the click, so on a track full of note clips the envelope could only be drawn in the gaps between them.
- Envelopes left in a session by an effect that is no longer on the track, or by an instrument swapped for another, drive nothing: they no longer show themselves over the lane or light the A button.
- The A button's tooltip reads "Automation Envelopes". It said "Volume and pan envelopes", though the button also shows effect and instrument parameters' envelopes.
- The A button's menu names an instrument track's instrument on its own: **Show <instrument> Envelope**, above the effects, which are numbered from 1 as the FX menu numbers them. Before, the instrument was listed as effect 1 ("Show 1. <instrument> Envelope") and the effects from 2, so the numbers did not match the FX menu's. Its parameters were already automatable (drawn, written from its window with Write Automation, saved, played, copied and moved with time like an effect's); the guide now says how.
- Measured by the tests (2 new checks): an envelope drawn on the built-in synth's level comes back from a session file exactly, the synth reloaded into the same slot; and played from the reloaded chain, the synth follows it (a tenth of the sound at -20 dB, then the full sound, its level left at the envelope's end), bit for bit the same as before saving. The A menu's new names have no automated check. The whole suite passed (1,707 checks; 340 commands, 107 default keys).

**Release 0.16.3: The home screen first, Option-drag copies, selecting points, ripple for a time range, envelopes that show themselves**
- `Torqwave-0.16.3.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.16.3-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.16.3-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.16.3-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its SHA-256 file, built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows and Linux workflows: the changes below on top of 0.16.2. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows and Linux packages are built on runners without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 at the release's tag, signed it, and the whole suite (1,704 checks) passed with the signed app from the disk image as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.16.3 with the bundle ID `org.torqwave.editor` and holds arm64 alone, and it is stamped as a release build.
- Checked on Linux (Ubuntu 24.04 in a container, x86_64, a virtual display with a window manager, no audio device): 1,669 checks passed, the new ones among them; the two that failed (ffmpeg reading a WAV's broadcast details, and the rack's device-list announcements) are the two that failed there for 0.16.2, from that setup.

**Starting on the home screen**
- Torqwave starts on the home screen. Before, it reopened the file that was open when it quit, so the home screen showed only after closing every file. Settings > Start > On start chooses between the home screen (the default) and Reopen the last file, which works as before.
- A file or session you save goes on the recent list, so it shows on the home screen and in File > Open Recent: Save and Save As for audio files (through the rack too), and Save Session and Save Session As. Before, only opening one put it there, so a new recording or a session you had just built showed only after you opened it again.

**Ripple for a time range**
- With a time range drawn (the Range tool, or across the marker lane), Edit > Delete and Cut act on the range and follow the Ripple setting: No Ripple clears it and leaves the time; Ripple Track clears it and closes the gap on each track that had something there; Ripple All Tracks takes the time out of the session, markers and all. Cut and Copy copy the clips in the range, so Paste puts them at the cursor. The menu reads Delete Range, Cut Range and Copy Range while they do. A click on a clip, a point or an empty lane turns them back to clips. Before, Delete and Cut acted on the selected clips whatever the range, and cutting a piece out of a clip took splitting it first. Ripple still starts off.

**Envelopes show themselves**
- A track with automation shows its envelope over its lane without choosing it in the A menu: volume first, else pan, the surround pan, or the first automated effect parameter. Hide Envelope there keeps it hidden. Before, a track's envelopes showed only once chosen, so automation written by accident (with Write Automation on) was invisible. The guide said the A button is lit while an envelope shows; it is lit when the track has automation, and the guide says so now.

**Copying clips by dragging, and selecting and deleting points**
- Option-drag a clip in a session to copy it, as in other multitrack editors: the clip stays, a copy lands where it is dropped (on another track too), and every selected clip and note clip comes along. Option over a clip shows the copy cursor; pressing Option during a drag copies as well. The copies are selected afterwards, and the copy is one undo step ("Copy Clip", or "Copy N Clips"). Option on a clip's edge still stretches it.
- Points on an envelope or a clip gain line can be selected: a click selects a point (it is drawn larger and white), a point you add or drag stays selected, and Shift-click adds or removes others on the same line. Delete or Backspace removes the selected points as one undo step, and Edit > Delete reads Delete Point. Selecting clips drops the points, and points on a line that is hidden are never deleted. Before, Option-click was the only way to remove a point.
- With Option held over an envelope or clip-gain point, the pointer turns into a dot with a red minus, so you can see a click will remove it. Before, it stayed a hand. An Option-click that misses a point now does nothing; before, it added a point.

**Release 0.16.2: The guide in the app, a home screen, ripple and layered clips, workspace accents, LV2**
- `Torqwave-0.16.2.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.16.2-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.16.2-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.16.2-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its SHA-256 file, built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows and Linux workflows: the changes below on top of 0.16.1. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows and Linux packages are built on runners without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 at the release's tag, signed it, and the whole suite (1,696 checks) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.16.2 with the bundle ID `org.torqwave.editor` and holds arm64 alone, and it is stamped as a release build.
- Checked on Linux (Ubuntu 24.04 in a container, x86_64, a virtual display with a window manager, no audio device): the suite ran 1,663 checks; all the LV2 checks passed, and the two that failed (ffmpeg reading a WAV's broadcast details, and the rack's device-list announcements) fail the same way there on the tree before these changes, so they come from that setup, not this release.

**The user guide inside the app**
- Help > User Guide (F1) opens the guide in a window of its own: its pages listed down the left, the page on the right with its headings, lists, tables, links and screenshots, styled by the theme. Links between pages open there, with Back and Forward; other links open in the browser. Help > Keyboard Shortcut List opens the shortcut list there. The pages, the shortcut list and their screenshots are built into the app, so it needs no connection. The Mac has a Help menu now.
- The tests parse every page and check that each picture decodes and each link reaches a page and heading the app carries, or the web.

**A home screen**
- With nothing open, the editor shows buttons for New File, Open..., New Session, Open Project... and the User Guide, and up to twelve recent files, sessions and projects as cards. A card shows an audio file's waveform, a session's tracks with their clips in their colours, or a project's session or the file it had in front, with its kind, when it was last saved and its folder; a click opens it. The pictures are made in the background and kept on disk by each file's path, size and time, so the next launch draws them at once. Before, the empty editor listed the recent files by name.

**Splitting several clips at once**
- Split Clip (Cmd-K) cuts every selected clip and note clip the cursor crosses. Split All Tracks (Shift-Cmd-K), or a Shift-click with the Split tool, cuts every clip there on every shown track, skipping locked ones. Each is one undo step. Before, Split Clip cut one clip.

**Ripple editing**
- Multitrack > Editing and Recording gains No Ripple, Ripple Track and Ripple All Tracks (also in Settings > Multitrack, and kept). Deleting or cutting clips then closes up the time they leave: on their own track (its later clips, note clips and envelope points move left), or across the whole session, markers and envelopes too, where no other track still plays over that time. No Ripple leaves the gap, as before.

**Layered overlapping clips**
- With Multitrack > Editing and Recording > Layer Overlapping Clips on (kept with the session, an undo step), the clip on top of an overlap is heard alone, as the lanes draw it, except over its fades, so crossfades still sound both. Bring Clip to Front and Send Clip to Back (also in a clip's right-click menu) restack the selected clips. Off, overlapping clips mix as before.

**An accent colour for each workspace**
- Window > Workspace > Accent for <workspace> gives the current workspace an accent of its own, shown whenever that workspace is in use, so each one is told apart at a glance. The App's Own goes back to the accent chosen in View > Theme, and choosing one there clears the current workspace's. Save Workspace keeps the accent, and the accents are kept with the workspaces.

**LV2 plug-ins on the Mac and Linux**
- The plug-in scan finds LV2 effects and instruments in the LV2 folders (`~/.lv2`, `/usr/local/lib/lv2`, `/usr/lib/lv2`, on the Mac also `~/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/LV2` and `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/LV2`, and the folders in `LV2_PATH`), checks each in a worker as it does VST3 and Audio Units, and lists them under LV2 in the plug-in menus, for the rack, track effects and instrument tracks. Windows stays VST3 only for now: its builds run no tests yet.
- The tests build a small LV2 gain into a bundle and check that the scan finds it in the app's worker, that it loads, plays at the level its control is set to, keeps that setting in its state, and goes through the rack and back from a saved rack.

**Release 0.16.1: Instruments as you play them**
- `Torqwave-0.16.1.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.16.1-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.16.1-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.16.1-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its SHA-256 file, built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows and Linux workflows: the changes below on top of 0.16.0, from a round of use of 0.16.0 with instrument tracks; in the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) as rows 33 to 41. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows and Linux packages are built on runners without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 at the release's tag, signed it, and the whole suite (1,634 checks) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.16.1 with the bundle ID `org.torqwave.editor` and holds arm64 alone, and its loading screen and About box show the plain version, as a release build should.

**Instruments sound as you play and edit them**
- The piano roll plays what you do: a click on its keyboard sounds the key until the mouse is let go, a drag along the keys plays each one it passes, and a note sounds while it is put in, clicked or dragged, at each pitch it moves to, through the clip's instrument. Before, nothing sounded until the session played.
- Notes played live (the Keyboard panel and the MIDI inputs) go to the instrument of the track being worked on: the selected track when it is an instrument track, otherwise the track of the note clip in the piano roll. Before, they went to the selected track alone, so with an audio track or nothing selected they went nowhere.
- The Keyboard panel says what it plays ("Plays Synth's instrument"), or why nothing will sound: not in the Multitrack view, no instrument track to play, or a track without an instrument yet.
- A note held while the target changes ends on the instrument it started on: the new track takes over once no note is held.
- An instrument plug-in's own keys sound while the session is stopped: with an instrument's window open the instruments keep running (and for ten seconds after it closes, for the release). Before, a stopped session ran its instruments only after a note came in through the app, so notes clicked in the plug-in's window made no sound.
- Notes played live no longer stick, and the sound no longer stalls, while the app reads or changes the plug-ins (saving a plug-in's state, as it does soon after one is loaded). The audio thread had two problems while the plug-ins were held. It dropped that block's live notes, so a note's off could be lost and the note left sounding; now they wait and reach the instrument on its next block. It also waited for the plug-ins to come free when telling whether a track has an instrument; now it never waits.
- A clip's note sounding when playback stops ends at the stop. Before, its note-off never came, and when the instrument went on running stopped (its window open, or a note just played live) the note went on sounding. Now, the next time an instrument runs after a stop (or a jump), it is sent an off for each note it was given and not yet ended, then All Notes Off and All Sound Off: many plug-ins ignore those two alone. The block playback stops in starts no clip notes.

**Right-click menus on the lanes**
- A right-click on the multitrack lanes away from a clip, below the tracks or on the ruler away from a marker opens a menu:
  - Add Track, Add Instrument Track (with the instruments to choose from) and Add Bus;
  - on an instrument track's lane, Add Notes Here... and Add Trigger Here, at the clicked place;
  - Paste Here and Select All Clips.

  Before, a right-click there did nothing (or started a selection); clips, note clips, markers and track headers had their menus, the rest none.
- In the track list on the left, a track header's menu ends with the same Add Track, Add Instrument Track and Add Bus, and the empty space below the headers opens the lane menu (before, the list's holder took every click on its empty space, so nothing opened there). A right-click on a track's name now opens its menu too; before, the name took the click and nothing opened. A double-click on the name still renames it.

**The track list sizes**
- The track list on the left of the multitrack view can be made wider: drag the edge between it and the lanes (the pointer shows a resize arrow there), from its old 220 px up to 600 px. The width is kept for next time. Before, it was fixed at 220 px.

**File > Open takes sessions and projects**
- File > Open shows sessions and projects (`.tqsession`, `.tqproject`, and SpectraBlade's `.sbsession`, `.sbproject`) as openable beside audio files, and opens each as a session or project. Before, they were greyed out there, and only File > Open Session... or Open Project... could open them.

**Which build is running**
- The loading screen's version is larger and brighter, and a build that is not a release says so, with when it was built and its commit, for example "Version 0.16.1 (test build Nov 18 09:21, 4906229+changes)". The About box shows the same. A release build (made at its tag with no uncommitted changes, or by the release workflows) shows the plain version. Before, the version was small and dim, and every build of a version looked the same.

**No Open buttons**
- The Open... button is gone from the toolbar and from the top of the Files panel: File > Open (Cmd+O) opens files, as before.

**Checked for these changes**
- New checks in the suite:
  - a note played live into a stopped session sounds through the track's instrument (the built-in synth);
  - a new track takes over only once the held note is off;
  - the piano roll's keys and notes sound and end as the mouse goes down, moves and is let go;
  - a live note-off that comes while a plug-in's state is being saved reaches the instrument once the save ends (the same check lost it before the fix);
  - the lane menu offers what it should on an audio lane, an instrument lane and below the tracks, its Add Track adds one, and the empty track list below the headers is the view's to click (the same check failed before the fix);
  - the track list's edge shows a resize pointer, drags the list to 340 px and reports it, and stops at 600 and 220 px;
  - File > Open's file patterns take audio, sessions and projects and nothing else;
  - the version shown is plain for a release build and stamped for a test build;
  - stopping playback in the middle of a clip's note sends the instrument that note's own off and every note off (the same check failed before the fix);
  - stopped, a note clip's note at the cursor is not played again block after block (before the fix it started in all 24 of 24 blocks).
- By hand in the app on the development Mac: the piano roll's keys and notes sound, its first note no longer sticks, and a stop ends a note on Native Instruments' Massive (VST3). A probe that loaded Massive, played a note clip and stopped in the middle of a note measured the output silent from 0.25 s after the stop on (the release fading before that). The header menu's new items, the right-click on a track's name, the track list's width, File > Open with sessions and the loading screen's version have their automated checks only.

**Release 0.16.0: Torqwave**
- `Torqwave-0.16.0.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`Torqwave-0.16.0-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `Torqwave-0.16.0-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`Torqwave-0.16.0-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its SHA-256 file, built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows and Linux workflows: the new name and artwork and the editor tab's name below, on top of 0.15.10, with nothing else changed. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows and Linux packages are built on runners without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64, signed it, and the whole suite (1,623 checks, the command table audit and the three SpectraBlade checks among them) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.16.0 with the bundle ID `org.torqwave.editor`, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app, copied to Applications, launched and quit cleanly; in it, the main panel's tab read Editor and Multitrack as the view switched. Its first launch on a Mac with SpectraBlade's settings was checked on a development build: the settings, Sound Library and recovery folder came across, with no SpectraBlade name left in the settings. Nothing has been listened to for this release.

**The main panel's tab is named for the view**
- The tab of the main panel (the one that can't be closed) now reads Multitrack in the multitrack view and Editor in the editor, and changes as you switch; it read Editor in both.

**SpectraBlade is now Torqwave**
- The app has a new name, Torqwave, everywhere: the app and its window, menus and installers, the bundle ID (`org.torqwave.editor`), the settings and data folders, and the files it saves (`.tqsession`, `.tqproject`, `.tqrack`).
- What SpectraBlade left behind still works. On the first launch Torqwave copies SpectraBlade's settings (audio devices, the plug-in list and everything else it remembered), its effect presets, Sound Library and recovered sessions to its own folders, leaving SpectraBlade's in place. SpectraBlade's sessions, projects and racks (`.sbsession`, `.sbproject`, `.sbrack`) open, with the built-in effects saved in them, and its presets load. The Windows installer removes SpectraBlade's program folder and file types and opens `.sbsession` and `.sbproject` files with Torqwave; the Linux `install.sh` removes what SpectraBlade installed. On the Mac, drag SpectraBlade to the Trash.
- New artwork for the new name: the app icon and the banner (on the loading screen and in the README) show a stack of waveforms seen in depth, each hiding those behind it, twisted along the stack: white lines on black with the front one in orange, the name in white with WAVE in the same orange, where SpectraBlade's showed a razor blade. `Art/make-icon.py` and `Art/make-banner.py` draw them from the mark in `Art/ridges.py`; the icon has fewer, bolder slices so it reads at small sizes, and a second drawing for the Dock and smaller (`Art/torqwave-icon-small.png`, CMake's `ICON_SMALL`), with 12 slices three times as thick, larger in the tile.
- Racks and settings from before SpectraBlade (WaveDrill's `.wdrack`, Waveloom's `.wlrack`) are no longer read.

**Release 0.15.10: The spectrogram's tools**
- `SpectraBlade-0.15.10.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`SpectraBlade-0.15.10-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `SpectraBlade-0.15.10-windows-x64.zip`) and the Linux tarball (`SpectraBlade-0.15.10-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its SHA-256 file, built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows and Linux workflows: Milestones 390 to 392 below on top of 0.15.9, asked for while using the 0.15 line by hand: a Time Selection tool beside a Box tool that draws a box on a plain drag (390), the box resized by its edges and corners (391), and a new box or drawing taking over while one plays (392); in the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) as rows 29 to 31. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows and Linux packages are built on runners without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 from the pushed source, signed it, and the whole suite (1,620 checks, the command table audit among them) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.15.10, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for Milestone 392 and after it and quit cleanly. Tried by hand by the user on the development build: the tools, the box resized by its edges and corners, and a new box while one plays; nothing has been listened to for this release.

**Milestone 392: A new box while one plays**
- Asked for during the round of use: with a box playing (only what is inside it), drawing a new box let the file play across every frequency over the new box's time, since the sound made for the old box was dropped and nothing made for the new one. Now a new box or drawing made while the inside of one plays takes over: once the mouse has let go of it, its inside is processed in the background (the transport reads "Processing...") and plays from its start, with the old box's sound (nothing outside it) staying until then; only a plain selection with no box or drawing lets the file play as it is. Resizing a box with its edges and corners (Milestone 391) is treated the same way.
- Measured by the tests (1 new check): drawings compare point for point, and differ by a dab moved, a stroke that takes instead of adds, or a lasso point moved (what tells a new drawing from the one playing). The change of what plays goes through the window's timer, which the tests do not drive. The whole suite passed (1,619 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the take-over on screen; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 391: A spectral box resized by its edges and corners**
- Asked for during the round of use: a box of time and frequency that came out wrong had to be drawn again. Now, with the Time or Box tool in hand, the box's edges and corners drag to resize it: an edge moves in time (left and right) or frequency (top and bottom), a corner both, and the other edges stay where they were; the pointer shows the resize arrows over them, and the box's corners are drawn as small handles. A drag inside the box, away from its edges, starts a new selection as before.
- Measured by the tests (1 new check): a box's right edge dragged later moves the selection's end and nothing else, its top-right corner dragged higher and later moves the end and the band's top, its bottom edge dragged lower moves the band's bottom, and a drag inside it starts a new selection. The whole suite passed (1,618 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the handles and the pointers on screen.

**Milestone 390: A Time Selection tool, and the Box tool makes a box**
- Asked for during the round of use: the Box Selection tool could not be used in the spectrogram; a plain drag with it selected time across every frequency, and only Option-drag made a box. The strip's tools mirror Audition's keys (E, D, P), but the box tool was really a time tool with Option for a box. Now the strip starts with a **Time Selection** tool (A), the one in hand to start: a drag selects time across every frequency, Option-drag in a spectrogram a box of time and frequency, as before. The **Box Selection** tool (E) does what its name says: a plain drag in a spectrogram selects a box of time and frequency, Option-drag time alone; in a waveform lane it selects time. Shift extends the time selection with either. The crosshair pointer shows over a spectrogram with the box tool too. Edit menu and the tools' descriptions say so.
- Measured by the tests (2 checks extended): with the box tool a plain drag in a spectrogram selects a box (its band inside the spectrogram's frequencies) and Option-drag time alone, and the time tool the other way round; the strip's five tool buttons sit in order (Time, Box, Lasso, Brush, then Heal), the one in hand lit, each running its command. The whole suite passed (1,617 checks; 331 commands, 105 default keys) on a second run, the first having failed only the video panel's playback-drift check, which the notes record as timing-sensitive under load; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the tools on screen.

**Release 0.15.9: Linux**
- `SpectraBlade-0.15.9.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, the Windows packages (`SpectraBlade-0.15.9-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `SpectraBlade-0.15.9-windows-x64.zip`) and, new, the Linux tarball (`SpectraBlade-0.15.9-linux-x86_64.tar.gz`), each with its SHA-256 file, built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows and Linux workflows: Milestone 389 below on top of 0.15.8, SpectraBlade on Linux. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows and Linux packages are built on runners without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 from the pushed source, signed it, and the whole suite passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.15.9, holds arm64 alone, names Linux in its About box, and carries the licence with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; nothing has been listened to on the Mac.

**Milestone 389: Linux**
- SpectraBlade builds and runs on 64-bit Linux from the same source (PR #31). What Linux lacks that the Mac and Windows have from the system is filled in: the Video panel's picture, decoded by ffmpeg behind the interface of JUCE's VideoComponent (which Linux doesn't have), so every video type shows without previews; AAC export through ffmpeg's own AAC encoder, written into the file as on Windows (exact length, room for tags); M4A opened through ffmpeg, trimmed to the edit list's length; MP3 read exactly (JUCE's reader seeks two frames away in VBR files and counts MPEG-2 and 2.5 frames as 1,152 samples: on Linux the file is read only onwards or from the start, and MPEG-2 and 2.5 lengths are halved); the audio device probed at launch (ALSA, or JACK when that was saved); the playhead drawn behind the device's own delay (about five buffers through PipeWire's ALSA plug-in, where JUCE's ALSA device reports three); the scan tests' VST3 bundles in the Linux layout; Linux wording for the audio-service hint and the licence's place; video previews cached under `$XDG_CACHE_HOME`; and the About box naming Linux. `Scripts/package-linux.sh` makes a tarball with an installer (`~/.local` for one person, `--system` for `/usr/local`, `--uninstall`), a desktop entry and the scalable icon, and `.github/workflows/linux.yml` builds it on Ubuntu 24.04 and attaches it to releases.
- Measured on Ubuntu 24.04 (GNOME on Wayland through XWayland, PipeWire): the whole suite, 1,594 of 1,595 checks; the one left is ffmpeg 6.1 reading a WAV's INFO comment and year over bext's description and date, as newer ffmpeg doesn't (see [Testing](docs/TESTING.md)). The PipeWire delay measured with `snd_pcm_delay` at buffers of 128 to 2,048 samples. ffmpeg's AAC encoder leaves 10 to 20 dB more error than Apple's at 96 to 192 kbps, so the AAC checks at those rates have Linux thresholds, as they have Windows ones. The app was launched, played through PipeWire and showed an MP4's picture in the Video panel; the installer was run.
- Not tried: other distributions, X11 sessions, JACK, and listening for more than a few seconds.

**Release 0.15.8: Loop Playback round the whole file**
- `SpectraBlade-0.15.8.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, and the Windows packages (`SpectraBlade-0.15.8-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `SpectraBlade-0.15.8-windows-x64.zip`, with their SHA-256 files) built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows workflow: Milestone 388 below on top of 0.15.7, asked for while using the 0.15 line by hand: with nothing selected, Loop Playback goes round the whole file or session instead of stopping at the end; in the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) as row 28. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows packages are built on a runner without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 from the pushed source, signed it, and the whole suite (1,617 checks, the command table audit among them) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.15.8, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for Milestone 388 and quit cleanly. The loop round the whole file has not been tried on screen; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 388: Loop Playback round the whole file or session**
- Asked for during the round of use: with Loop Playback on and nothing selected (setting up a plug-in in the rack, say), the file played to its end and stopped, so it had to be started again by hand; the loop only ever went round a selection or time range. Now, with nothing selected, **Transport > Loop Playback** (Cmd-L) plays the whole file round and round from the cursor (in the multitrack view, the whole session), back to the start at the end with no gap; a selection still loops as before. The whole-file loop has no stop of its own: turning the loop off lets the play run on to the end. The command's description and the Playback setting say so.
- Measured by the tests (1 new check): the player with a loop over the whole file plays from the cursor round the end back to the start, sample for sample, with no stop frame; cleared, it plays to the end of the file and then silence. The whole suite passed (1,617 checks; 330 commands, 104 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the loop with a plug-in window open.

**Release 0.15.7: The effects rack, live until applied**
- `SpectraBlade-0.15.7.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256, and the Windows packages (`SpectraBlade-0.15.7-windows-x64-setup.exe` and `SpectraBlade-0.15.7-windows-x64.zip`, with their SHA-256 files) built from the release's tag and attached by the Windows workflow: Milestones 384 to 387 below on top of 0.15.6, asked for while using the 0.15 line by hand: an Apply button on the rack panel (384), Save As written through the rack as it plays (385) and every other export too (386), and applying the rack emptying it, with Undo bringing it back (387); in the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) as rows 24 to 27. A dogfooding release: built for Apple silicon only on the Mac, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)); the Windows packages are built on a runner without an audio device, so untested there.
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 from the pushed source, signed it, and the whole suite (1,616 checks, the command table audit among them) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.15.7, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for Milestone 387 and quit cleanly. The rack's Apply button, the switches in the save and export dialogs, and the undo that brings the rack back have not been tried on screen; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 387: Applying the rack empties it, and Undo brings it back**
- Asked for during the round of use: after Apply Effects Rack the rack stayed live, so what played was run through the effects a second time, on top of the audio they were already in. Now applying the rack writes it in and empties it, in one undo step: **Undo** of the step brings the rack back as it was applied (its effects loaded again, with their settings and on/off switches), and **Redo** empties it again. The step remembers the rack in the undo history, so going back to it from the History panel does the same, switching between open files leaves each file's history its own, and a project saves it with the step and brings it back.
- Measured by the tests (1 new check, 1 extended): a step that applied the rack keeps the rack for undo, other steps keep none, and the history carries it through getSteps and restoreHistory; a project opened again after its folder moved gives the step its rack back, on that step alone. The whole suite passed (1,616 checks). The emptying and the undo that reloads the rack go through the window, which the tests do not drive.
- Not tried by hand: the apply and the undo on screen.

**Milestone 386: Every export through the effects rack**
- Asked for during the round of use: Save As could write the file through the rack, but Save Selection As, Save Each Region As, Save Pieces Between Markers As, Export as MP3 or AAC and Export with ffmpeg still wrote the audio without it. Now, with an effect on in the rack, **Save Selection As...**, **Export as MP3 or AAC...** and **Export with ffmpeg...** carry the same switch as Save As, **Through the effects rack, as it plays** (on to start), and **Save Each Region As...** and **Save Pieces Between Markers As...**, which have no dialog of their own, write through the rack whenever an effect is on in it. Each renders the whole file through the rack's effects that are on, in their order, then writes the part asked for; the open audio and the rack stay as they are. Cancelling the render writes nothing.
- Measured by the tests: the render through the rack and the skipping of bypassed slots were already measured; the whole suite passed (1,616 checks; 330 commands, 104 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly. The dialogs are modal and not read by the tests.
- Not tried by hand: the switches on screen.

**Milestone 385: Save As through the effects rack**
- Asked for during the round of use: the rack could be heard but not saved without applying it first, while the processing is meant to stay live until it is applied or bounced. Now, when the rack holds an effect that is on, **File > Save As...** adds a switch to its sample-format dialog, **Through the effects rack, as it plays** (on to start): the new file is written through the rack's effects that are on, in their order, over the whole file, with its markers and file info; the open audio, its undo history and the rack stay as they are, so a plain Save still writes the audio without them. Cancelling the render saves nothing. Without an effect on, Save As is as it was.
- Measured by the tests: the render through the rack's copies and the skipping of bypassed slots were already measured (the offline render checks); the whole suite passed (1,615 checks). The Save As dialog itself is modal and not read by the tests.

**Milestone 382: Transpose, velocity and quantize for notes in the multitrack view**
- Asked for during the round of use: the notes of a note clip could be changed only one clip at a time in the piano roll. Now a **Notes** group under Multitrack works on every selected note clip at once: **Transpose Notes...** moves their notes up or down by semitones (each pitch kept to 0 to 127), **Note Velocity...** gives every note a velocity from 1 to 127 or scales the velocities they have by a percentage, and **Quantize Notes...** moves each note's start to the nearest line of the beat divided by 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12 or 16, on the session's tempo map. Each is one undo step.
- Measured by the tests (3 new checks, 1 extended): transposing keeps each pitch to 0 to 127 and does nothing by 0 or for a clip that is not there; velocities set or scaled stay within 1 to 127; quantizing moves notes to the nearest session line, never before the clip's timeline start; through the view the three commands act on every selected note clip as one undo step each. The whole suite passed (1,614 checks; 330 commands, 104 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the three dialogs on screen.

**Milestone 381: The musical timebase**
- Asked for during the round of use: clips were placed in time alone, so a tempo change left them where they were, the beats moving under them. Now each track has a timebase. On a **musical** track (**Musical Timebase** in the track's menu, or Multitrack > Tempo > Musical Timebase for the selected track; on for new instrument tracks, off for audio tracks and sessions saved before this) the clips keep their place in bars and beats when the tempo or a tempo change is set, tapped or removed: each moves to the beat it started on, a note clip's notes, lanes, loop and length scaled with its bars, an audio clip moved with its audio as long as it was (a locked one left). A track in time keeps its clips where they are. The timebase is saved in session files and track templates.
- Measured by the tests (3 new checks): new instrument tracks musical and audio tracks in time, one made musical once and never a bus or a folder; the tempo halved moves a musical track's clips to their bars, doubling a note clip's notes, lane, loop and length and keeping an audio clip's length, while a track in time keeps its clips; a tempo change at a note clip's bar squeezes it to the bar's new length and removing the change puts it back; the timebase round-trips the session file and an old file loads in time. The whole suite passed (1,610 checks; 327 commands, 104 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the menu item and a tempo change on screen; nothing listened to.

**Milestone 380: A Mute tool, and Clip Properties for note clips**
- Asked for during the round of use: a **Mute tool** (key 7, a speaker with a cross on the toolbar) silences a clip, audio or notes, with a click, and lets it be heard again with another. The **Clip Properties** panel now shows a note clip when one was clicked last: its name, start, length and loop length (0 plays the notes once) to read and type, each change an undo step, with the gain, fade and note fields put away; its line says how many notes and lanes it has, whether it repeats, and whether it is muted.

The whole suite passed (1,607 checks; 326 commands, 104 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.

**Milestone 379: A rubber band and a Range tool**
- Asked for during the round of use: there was no way to select several clips by dragging round them, and the only way to a time range in the lanes was the Select tool's drag on empty lane. Now the Select tool rubber-bands: a drag across empty lane space selects the audio and note clips the band crosses (a grouped clip with its group), Shift adds them to what was selected, and the band is drawn as it goes. The time range moves to a **Range tool** (key 6, a dashed box on the toolbar): a drag anywhere across the lanes, over clips or not, selects a time range and moves nothing, the cursor at its start; a click sets the cursor. A drag along the marker lane under the ruler still selects a range with any tool.
- Measured by the tests (2 new checks, 1 extended): a band over the first audio clip and the first note clip selects both and no time range, a Shift band adds the second note clip and a plain band selects it alone; the Range tool's drag over a clip selects the range and leaves the clip where it was, its click sets the cursor and clears the range; the toolbar has six buttons, Range after Glue. The whole suite passed (1,605 checks; 325 commands, 103 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the band and the Range tool on screen.

**Milestone 378: Several note clips selected at once**
- Asked for during the round of use: only one note clip could be selected, so note clips could not be moved, copied or deleted together. Now Shift-click adds a note clip to the selection or takes it out, as it does an audio clip, and the two kinds share one selection: a drag on any selected clip moves them all in time, each keeping its track; Nudge, Quantize Clips to Grid, Move Clips to Cursor, Mute Clip, Cut, Copy, Paste, Duplicate and Delete work on the lot (pasted note clips go onto the selected track, or one new instrument track when it takes no notes, keeping their spacing); Select All and the other Select commands take note clips too. A plain click still selects one clip alone, and the piano roll follows the note clip clicked last.
- Measured by the tests (5 new checks): Shift-click adds and removes a note clip and an audio clip joins the same selection; a drag moves every selected clip by the same time, stopping at the session's start, tracks kept; a nudge moves them by one step; Mute Clip mutes and unmutes them all; two copied note clips paste onto one new instrument track keeping their spacing, duplicate after themselves and delete together, and Select All takes both kinds; Quantize moves every selected clip of either kind onto the grid. The whole suite passed (1,603 checks; 324 commands, 102 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: Shift-click and group drags on screen.

**Milestone 377: The ruler in time, in bars, or both**
- Asked for during the round of use: the ruler showed the time, or bars and beats, never both. Multitrack > Tempo > **Time with Bars** (with Bars and Beats on) adds a second, smaller row of time labels under the bar numbers, so a session is read in bars and in seconds at once; snapping and the grid stay on the beats.
- Measured by the tests (1 check extended): with Time with Bars on, the ruler holds more ink in the text colour than with the bars alone (counted over the whole ruler band, antialiasing allowed for). The whole suite passed (1,597 checks; 324 commands, 102 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the two-row ruler on screen.

**Milestone 376: A toolbar for the multitrack view**
- Asked for during the round of use: there was no way to draw a note clip straight into a lane, and no toolbar in the multitrack view. Now a toolbar sits under the lanes with five tools, each with an icon, a key (1 to 5) and a line saying what it does: **Select** (the usual mouse: clicks and drags select, move and trim clips), **Draw** (a drag along an instrument track's lane makes an empty note clip there, selected, ready for the Piano Roll; below the last track it makes an instrument track; on an audio track nothing), **Split** (a click on a clip, audio or notes, cuts it where it is clicked, a clip's cut moved to a zero crossing when that is on), **Stretch** (a drag on a clip's edge stretches it, audio as Option-drag did, and now notes too: the notes, lanes, loop and length scale with the clip) and **Glue** (a click on a note clip joins it with the next one on its track into one clip that plays what both did, a repeating clip's passes written out; also Merge with Next in a note clip's menu). Right of the tools, the session's tempo and beats in a bar (a click opens Tempo...) and a Bars switch, lit while the ruler counts bars and beats. The tools are under Multitrack > Tools too.
- Measured by the tests (6 new checks): flattening a repeating clip writes its passes out; merging two clips makes one from the first start to the last end that plays what both did, the second clip's notes and lanes moved along; stretching scales notes, lanes, loop and length keeping the start or the end, and refuses a ratio of 0 or not a number; Draw makes a note clip along an instrument lane, a new instrument track below the tracks, nothing on an audio track or from a click; Split cuts audio and note clips where clicked; Stretch scales a note clip's notes with its length; Glue joins a note clip with the next and does nothing without one; the toolbar's buttons are lit for the tool in hand and run their commands, the tempo reads as "98.5 bpm, 3/4", Bars lights with the ruler. The whole suite passed (1,597 checks; 323 commands, 102 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the toolbar and the tools on screen.

**Milestone 375: The version on the loading screen**
- Asked for during the round of use: the loading screen now shows the app's version, small in its bottom right corner ("Version 0.15.2" and so on), clear of the status line and the Continue button, so a screenshot or a glance says which build is starting.

- Not tried by hand: the corner on screen.

**Milestone 374: Instrument tracks apart from audio tracks**
- From use of 0.15.2: every track had an Inst button, so an instrument could go on an audio track, and a track could hold audio clips and note clips at once, which makes no sense. Tracks now have a kind. An **instrument track** holds note clips and their controller lanes alone, played by the instrument first in its chain, and has the Inst button; an **audio track** holds audio clips alone and has the input and record buttons; neither is a bus or a folder, and an instrument track is never 5.1 or 7.1. Add Instrument Track... makes an instrument track, and so does a Standard MIDI File's import for each of its tracks; Add Notes..., Add Trigger and Instrument are in an instrument track's menu alone, 5.1 Track and 7.1 Track in an audio track's; Record Notes asks for an instrument track. A clip that an edit would put on the wrong kind of track (a file dropped on an instrument track, a note clip pasted with an audio track selected, a clip dragged past the last track) goes on a new track of the right kind instead, and a clip dragged onto the wrong kind stays where it was; copied time pastes each kind of clip onto its kind of track. **Instrument Track** in a track's menu turns a track with no clips of its own kind into the other kind (an instrument track's instrument is removed on the way back); track templates keep the kind.
- Session files save each track's kind. A session saved before this reads a track with an instrument heading its chain, or with note clips and no audio clips, as an instrument track, and any clips still on the wrong kind of track (audio clips on an instrument track, note clips on an audio track) go onto a new track of the right kind after it, named like it, so nothing is lost.
- Measured by the tests (4 new checks, 4 changed): each kind of clip on its kind of track or a new one, moves onto the wrong kind refused, a kind changed only on a track with no clips of its own kind, pasted time sorted by kind, a file keeping the kinds and one from before kinds given them, clips on the wrong kind moved to a track of their own; Add Instrument Track makes an instrument track. The whole suite passed (1,590 checks; 318 commands, 97 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- In the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) as row 15. Not tried by hand: the menus and the headers on screen.

**Milestone 373: The pan after the effects**
- From use of 0.15.2: a stereo track panned hard left was still heard on the right. The mixer panned each clip as it was read, before the track's effects, so an instrument's output, which the effects chain adds, was never panned at all, and any stereo effect after the pan (a reverb, a widener) spread sound back into the other channel. Now each clip is placed into the track unpanned (a mono clip into both channels), the effects and the instrument run, and the pan is applied once to what the track puts out, before its volume: a track whose sources are all mono (its clips, its live input, no instrument) keeps the constant-power law, so a mono clip plays exactly as before at every pan; a track with any stereo source (a stereo clip, a stereo input, an instrument) is balanced, so hard left silences the right. A mono clip on a track that also has stereo sources is placed at -3 dB each side, the level the centre gave it before. The pan envelope is read where the pan is applied, and a pan hard to one side now leaves the other side exactly silent (the law's cosine of a right angle is not quite zero in single precision). Effects on a mono track now hear the clip at full level in both channels, where before they heard it panned; a frozen track's audio was panned when it was frozen, as before.
- Measured by the tests (4 new checks): a mono clip and a stereo clip through a channel-swapping effect, hard left, play on the left alone, the stereo clip's right channel now on the left; without effects the laws give what they gave; a mono clip beside a stereo clip is placed at -3 dB each side; an instrument track hard left plays on the left alone, the left as it was. The whole suite passed (1,586 checks; 318 commands, 97 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- In the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) as row 14. Not listened to.

**Milestone 372: Open files dragged into the multitrack view**
- From use of 0.15.2: a file in the Files panel could not be dragged into the multitrack view. Now a row of the panel dragged into the view adds that file as a clip where it lands, on the track there or a new one past the last, at the frame it is dropped at (on the grid when snapping is on): the audio as it is in the editor, unsaved changes and all, named after the file (Untitled for one never saved); an empty session takes the file's sample rate, otherwise the audio is converted to the session's; one undo step, "Add <file>". The clip's file is the one on disk, so a session saved to disk reloads the file as saved there: save the file first for a session to find it again as heard. A click in the panel now switches files on mouse up, so a drag does not switch.
- Measured by the tests (2 new checks): the view takes a row's drag, nothing else, and names the row, the track and the frame; a file added to a session carries the editor's audio, its name, the rate conversion, and Untitled for a file never saved; an empty file adds nothing. The whole suite passed (1,582 checks; 318 commands, 97 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- In the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) as row 13. Not tried by hand: the drag on screen.
