**Milestone 369: Bouncing, freezing and stems of a track with only note clips**
- Found reading the code, not by use, after the 0.15 line landed: Bounce Track and Freeze Track were greyed out unless the selected track had an audio clip, and Export Stems (and the folder stems) wrote a stem only for tracks with audio clips, so an instrument track playing note clips alone could not be bounced, frozen or exported, though Mix Down rendered it. The rendering underneath handled note clips; only the enabling checks and the stem list looked at audio clips, and the milestone 363 changelog claimed all four worked. A track now counts as having something to render when it has audio clips or note clips, and a bounce or freeze reaches to the end of its last clip of either kind (the freeze's tail after it, as before). In the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) as row 11.
- Measured by the tests (1 new check): a track with only a note clip is a stem, its reach is its last note clip's end, and it keeps the clip isolated for a bounce; a track without clips still has none. The whole suite passed (1,578 checks; 317 commands, 97 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: a bounce, freeze or stem of an instrument track on screen; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 368: Every key accounted for**
- Window > Keyboard Shortcuts now lists, after the commands, the keys that panels handle themselves while they have the focus (the piano roll's arrows, Delete and Cmd-A; the Sound Library's Return, Space, 0 to 5 and F; the on-screen keyboard's letters; the marker list's Return and Delete; the effect dialogs' Space, Return, Escape and arrows), each under its panel with what it does, found by the search like a command, marked as fixed: they can't be changed there, and clash with no command's key since they work only in their panel. docs/SHORTCUTS.md has the same list under Inside panels.
- The command audit the suite runs now also checks docs/SHORTCUTS.md against the table: every default key must be in a row of the document's command sections that names its command, written the document's way, and every key in those rows must be a default of the command named, so a key changed in one place and not the other fails the suite. It reads keys written as `a ? b : c` and as character literals too (it had been skipping those quietly: 97 default keys are counted now, not 82), and a test checks that every panel key the window lists is in the document's Inside panels table, written alike. The first run of the document check found Previous Region and Next Region (Shift-Cmd-[ and Shift-Cmd-]) missing from the document; they are in now.
- Measured by the tests (1 new check, 2 changed): the audit script passing on the table and the document; the panel keys each in the document; the shortcuts window's rows listing the panel keys after the commands, read-only, found by a search of their panel and words, left out of the changed-only list. The whole suite passed (1,577 checks; 317 commands, 97 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the window on screen.

**Milestone 367: Controller lanes**
- A note clip can carry controller lanes: the mod wheel, the pitch bend or any CC (0 to 127) as an envelope of values from 0 to 1 over the clip's timeline, repeating with the pattern, one lane a controller. They are sent to the instrument with the notes: at the clip's start, at each pass's, and at each 64-frame automation step of the session where the value they round to changed (a CC's 0 to 127, the bend's 0 to 16383 with 0.5 at rest), before the notes at that frame, so a note starts with the wheel where the lane has it. The same whatever the blocks. Lanes ride the timeline, so trims, splits, copies and pasted time keep them with the notes, and they are saved in the session file (LANE elements with POINT children).
- The piano roll's Lane choice (None, the mod wheel, the pitch bend, breath, volume, pan, expression, sustain, resonance, cutoff, then every other CC by number) shows the lane under the notes as a line over the timeline, dashed at the rest value when it has no points: a click puts a point there (snapped to the grid, the value from the height), dragging moves it, Option-click removes it, each one undo step (Lane Point, Remove Lane Point).
- Record Notes keeps the controller moves played with the notes (CCs and the pitch bend) as lanes on the recorded clip; what a keyboard sends live was already passed through to the instrument with the notes.
- Measured by the tests (4 new checks, 3 changed): a rising wheel's 128 values going out at the clip's start and each step where the rounded value changes, a bend at rest once, both before the note at that frame, the same over blocks of 1, 997 and 4,096 frames; a repeating clip's lane starting over with each pass; a lane's points tidied into order and 0 to 1, a second lane for the same controller and an empty one dropped; the file round trip with lanes; captured wheel and bend moves as lanes on the recorded clip; the piano roll's lane taking, moving and dropping a point, and the notes having the whole height without one. The whole suite passed (1,576 checks; 317 commands, 82 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the lane on screen, a wheel on a MIDI keyboard; nothing has been listened to. This completes the 0.15 line's plan; what is left is use.

**Milestone 366: Live MIDI input**
- MIDI keyboards play the selected track's instrument: every MIDI input the Mac (or Windows) has is on from the start, and File > Settings > Audio Device (and Window > Audio Device Settings...) now lists the MIDI inputs to turn each on or off, kept with the device settings. What is played goes into the instrument first in the chain of the track selected in the multitrack, at its frame in the block, through the effects after it, the track's volume, pan, sends and buses; stopped, the session still sounds for ten seconds after the last message, so the instrument is heard without pressing Play (unless input monitoring or scrubbing has the output).
- Window > Keyboard: an on-screen keyboard (five octaves, played by the mouse or the computer's keys as JUCE lays them out), docked under the editor, playing the same way.
- Record Notes (Multitrack > Editing and Recording, Option-R): plays from the cursor (or on, when already playing) and keeps the notes played live, from a MIDI input or the keyboard, at their session frames; stopping playback, or the command again, ends it and puts them on the selected track as a note clip (named Recorded, from the record start or the first note, a note still held ended at the stop), selected, as one undo step. Nothing played makes no clip.
- Import MIDI File... (Multitrack menu, also .mid files dropped on the lanes or chosen with Import Audio...): each track of a Standard MIDI File with notes becomes a note clip on a new track named after it, the notes at the session's rate through the file's tempo map, at the cursor, as one undo step; what isn't a MIDI file, or has no notes, is named in an error.
- Measured by the tests (8 new checks; `Tests/MidiInputTests.cpp`): queued messages coming out of the next block in order, then nothing, and the instrument kept running for the tail; captured notes at their session frames (a held note ended at the stop) and the clip they make from the record start; nothing kept outside a capture; a MIDI file's tracks with notes read into clips named by the track at the session rate through the tempo (a quarter note 24,000 frames at 120), the empty track left out; what isn't a MIDI file, one with no notes and a missing file refused with a reason; a key on the on-screen keyboard reaching the live input as its note on and off; the live messages reaching the track's instrument at their frames across a block mixed in two runs, another track's instrument hearing nothing, and a block without messages playing nothing new. The whole suite passed (1,572 checks; 317 commands, 82 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: a MIDI keyboard on the Mac (none was at hand), the on-screen keyboard, Record Notes, the MIDI import dialog, the sound while stopped; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 365: Note clips as clips**
- Note clips are now handled like audio clips on the lanes: dragged to another track as well as in time; trimmed by their edges (snapping as clips do); split at the cursor (Split Clip, Cmd-K); cut, copied and pasted (Cmd-X, Cmd-C, Cmd-V: onto the selected track at the cursor, or a new track without one); duplicated right after themselves (Cmd-D); and carried with copied time (Copy Time, Paste Time), cleared with deleted time and split by inserted time, cut to the edges as clips are. For that a note clip has an offset, like a clip's: its notes live on a timeline that starts that many frames before the clip, so trimming or splitting leaves them where they were in the session (a note across a new start isn't played; one across a new end is cut off there), and trimming back out brings them back. The offset is saved in the session file.
- Add Trigger in a track header's menu puts a one-note clip (a hit: C4, a tenth of a second, full velocity) at the cursor, selected, for drum-style hits on an instrument without the dialog.
- The piano roll shows a note clip's whole timeline: the part before the clip's start (after a trim or split) washed out with a line where the clip starts, as the part past the pattern already was; and dragging the first repeat line sets the loop length (snapped to the grid), one undo step.
- Measured by the tests (6 new checks, 2 changed): a repeating clip trimmed at both ends playing exactly what it did in that time (the note across the new start left out, the one across the new end cut off there), trimmed back out playing as before and not before its origin; split in two with the parts playing what the whole did and a note across the split cut off there, refused at an edge; a copy placed on the track and on a new track; copied time carrying the clips cut to it and pasted time playing them in time; moves onto a bus (in time only) and past the last track (a new one); deleted time cutting back a clip across its edge and inserted time splitting one; the file round trip with the offset; the piano roll's timeline and loop-length drag. The whole suite passed (1,564 checks; 314 commands, 82 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: dragging, trimming and pasting note clips on screen, the loop line in the piano roll; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 364: The piano roll**
- Window > Piano Roll (a panel docked under the editor, like the Pitch Editor) edits the selected note clip's notes on a grid of pitch against time: a keyboard down the left with each C named, rows for the black keys darker, the clip's length across, grid lines at each division of the session's beat at the clip's start (Beat, 1/2 to 1/32, or Off), a line at each repeat of the pattern and the time past it washed out. Double-clicking a note clip in the multitrack, or Piano Roll in its menu, selects it and opens the panel; the panel follows the selection. A click on empty grid puts a note there (at the grid line before the mouse, one grid step long, or with the grid off the length last dragged to, at the velocity the slider shows); dragging a note moves it in pitch and time, snapped; dragging its right edge changes its length; the selected notes move and resize together. A click selects a note, Shift-click adds or takes it away, a Shift-drag on empty grid selects a box of them, Option-click removes one, Delete or Backspace removes the selected, Cmd-A selects all, the arrow keys move the selected a semitone or a grid step (Shift: an octave, or a quarter step), the velocity slider sets theirs (and new notes'), the mouse wheel scrolls the pitches. Each change is one undo step in the session (Add Note, Move Notes, Note Length, Note Velocity, Remove Note), so Undo in the multitrack takes it back; the notes play as soon as they are placed.
- Measured by the tests (10 new checks; `Tests/NoteEditorTests.cpp`): the grid at a quarter beat (6,000 frames at 120), rows and columns read back; step entry at the grid line before the click, a step long, selected, one undo step; a drag moving a note two semitones and two steps, snapped; its right edge dragged to three steps; Shift-click and the velocity slider on both selected notes; the arrow keys (semitone, step, octave); Option-click, Cmd-A and Delete; a Shift-drag box, a click past the clip's end adding nothing, the grid off; a different clip clearing the selection and nothing shown; drawn in every theme. The whole suite passed (1,558 checks; 314 commands, 82 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the panel on screen, its keys, the mouse wheel; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 363: Instrument tracks**
- The second step of the 0.15 line. A track's FX menu has Add Instrument (the Basic Synth and every VST3 or Audio Unit instrument the scan found, one a track; Replace Instrument once it has one): the instrument goes first in the track's chain and stays there (nothing moves above it), its window opens as a new effect's does, and its parameters take envelopes and touch-to-write like any effect's. What it plays is added to the track's audio (clips and live input) and goes through the effects after it, the track's volume, pan, sends and buses; on a 5.1 or 7.1 track it plays into L and R, with no copies made. Freezing, bouncing, mixing down and stems run the same chain, loaded afresh from the session's XML as before, so they carry the instrument.
- Note clips, what plays it: Add Notes... in a track header's menu puts a clip at the cursor (the Generate from Instrument fields: notes by name or MIDI number, together or one after another, velocity, hold, the pattern's length, and how many times it repeats). A note clip is drawn on the lane with its notes as bars over their pitch range and a line at each repeat, named by its pitches, marked (no instrument) until the track has one; click to select it, drag to move it (snapping as clips do), double-click or Edit Notes... to change it, Mute and Remove in its menu, Delete removes it. The clips are undoable and saved in the session file (NOTECLIP elements under the track, NOTE children in frames); deleting time removes the ones inside it and moves later ones back, inserting time moves them on, a duplicated track copies them, freezing a track renders them. Not yet: trimming, splitting, copying and pasting them, or carrying them with copied time; a note clip plays nothing from a track without an instrument. The instrument is given each note at the session frame of the audio it is working on (after a jump every note is turned off first), so its sound lands in time through its latency like an effect's.
- Measured by the tests (18 new checks; `Tests/InstrumentTrackTests.cpp`): a note clip's MIDI the same over blocks of 1, 997 and 4,096 frames (offs before ons at one frame, the last pass cut off at the clip's end, a note at the pattern's length left out, once without a loop, limits; this check caught the note-off at the clip's end frame going missing from a block starting on that frame, fixed before the milestone); the edits (track only, tidied values, replace, move, mute, remove, the session's length, deleted and inserted time, duplicate); the session file round trip; a chain with a click instrument of 37 frames' latency and a gain after it (instrument first however added, through moves; each click on its session frame, summed with the clip and halved; the same mix in blocks of 64 and 1,000; all notes off after a jump and in time from there; muted and bypassed leave the audio alone; on a 5.1 track no copies, L and R alone); the Basic Synth on a track (sound through the note, silence after; a level envelope at -40 dB giving a hundredth; the note clips kept in what freezing renders; the chain a bounce loads from XML with the instrument first mixing the same bit for bit). The whole suite passed (1,548 checks; 313 commands, 82 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the menus, the dialog, dragging a note clip, a third-party instrument on a track, the window; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 362: Generate from Instrument, and the Basic Synth**
- The first step of the 0.15 line (instruments, MIDI and automation for sound design; the roadmap has the rest). Effects > Generate > Generate from Instrument... plays notes on an instrument plug-in and puts the sound in at the cursor, or the start of the selection, as the other generators do: the instrument chosen from the built-in Basic Synth and every VST3 or Audio Unit instrument the plug-in scan found (they were scanned and checked before, with a note played through each, but kept out of the effect menus); the notes typed by name (C4, F#3, Bb2, with C4 middle C) or MIDI number, separated by commas, played together or one after another, each held for a time, with a velocity and the whole length (tails included, up to ten minutes). Instrument Window... opens the instrument's own controls: the instrument sits on a bench, kept with its settings from one use to the next, and what is set in its window is what plays; a change there, or in the dialog, renders the preview anew (the first 30 s, as generated previews do), and Insert renders in the background through a fresh copy given the bench's state, so the window stays open. The sound is rendered offline with the instrument's latency dropped, a mono instrument on every channel and a stereo one's sides on each pair.
- The Basic Synth, built in beside the effects: an oscillator (sine, triangle, sawtooth or square, the last two without aliasing) through a resonant low-pass filter and an ADSR envelope, sixteen voices, velocity setting each note's level; eight hosted parameters, so on a track (a later milestone) they automate like any effect's. Built-in effects can now be instruments (an output alone, MIDI taken).
- Effect dialogs can carry an extra button and be nudged from outside (what the bench's window reports), which Generate from Instrument uses.
- Measured by the tests (15 new checks): pitches from names and numbers; phrases and their MIDI; the synth made from its description, a bare sine at exactly its velocity from its cycle's start and silent after the hold, velocity and level, the release, a chord's three partials, notes in turn, sixteen voices and All Notes Off, its state, the sawtooth's and square's fundamentals at 2/π and 4/π of their peaks; rendering through a test instrument with its latency dropped (also one longer than the sound), a mono output on every channel, prepared offline and released, stopped by progress, a bad phrase refused; and a fresh instance from a description and state playing exactly what the instance plays. The whole suite passed (1,530 checks; 313 commands, 82 default keys); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the dialog, the bench window, a third-party instrument in the app, the preview on screen; nothing has been listened to.

**Milestone 361: The Windows program's file properties give its version**
- Found testing the 0.14.12 installer: `SpectraBlade.exe` from the Windows packages said 0.14.5 in its file properties (Details in Explorer, and what Windows reads), though the installer, its uninstall entry and the About box said 0.14.12. JUCE writes a target's Windows version resource once, by a rule that doesn't depend on the version, so a build folder first made at 0.14.5 kept stamping 0.14.5. `CMakeLists.txt` now removes the generated `.rc` at each configure on Windows, so it is made again from the current version.
- Checked on Windows 11: the package script remade both resources, the program reads 0.14.12 as its file and product version, and the whole suite passed.

**Milestone 360: Low-rate MP3 read from part way in on Windows**
- Found by Milestone 359's new check, run on Windows: a 22.05 kHz mono MP3 at V9 read from part way in gave other samples than reading it from the start (up to 0.71 off). Windows' MP3 reader starts its decoder some frames before where a read begins, so the bit reservoir has what the first frames borrow; those frames were counted at 10, or by their whole size, where it is the audio data after each frame's header and side information that the reservoir reaches back through (255 bytes in MPEG-2), and that file's 26-byte frames hold 13. The count is now taken from the file's smallest frame's audio data (23 frames for that file; 10 still at 44.1 kHz). Windows only (`GaplessMp3.cpp`); the Mac reads MP3 through Core Audio.
- Measured by the tests on Windows 11: the whole suite passed; MP3, M4A and the 22.05 kHz V9 MP3 read from seven positions in any order give what reading them whole gives, to 0.0000000.

**Release 0.14.12: Windows, new screenshots, and MP3 read the same from anywhere**
- `SpectraBlade-0.14.12.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256: Milestones 357 to 359 below on top of 0.14.11. One fix in the macOS app, found by the Windows port's tests: an MP3 with a variable bit rate read from part way in (a preview, an audition, a session clip) began with wrong sound, and now reads as from its start. New besides is the Windows port in the same source (built and tested on a Windows 11 machine for Milestone 358, with no Windows package on the Releases page yet: the Windows workflow builds one on every push, without the tests), the README's screenshots retaken from this build, and an About box that says what the app runs on. A dogfooding release, built for Apple silicon only, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)).
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 from the merged source, signed it, and the whole suite (1515 checks, the command table audit among them) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.14.12, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for Milestone 359 and quit cleanly. Nothing in this release has been listened to; the About box was not looked at on screen.

**Milestone 359: MP3 read from part way in gives the same samples on the Mac**
- Dogfooding: the Windows port's new check, that an MP3 read from part way in gives what reading it all gives, failed on the Mac (0.537 off on a half-scale sweep). Core Audio decodes the first frames after a move into a variable-bit-rate MP3 wrongly: their bits sit in the frames before the move (the bit reservoir), which it doesn't decode first. So a preview, an audition or a session clip of such a file that started part way in began with wrong sound. Measured outside the app with Core Audio alone, on LAME-encoded sweeps: the wrong stretch after a move reached 1,152 samples in a 44.1 kHz V2 file, 3,456 in a V4 file (0.08 s) and 9,216 in a 22.05 kHz mono file at 9 kbps (0.4 s), at most positions of the V4 and V9 files and some of the V2 ones, and none in the constant-rate, ABR and V0 files. On the Mac the app's formats now put Core Audio in a format of its own, whose MP3 readers move to 32,768 samples before the sample asked for and drop what is decoded on the way (reading on from where the last read ended, as playing and loading do, moves nothing); with that margin every start of 13 files (8 to 48 kHz, mono and stereo, 8 to 320 kbps, V0 to V9, ABR, with and without the LAME tag) reads to 0.0000000 of the whole read. The Mac's and Windows' lists of formats are one list now.
- Measured by the tests: MP3 (V4, the last written), M4A and a new 22.05 kHz mono V9 MP3 read from seven positions in any order give what reading them whole gives, to 0.0000000; the whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: previews of MP3s on screen; listening.

**Milestone 358: Windows**
- SpectraBlade builds and runs on 64-bit Windows from the same source, with MSVC (Visual Studio 2022 or later): tried on Windows 11, set up for Windows 10 or later. The window has its own menu bar (Help > About, File > Exit); keys read Ctrl and Alt where the Mac's read Cmd and Option, and Explorer stands for Finder. Audio goes through Windows Audio (WASAPI) or DirectSound; plug-ins are VST3 (Audio Units are the Mac's), scanned out of process as on the Mac, a crashing plug-in ending its worker without Windows' error dialog and a 32-bit-only one reported as having no 64-bit code.
- Files: AAC is written by Windows' own encoder (Media Foundation), into .m4a files laid out as the Mac's are, with an edit list leaving out the encoder's delay (measured for each format, as Windows doesn't document it and it changes with the rate); .m4a (AAC and Apple Lossless) is read by Windows' decoders; MP3 by Windows' decoder from an index of its frames, gaplessly from the LAME tag, as JUCE's MP3 reader lands a frame or more away when it seeks; AC-3 through ffmpeg starts where Core Audio starts it; AIFC and CAF open through ffmpeg. The Sound Library's SQLite is built in, with its word search (FTS5). Writing tags into a WAV replaces it with Windows' ReplaceFile. ffmpeg is found on the PATH and where winget, Scoop and Chocolatey put it.
- Video: Windows' player (DirectShow) can't read MP4 or QuickTime, so AVI is shown itself and MP4, M4V, MOV, MKV and WebM through previews ffmpeg makes as Windows Media Video, in the local application data folder.
- Opening a file from Explorer while SpectraBlade runs opens it in the running window, which comes to the front.
- `Scripts/package-windows.ps1` builds, runs the tests and writes `SpectraBlade-<version>-windows-x64.zip` and `SpectraBlade-<version>-windows-x64-setup.exe` (Inno Setup, `Scripts/SpectraBlade.iss`: per-user without administrator rights, a Start menu entry, SpectraBlade in Open With for WAV, AIFF, FLAC, Ogg, MP3 and M4A, sessions and projects opened with it as a task), each with its SHA-256. The C++ runtime is linked in, and Media Foundation loaded only if Windows has it (N editions don't without the Media Feature Pack). The Windows workflow builds both on every push, without the tests.
- Measured by the tests on a Windows 11 machine (an RDP audio device with two outputs, no VST3 plug-ins installed): the whole suite passed (1486 checks, with Milestones 349 to 357 merged in; the installed-VST3 scan and the 8-output master rack checks skipped), run by the package script with the built program as the scan worker. AAC at 96 to 320 kbps reads back 0 frames late and 0 over, 1,420 bytes besides the audio; MP3 at 320, 192 and 128 kbps and V0, V2 and V4 reads back 0 frames late and 0 over; MP3 and M4A read from seven positions in any order give what reading them whole gives, to 0.0000000; Windows' AAC encoder keeps the sweeps 48.6 dB above the error at 320 kbps, 49.0 at 256, 47.8 at 192, 45.4 at 128 and 44.3 at 96 (its own levels in the tests; Apple's reaches 57.5 at 320), and Windows' MP3 decoder 76.8 dB at 320 kbps. The installer installed silently for the current user, registered the file types, Open With entries, App Paths and its uninstall entry, opened an .m4a sent from a second launch in the running window and a session double-clicked in Explorer there too, and uninstalled leaving the registry as it was before. The interactive installer was clicked through once, by hand.
- The macOS build, checked by hand on a Mac after these changes, works as before.
- The About box names both systems (for 64-bit Windows 10 or later as well as the Mac), and on Windows says LICENSE.txt is beside SpectraBlade.exe.
- The Windows workflow built both packages on GitHub's runners.
- Not tried: Windows 10; real plug-ins, a multi-output interface, ASIO, and recording on Windows; listening.

**Milestone 357: New screenshots, and the About box says what it runs on**
- Dogfooding: the README's screenshots were from 0.9.5 and showed the old lone Multitrack button, not the Editor | Multitrack switch, the tool strip or the status line's mode prefix; and the About box said nothing about where the app runs. All five screenshots are retaken from this build with the demo files (the editor, a multitrack session, the Analysis workspace in the Darker theme, the Light and High Contrast themes), window captures masked at the interface name and scaled to 1,600 wide in sRGB, and their captions name the switch and the tools. The About box now says the app is for macOS 13 or later on Apple silicon, with no Windows, Linux or Intel build, and credits JUCE, LAME, Signalsmith Stretch and Linear and the UCS list as the README does.
- Measured by the tests: the whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly. The screenshots were looked at one by one.
- Not tried by hand: the About box on screen.

**Release 0.14.11: a new file, settings in one place, and the Sound Library by format**
- `SpectraBlade-0.14.11.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256: Milestones 349 to 356 below on top of 0.14.10, six of them fixes from the [dogfooding log](docs/DOGFOODING.md) (rows 4 to 9) and two things asked for while dogfooding (File > New File and File > Settings). A dogfooding release, built for Apple silicon only, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)).
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64, signed it, and the whole suite (1511 checks, the command table audit among them) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.14.11, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for each milestone. Nothing in this release has been listened to; the parts of each milestone not tried by hand are listed with it below.

**Milestone 356: WAV first, and folders left out of the list**
- Dogfooding, after M355: the list should show WAV files alone until another format is asked for, and a folder (of AIFFs, say) should be leavable out of the list when it is come across. The Formats menu now starts with WAV ticked and the rest off; each is opted into, the last one ticked cannot be unticked, and the button names what is shown ("WAV", "WAV, AIFF", "3 Formats", "All Formats"). A folder is left out from its right-click menu in the By Folder tree ("Leave Out of the List", where it then reads "(left out)" and dimmed) or from a row's menu ("Leave "Aiff" Out of the List", the sound's own folder); everything inside it leaves the list, the counts and the search. The Folders menu lists the folders left out, each with "Show in the List Again", as does a Folders Left Out... button on the Settings window's Sound Library page. Both choices are settings kept between runs (the formats as before; the folders as a list of paths).
- Measured by the tests: an index queried for WAV alone, or leaving the AIFF folder out, finds and counts only the WAVs, searched too; the window starts with WAV alone (2 of 3), adds AIFF through the menu's toggle and reports it, leaves only the AIFFs, keeps the last format, shows every format, takes a list from the app (none meaning WAV); a folder left out drops its sounds (reported once), comes back with Show Again, and the app's list is taken without repeats. The whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the menus and the Settings page on a real screen.

**Milestone 355: The Sound Library lists the formats you choose**
- Dogfooding: a library holding the same sounds as WAV and as AIFF listed each twice, and there was no way to leave one format out. Above the list, after the tag filter, a Formats menu ticks each format the index reads (WAV, AIFF, FLAC, Ogg Vorbis, and Other for what CoreAudio reads: MP3, M4A, CAF and more); unticking one leaves its sounds out of the list, the counts and the search, and the button says what is hidden ("Hiding AIFF"). The choice is a setting: it is kept between runs, and the same switches sit on the Settings window's Sound Library page, each side following the other. The index does the leaving out in its query (`Query::hiddenFormats`), so a library of a million sounds pays nothing extra; `SoundLibrary::formatChoices()` lists the formats a scan can give a sound, leaving out any (MP3) that an earlier reader always takes first.
- Measured by the tests: the choices and their labels (CoreAudio's as Other, MP3 not offered on its own); an index of two WAVs and an AIFF queried without AIFF finds and counts the WAVs alone, searched too; the window lists the rest when the app hides a format (a name not offered dropped, nothing reported back), reports each change made through the menu's toggles, shows every format again, and can leave only the AIFFs. The whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the menu and the Settings page on a real screen.

**Milestone 354: File > Settings, every setting in one place**
- Asked for while dogfooding: the app's settings were spread over the View, Transport, Multitrack and Window menus, the open panel and two windows. File > Settings... (Cmd-,; also under the application menu) is one window with sections down the left: Look (theme, accent, waveform colour, time format, what the editor shows), Editing (select at zero crossings, fade shape, file tabs, project history, preview in the open panel), Playback (loop, how surround files play), Multitrack (snapping, zero crossings on drags, crossfades, writing automation), Recording (pre-roll, post-roll, count-in, metronome, input monitoring, loop recording), Audio (the interface, as Audio Settings showed it), Plug-ins, Keys and the Sound Library. Each row reads its setting and writes a change through at once; the menus' switches stay in step, and a change made from a menu shows in the window at once. Audio Settings (the toolbar button and its Window menu item) opens the Audio page; its key Cmd-, now belongs to Settings. The panel is `Settings::Panel`, built from a model of rows, so it holds no settings of its own.
- Measured by the tests: the panel shows a page with a switch, a choice, a number and a made component, each starting from its setting and writing a change through at once, reads them again on a refresh, shows a section by name with its button working, and falls back to the first for a name not there. The command audit counts 312 commands and 82 default keys; the whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the window on a real screen.

**Milestone 353: The drawing goes once its inside is removed or healed**
- Dogfooding: after Delete (or Remove Frequencies) on a box, lasso or brush selection, Space played nothing. The drawing stayed selected, and Space plays only what is inside a drawing, which was now silence. Once what is inside has been removed or healed the drawing has done its work: it goes, the time selection stays, and Space plays the selection as it now sounds. Attenuate Frequencies keeps the drawing, for another step or a listen to what is left inside.
- Measured by the tests: clearing the spectral selection drops the drawing and the band and keeps the time selection; the whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: Delete then Space in the running app.

**Milestone 352: Delete on a spectral selection removes its frequencies**
- Dogfooding: Delete with a box, lasso or brush selection in the spectrogram took the selection's time out of the file, shortening it, when what was wanted was the frequencies inside the drawing gone. Delete now does what Remove Frequencies (Option-Delete) does whenever a spectral selection is in place, keeping the file's length, and its menu text says which it will do; with a plain selection it takes the time out as before.
- Measured by the tests: the whole suite passed (the spectral removal itself is held by the spectral shape tests); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: Delete over a drawing in the running app.

**Milestone 351: A preview started during play loops**
- Dogfooding: with an effect dialog open and its preview on while the file was already playing, the play did not loop; it ran to the selection's end and stopped, or ran on past the selection. The rendered preview set its loop but left the play's own stop point where Play had put it (the selection's end), so an effect whose result runs past that (a stretch, an echo, a reverb) was stopped there by the editor; and it never moved a playhead that was outside the loop into it, so the loop never came round. A preview started while playing now clears the stop (a preview loops until the dialog ends it) and brings the playhead into the loop when it is outside, as a preview started from a stop and the Bypass switch already did.
- Measured by the tests: the whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the preview looping in the running app.

**Milestone 350: A new effect's window opens at once**
- Dogfooding: an effect added to the rack, or to a track through its FX menu, sat in its slot until Edit was clicked. Now its window opens in front as soon as the effect is in, in the rack (`RackView::addEffect`) and on a track (`MultitrackView::addTrackEffect`) alike; Edit brings a window back as before.
- Measured by the tests: added through the rack view, the second of two effects asks for its window as slot 1 as soon as it is in; added through a track, the track's new effect does the same. The whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the window coming up in the running app.

**Milestone 349: File > New File**
- Dogfooding: there was no way to start a file. The only paths to a new one were Record with no file open, which took the interface's rate and the input's channels, and Paste. File > New File... (Cmd-N) now makes a file of silence at a chosen sample rate (the interface's to start with), channels (mono, stereo, 5.1 or 7.1), bit depth (16, 24 or 32-bit float) and length (0.1 to 3,600 s), to record, paste or generate into. It opens beside the files open as an opened file does, named Untitled until Save As, and the empty editor's text says so. The silence comes from one small block of zeros used over and over (`AudioEdits::blank`), so an hour of 7.1 at 192 kHz costs nothing until it is written into.
- Measured by the tests: a blank of 200,000 frames reads as silence from four segments of one block; a new stereo file of 10 s at 44.1 kHz for 16-bit opens beside a file already open, with its frames, channels, rate and bit depth, no file, not dirty, named Untitled, and closes back to the other. The command audit counts 311 commands and 82 default keys; the whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the dialog in the running app.

Now an effect dialog opened while the file plays starts with Preview on, so its settings are heard as they settle, and Stereo Width looks at the selection first: when the channels are the same it says so in its text, the preview's status line and on Apply, instead of doing nothing.

**Milestone 344: A selection changed while looping keeps playing**
- Dogfooding found the first one: lengthening the selection while it played looping went silent at the old end and the play stopped. The play's own stop stayed at the end the selection had when Play was pressed, while the loop followed the new one; now the stop moves with the loop (the player gained `setStopFrame`), and a play left outside the new selection (shortened behind it, or moved) starts the loop again from its start.
- Measured by the tests: a loop of 5,000 to 6,000 frames lengthened to 8,000 while playing, with its stop moved, keeps sounding past the old stop and wraps at the new end; with the stop left where it was, it plays silence past the old end, as the app did. The whole suite passed; the app launched and quit cleanly.

**Milestone 343: Five more themes, the dialogs' words straightened, and a page of each theme**

Each takes the four accents and the waveform colours like the others.

The nine pages were looked at: every panel follows its theme, nothing is unpainted or hard to read.

The whole suite passed (1496 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.

**Release 0.14.6: the lasso drawn as a line, a tool strip, the suite under the debugger and the sanitizers, and Extreme Stretch**
- `SpectraBlade-0.14.6.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256: Milestones 339 to 342 below on top of 0.14.5. Built for Apple silicon only, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)).
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64, signed it, and the whole suite (1496 checks, the lasso's look, the tool strip, the extreme stretch and the command table audit among them) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.14.6, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for each milestone. Nothing in this release has been listened to or clicked through; the parts of each milestone not tried by hand are listed with it below.

**Milestone 342: Extreme Stretch**
- Effects > Time and Pitch > Extreme Stretch... smears the selection, or the whole file, to 1 to 1000 times its length: each window of the sound (0.05 to 2 s, 0.25 by default) keeps its spectrum and loses its timing, so a texture or a chord becomes a pad that holds its pitch and colour while attacks, words and rhythm melt away. The method is the one Paul Nasca published for extreme stretching (windows taken from the input at a pace slower than they are laid down, their magnitudes kept and their phases thrown away), written here from that description: a Hann window in, the square root of one out, so the random-phase windows add in power to an even level, and the same phases for every channel so channels alike stay alike and a stretch repeats bit for bit. Presets: Pad, Drone, Glacier and Grainy. It records into sequences and runs in Batch Process, previews, and markers in the range keep their place in it.
- Measured by the tests: a second of two tones stretched eight times is eight seconds at the input's level within 0.4 dB, the 1 kHz tone holding -0.2 dB of the spectrum (-0.3 in the input) and sitting 12.4 dB over the 3 kHz tone (12.0 in the input), both channels alike and a repeat bit for bit; noise stretched eight times holds its level across the hops, every 50 ms within 0.33 dB of the whole; a factor of 1 keeps the length and the level within 0.11 dB; a stop gives nothing; a range stretched four times leaves the audio before and after bit for bit where it was and the middle at its level within 0.04 dB; the sequence step gives the function's result bit for bit; the command table audit found all 310 commands in a menu. The whole suite passed (1496 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand or listened to: the dialog, the presets and the sound of a stretch. A lone tone wobbles when stretched (its few bins beat with their random phases), as the method does.

**Milestone 341: Under the debugger and the sanitizers**
- The whole suite run as a Debug build (JUCE's assertions and leak detector on) and under the Address and Undefined Behaviour Sanitizers, for the first time, and what they found fixed. Closing a plug-in's window did not tell the plug-in its editor had gone, so opening the window again showed the generic list of parameters instead of the plug-in's own window; now it does. The Sound Library's folder and category trees had unnamed roots, so JUCE could not save their openness and the folder tree forgot what was open whenever a scan rebuilt it; both roots are named and the tree keeps its state. An MP3 export with no tags wrote an empty block to the file (harmless, but asserted); the MPEG-4 box names with a 0xA9 byte were built as if ASCII (they are compared as bytes, so nothing showed; now built byte by byte). A crash writes its stack to the log, `~/Library/Logs/SpectraBlade/SpectraBlade.log`, under the lines that led to it, and the README says how to report a problem. The last compiler warning (a float comparison in Match Loudness) is gone. In the tests themselves: the noise reduction test measured its tone over 408,000 samples of a 384,000-sample buffer (reading past the end, and passing by luck), the EQ window test deleted an editor without telling its processor, a check's text held a non-ASCII character, and the scrubbing speed check gets room under Debug. The README's table was wrong about Opus: this JUCE opens it itself, tags and all; ffmpeg is only needed to write it.
- Measured by the tests: the Debug suite passes (1484 checks), with only assertions from JUCE's own code left (its Opus reader's tags, a label grabbing focus off screen in the mixer test, the VST3 host on kHs Gain), listed in docs/TESTING.md; the sanitizer suite passes too (1484 checks), its only reports shifts inside the LAME encoder and JUCE's JPEG library, after two more test slips it found were fixed (references into a session's track list kept across an addition that moved it, and a 23-byte literal copied as 30). New checks: the folder tree lists the library closed, opens into its 2 folders and is still open after a rescan rebuilds it; a processor holds its editor as active until told it has gone, then makes another. The Release suite passed (1484 checks) with no compiler warnings; the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: reopening a plug-in's window in the running app, and the folder tree across a scan.

**Milestone 340: A tool strip**
- The strip under the editor starts with the tools: Box, Lasso and Brush Selection as icon buttons (a box of dashes, a loop of rope, a stroke of paint ending in a dab), the one in hand lit with the accent, and Heal (a plaster), on when there is a drawing or a box of frequencies to heal. Each runs its command, with the menu item's tooltip and key, so you can see which tool you hold and switch without the keys. The times follow the tools, dropped from the right as before when they don't fit.
- Measured by the tests: the four buttons sit left of the times in order; the lit one follows the view's tool (Box, then Lasso after a switch); clicking Box, Lasso and Brush runs their commands and Heal does nothing until a drawing is placed, then runs its own. The whole suite passed (1482 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the icons on a real screen, in the Light and High Contrast themes.

**Milestone 339: The lasso drawn as a line**
- The lasso tool draws freehand, as it did, but now shows what it is doing: while the mouse is down only the line drawn so far is shown, in black and white dashes that read on any colour of spectrogram, with a faint dotted edge from the mouse back to the start to show where the shape will close when the mouse goes up. Before, the shape was closed and washed in from the first moment, so what was being traced was hard to see and the straight closing edge moved with the mouse. Once closed, the shape is washed white (dark for a stroke that takes away) and edged in the same dashes (a brush stroke is filled, as before). Over a spectrogram the lasso tool has a pointer of its own, a loop of rope with a dot at the point that draws; the brush keeps the crosshair and its circle.
- Measured by the tests: with three corners of a lasso drawn and the mouse still down, the pixel at its centre is the spectrogram's own (no wash), the line so far has pixels brighter than 0.5 and darker than 0.2 along it (the dashes) and the closing edge is drawn fainter; released, the centre is washed by more than 0.1 over the selection under it and the closing side edged in dashes; the lasso's pointer over a spectrogram is neither the crosshair nor the arrow, and the brush's is the crosshair. The whole suite passed (1481 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: drawing with a mouse or trackpad in the running app.

**Milestone 338: Time formats**
- View > Time Format chooses how every time is shown, for the whole app and kept for next time: Minutes and Seconds (as before), Decimal Seconds, Samples, or SMPTE timecode at 24, 25, 29.97 drop (hh:mm:ss;ff, the two frames skipped at each minute but the tenth), 29.97 or 30 fps. The editor's and the session's rulers, the transport's time, the strip under the editor, the Amplitude Statistics' peak times, the marker list and the clip list follow it, and the rulers mark whole samples or whole frames of timecode (1, 2, 5, 10 frames, then seconds) instead of fractions of a second. A time typed into Go to Time, Set Selection and the other dialogs is read in the format too: on Samples a whole number is samples; on a timecode hh:mm:ss:ff or mm:ss:ff at its frame rate, the dropped numbers refused on drop frame; seconds, m:ss and h:mm:ss still read in every format. Clip Properties' fields, CSV exports and the Sound Library stay in seconds. The marker and clip lists' columns are headed Start, End and Length without a unit.
- Measured by the tests: 83.304 s at 48 kHz reads 1:23.304, 83.304, 3998592, 00:01:23:07 at 24 and 25 fps, 00:01:23:09 at 30, 00:01:23:06 at 29.97 non-drop and 00:01:23;08 drop; an hour reads 01:00:00;00 drop; 3,200 random times written and read back in every format name the same text (within half a millisecond for seconds, the first sample of the frame for timecode); 00:01:00;02 drop parses and 00:01:00;00 does not; the rulers pick 0.1 s at a millisecond a pixel, 100 samples at a sample a pixel, one frame at 25 fps at 0.4 ms a pixel; the command table audit found all 309 commands in a menu. The whole suite passed (1478 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the rulers and the dialogs in the running app in each format.

**Milestone 337: Amplitude Statistics**
- Window > Amplitude Statistics: a panel that measures the selection, or the whole file without one (in a session, the selected clip under the time selection or all it plays) when you click Scan, in the background with its progress shown, and lists for each channel the peak and where it is, the true peak, the samples at full scale (32767/32768 or beyond), the DC offset, the RMS of the whole, of the loudest and of the quietest 50 ms window with sound, the spread between those two as the dynamic range, and the bits the samples need (8, 16, 24 or 32-bit float); under them the integrated loudness, loudness range, maximum momentary and maximum short-term of all channels together, as Loudness measures them; and a histogram of the windows by their RMS, a column a dB from -100 to 0, one outline per channel. Copy puts the table on the clipboard as tab-separated text. The panel says what it measured, and when the audio or the selection has changed since, so you know to scan again. It docks under the editor by default.
- Measured by the tests: a half-scale sine reads -6.02 dBFS with its peak at frame 12, RMS -9.03 dB, 40 windows in 2 s all alike (dynamic range 0.000 dB) and all in the -10 dB bin, needing 32 bits; a 16-bit square wave at full scale for 1 s, at a tenth for 0.5 s and then silent counts 48,000 samples at full scale, 16 bits, a loudest window of 0 dB and a quietest with sound of -20 dB (dynamic range 19.999 dB), 20, 10 and 10 windows in the 0 dB, -20 dB and silent bins, and a true peak of 2.1 dBTP from the oversampled overshoot; a constant 16-bit value reads a DC offset of 10 % and 16 bits; multiples of 1/128 read 8 bits and of 1/8,388,608 24; the loudness is bit-identical to Loudness::measure; the panel's scan finishes in the background and reports the range, a changed selection and an empty source. The whole suite passed (1459 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand or listened to: the panel in the running app.

**Milestone 336: The Multitrack menu in groups**
- The Multitrack menu was one list of 65 commands. The five panels (Mixer, Clip Properties, Clip List, Marker List, Session Notes) and Import Audio stay at the top; the rest are eight submenus by what they do: Tracks (adding, templates, duplicating, deleting, moving, hiding, selecting above and below, Unsolo All and Unmute All), Clips (split, duplicate, repeat, reverse, stretch, pitch, lock, mute, groups, gain lines), Arrange (nudging, moving to the cursor, trimming to it, closing gaps, quantizing), Time Range, Editing and Recording (snapping, zero crossings, crossfades, writing automation, input monitoring, loop recording), Tempo (the metronome and count-in with it), Surround, and Render and Export (bounce, freeze, mix down, stems, regions). The Effects menu's submenu helper now serves every menu.
- Measured by the tests: the command table audit found every one of the 300 commands still in a menu and no default key claimed twice. The whole suite passed (docs checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: opening the menu in the app.

**Milestone 335: The playhead on what is heard**
- An effect with latency in the rack or a live preview (the linear-phase EQ and band splits at 8,703 frames, the convolution reverb at 512) no longer puts the sound behind the playhead. The engine listens to its graph and tells the player the chain's latency as it changes; the playhead, the transport's time, markers dropped while playing, the video picture and automation written while playing all follow the frame being heard (the start while the effects fill, the pass before when a loop's position has just gone round), and the player plays silence on for that long past the end of a file, a session or a played selection, so the last of it comes through before the transport stops. With no latency nothing changes.
- Measured by the tests: with 1,000 frames of latency the heard frame is the start for the first 1,000 frames played, then 1,000 behind the position, and reaches a selection's end 1,000 frames after the position does; round a 1,000-frame loop with 700 of latency it is 700 behind on the pass before (5,348 against a position of 5,048); a file played to its end runs on a block of silence and stops in the next, and stops in the block the file ends in with no latency, as before; a preview effect with 64 frames of latency reaches the player through the engine's graph and goes when it is cleared. The whole suite passed (1424 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand or listened to: the playhead against the sound with Linear Phase on in the running app.

**Milestone 334: Undo for the strokes of a drawing, and keys for the brush's strength**
- While a drawing is being made, Command-Z takes back its last stroke: the Edit menu reads Undo Stroke, each press removes one more (a taking stroke, an added lasso, the first lasso in turn), the time selection follows the strokes left, and when the last goes the cursor lands where the drawing began. Once the file has changed since the stroke was drawn, Undo is the file's again and the drawing stays as it is; there is no Redo for a stroke.
- Lighter Brush and Heavier Brush have default keys, Shift-[ and Shift-] (Smaller and Larger Brush are [ and ]). The Keyboard Shortcuts window shows them as the characters they type, { and }. docs/SHORTCUTS.md lists them.
- Measured by the tests: three strokes drawn in the view (a lasso, a Shift lasso, an Option take) come back off one by one with the selection following and the cursor at the drawing's start, nothing to take back before or after; a lasso drawn and then the file changed leaves Undo to the file with the lasso kept. The command table audit passed with the new keys (300 commands, 80 default keys). The whole suite passed (1419 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: Undo Stroke in the running app, and the keys on a keyboard.

**Release 0.14.3: the command table audited, the nudge keys as asked, taking from a drawing, and the brush's strength**
- `SpectraBlade-0.14.3.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256: Milestones 331 to 333 below on top of 0.14.2. Built for Apple silicon only, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)).
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64, signed it, and the whole suite (1417 checks, the command table audit among them) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.14.3, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for each milestone. Nothing in this release has been listened to or clicked through; the parts of each milestone not tried by hand are listed with it below.

**Milestone 333: Taking from a drawing, and the brush's strength**
- Option-drag with the lasso or the brush draws a stroke that takes from the drawing: what it covers is no longer selected (Shift still adds). A drawing is now its strokes in the order they were drawn, each adding or taking, so a take can be painted back over, and the time selection stays the adding strokes' span. A stroke that takes is drawn as a dark wash with a dashed edge. With the box tool Option-drag still selects a box.
- Edit > Lighter Brush and Heavier Brush set how much the brush selects, 10 % to 100 % in steps of 10 (100 % to start; the menu items say the current setting, and the brush's circle is filled as strongly as it selects). A stroke's strength is its weight in the drawing: an effect on the drawing applies by that much where only that stroke was painted, so Remove Frequencies with the brush at 50 % takes the sound down by half (6 dB) and Heal blends half of the surroundings in. Where strokes overlap, the last one decides: an add raises the weight to its strength at the least, a take lowers it to one minus its strength at the most, so a take at 50 % over a full stroke leaves half. A lasso always selects fully.
- Measured by the tests: in the geometry, a box over 1 to 2 kHz with a take out of its middle reads as two ranges at a frame in the take and one outside it, a half-strength brush weighs a half, a half-strength take lowers a full part to a half, a stroke that only takes is no shape, and the span is the adding strokes'; in the audio, a box over a 4 kHz tone from 0.5 s to 2.8 s with 1.3 s to 2.1 s taken back out removes the tone before and after (144 dB down) and leaves the middle within 0.000 dB, and removing with the brush at half strength takes the tone down 6.02 dB; in the view, an Option-drag with the lasso adds a taking stroke whose middle is no longer selected while the rest still is and the time selection stays. The whole suite passed (1417 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: drawing a take or painting at a lower strength in the running app, and how the dark wash reads over a busy spectrogram.

**Milestone 332: The nudge keys as asked, and a word while Play waits**
- The nudge keys follow the table given: Shift-Left and Shift-Right move the selection's end earlier and later, Command-Shift-Left and Command-Shift-Right its start (milestone 330 had Shift-Left on the start and Command-Shift-Left on the end). docs/SHORTCUTS.md now lists them, with Step, Double and Halve Selection, which it had left out.
- Play with a spectral selection shows "Processing..." in the transport's time field until the inside of the selection is ready and plays (milestone 329 showed nothing while it waited).
- Measured by the tests: the command table audit (every command described, performed and in a menu, no default key claimed twice) passed with the new keys. The whole suite passed (1414 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the keys and the word in the running app.

**Milestone 331: The command table audited by the tests**
- Twenty-five commands went missing from the menus for two releases (milestone 328) because nothing checked the menus: the main window is not in the test build, and the look passes rendered panels, not menus. Now `Scripts/check_commands.py` reads the command table out of `Source/MainComponent.cpp` and the suite runs it: every command in the enum (up to the sentinel the registration loop runs to) must have a `case` in describeCommand (a name and a description) and one in perform, must be reachable from a menu (the menu bar, the app menu or another addCommandItem; a command that needs no menu item is listed in the script with the reason, and none is yet), and no two commands may claim the same default key (JUCE's key map gives a key to one command only, so a clash makes a command lose its key silently; a key written as `a ? b : c` counts both ways). The script found one thing: Stop was in no menu (it was only the transport bar's button), and it is now in the Transport menu after Play/Pause. The suite needs `python3`, which comes with the command-line tools it already needs.
- Measured by the tests: 298 commands, each described, performed and in a menu; 78 default keys, no two commands on one. Faults injected by hand into a copy of the source were each found: a command renamed out of its two cases, a command left out of the Edit menu, and a Command-O given to Stop as well as Open. The whole suite passed (1414 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the Transport menu's Stop in the running app.

**Release 0.14.2: twenty-five commands back in the menus, playing only what is inside a spectral selection, nudging the selection, and selecting at zero crossings**
- `SpectraBlade-0.14.2.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256: Milestones 328 to 330 below on top of 0.14.1. Anyone on 0.14.0 or 0.14.1 should take this one: those two left every command added since the Sound Library window (the Dynamic EQ on through Codec Preview) out of the menus. Built for Apple silicon only, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)).
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64, signed it, and the whole suite (1412 checks) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.14.2, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for each milestone. Nothing in this release has been listened to; the parts of each milestone not tried by hand are listed with it below.

**Milestone 330: Nudging the selection, and selecting at zero crossings**
- Edit > Nudge Selection Start Earlier (Shift-Left), Start Later (Command-Shift-Right), End Earlier (Command-Shift-Left) and End Later (Shift-Right) move one end of the selection by a pixel's worth of time at the current zoom (a frame at the least): Shift and an arrow grow the selection that way, Command-Shift and an arrow shrink it from that side. An end stops at the file's ends and a frame short of the other end; a frequency band selected with the box stays.
- Edit > Select at Zero Crossings, an option kept in the settings (off to start): every end of a selection made with the mouse (a click, both ends of a drag as it is dragged, a Shift-click's) lands on the nearest zero crossing of the channels' sum within 5 ms, and a nudged end goes on to the next zero crossing the way it moved, so each nudge steps from crossing to crossing when the view is zoomed in close. The drawings (lasso and brush) are not snapped. Selection to Zero Crossings still moves an existing selection's ends once.
- Measured by the tests: nudging either end by the step, stopping at the file's ends and a frame short of the other end, an empty selection left; the first zero crossing from a frame on the way asked (a 100-frame square wave crosses every 50th frame: from frame 110 later lands on 150, earlier on 100, a frame that is one stays, none in reach stays, none past the end), and the nearest in reach; in the view, a selection dragged over a 100-frame square wave with snapping asked for lands both ends on zero crossings (frames 9,700 and 48,300, from 9,696 and 48,288 without). The whole suite passed (1412 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the keys and the menu items in the running app, and nudging with the view zoomed right out (a pixel is then many frames).

**Milestone 329: Playing only what is inside a spectral selection**
- Space (and the transport's Play) with a box, lasso or brush selection in the spectrogram now plays only what is inside it: a box, its band of frequencies over the selection; a drawing, what is drawn round or painted over. Before, the selection's whole span played at every frequency, so a sound picked out in the spectrogram could not be heard on its own. The audio with everything else over the selection taken out is processed in the background (as Attenuate Frequencies at minus infinity would take the inside out; the same tapered edges) and plays in place of the file until playback stops, the selection moves, the file changes or an effect's preview starts; Space while it is being processed stops it, and the file itself is never changed. Loop Playback loops it.
- Measured by the tests: everything outside 3 to 8 kHz over 1 s to 3 s taken out of two tones leaves the 5 kHz tone at -12.04 dB (its own level), takes the 1 kHz tone down to the analysis floor (-130 dB) and leaves the audio before the selection bit for bit; keeping only a lasso drawn over a 4 kHz tone from 1 s to 2 s leaves it within 0.000 dB, takes a 500 Hz tone beside it down 162 dB on both channels, and leaves every sample more than 3,072 frames from the drawing bit for bit. The whole suite passed (1409 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: hearing it in the running app, and a long selection (the processing takes about as long as Attenuate Frequencies does on it, and Play waits for it; nothing says so meanwhile).

**Milestone 328: Twenty-five commands back in the menus**
- Every command added since the Sound Library window (milestone 280), from the Dynamic EQ (milestone 297) on, was missing from the menus and from the Keyboard Shortcuts window, in 0.14.0 and 0.14.1 too: the loop that registers the commands stopped at the Sound Library's, and a command the manager does not know is left out of a menu without a word. Back are Dynamic EQ, Multiband Stereo Width, Exciter, Maximizer, Transient Shaper, Capture Match Reference (from the selection and from a file), Match EQ, the Tonal Balance panel, Dither, Dialogue Isolation, Adaptive Noise Reduction, Mouth De-click, De-crackle, De-plosive, Breath Control, Wind, Rustle and Bleed, Wow and Flutter, Spectral Recovery, Rebalance, Capture Ambience, Ambience Match, Dialogue Contour, Azimuth and Codec Preview. The loop now runs to a sentinel at the end of the command list, so a command added after it cannot be missed again.
- Measured by the tests: none cover the menus (the main window is not in the test build); every command id was checked by script to have a description. The whole suite passed (1407 checks); the app launched and quit cleanly.
- Not tried by hand: the menus themselves in the running app.

**Release 0.14.1: Apple silicon only, a low voice's pitch, effects that say when they find nothing, and the Sound Library's folders pane**
- `SpectraBlade-0.14.1.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256: Milestones 324 to 327 below on top of 0.14.0. Built for Apple silicon only (the first release without an Intel half), signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)).
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64, signed it, and the whole suite (1407 checks) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.14.1, holds arm64 alone, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for each milestone. Nothing in this release has been listened to; the parts of each milestone not tried by hand are listed with it below.

**Milestone 327: the Sound Library's folders pane, and small things seen**
- The pane on the left of the Sound Library (By Folder and By Category) could not be sized, and a watched folder was shown by its whole path cut off at the end, so a long path hid the folder's own name. A bar beside the pane now sizes it by dragging (from 120 px up to what leaves the list 320), the width is kept in the settings, and a watched folder is drawn as its name with where it is before it in dimmer text, cut from its start (the part nearest the name is the telling part) when the room is short and dropped when none fits; the tooltip holds the whole path. Letting the list's columns stretch to fit the window was tried and taken out again: at 1,100 px wide the twelve columns squeezed every value to an ellipsis. The dialogs' status line (what the preview is doing, or a finding such as milestone 326's) wraps onto two lines in a smaller type instead of being cut off with an ellipsis. The spectrogram's frequency labels no longer overlap when a lane is short (2k, 1k and 500 ran into each other in a four-lane view): a label within 12 px of the last stays off, its tick stays on. Looked at: the editor and multitrack workspaces captured from the running app, the Sound Library view in both panes and the Adaptive Noise Reduction dialog rendered offscreen in the Dark and Light themes. The main window needed nothing seen. A second round rendered the rest in the Dark, Light and High Contrast themes, 78 renders in all: the Parametric, Graphic and Dynamic EQ panels, the multiband panel, the Levels, Spectrum, Phase and Tonal Balance panels, the Pitch Editor, the surround pad, the transport bar and the selection panel, the keyboard shortcuts editor, the loading screen, the video panel, the mixer, the multitrack view and its overview with the demo session, Clip Properties, the clip list, the markers, the effects rack, the undo history, the session notes, the Files panel and the file tabs. Nothing in them needed changing. (Rendered on a transparent background they first showed black behind the parts that paint no background of their own, the multiband panel and the transport bar among them; on the theme's base colour, as their windows supply it, they are right.) Also tried and dropped, for the voice-over of milestone 325: a two-harmonic frame taken as voiced when its pitch moves between the frames round it (hum with two harmonics was taken for a voice 70 to 97 % of the time in the prototype).

**Milestone 326: Adaptive Noise Reduction and Dialogue Isolation say when they find nothing to keep**
- On the handheld recording of a crowded room both effects lowered everything by about 12 dB and said nothing. Measured, the recording is a wall of sound: its 100 ms levels span 7 dB from the 5th to the 95th percentile (the voice-over spans 43), in each octave band only 3 to 28 % of frames stand 6 dB over the band's 1.5 s floor, the channels are incoherent above 1 kHz (a diffuse room, no near talker) and the envelope has almost no speech-rate modulation (9 % of it between 2 and 8 Hz, the voice-over 35 %). There is no near voice to keep, so a floor that tracks the least over the span (which the de-noise already is) is right that the whole is noise, and a crowd has no one pitch; what was wrong is that the user was not told. Both effects now report what they found: the share of the range's frames with sound 6 dB clear of the tracked noise (de-noise) or read as speech, voiced or fricative (isolation), and how far the typical block came down (the median over 4,096-frame blocks with sound in them). When the share is under a tenth, the preview's status line and a note after applying say so, with the numbers and why (noise alone, or a sound that never pauses taken for the noise; a crowd of voices with no one pitch not kept); the dialogs say the same. Measured on the recordings of milestone 319: the handheld has sound clear of the noise in 1.8 % of its frames and speech in 0.9 %, the typical block down 11.8 and 12.0 dB; the shortwave 31 % and 33 %; the voice-over 30 % and 27 % (its typical block, in a pause, down 6.0 and 10.8 dB), so neither says anything there. The audio the effects produce is unchanged. The preview's status line now shows any effect's finding, not only a generator's. In the tests, the de-noise scene's two words stand clear in 36 % of its frames and steady noise alone in none (the typical block down 11.8 dB); the test's noise that steps up 12 dB halfway stands clear in 16 % of its frames alone, the second or so until the tracker has followed the step, so a rising noise is not mistaken for a crowd. Not listened to; the note and the status line were not seen in the app.

**Milestone 325: a low voice's pitch**
- The pitch finder behind Dialogue Isolation, Wind, Rustle and Bleed and Rebalance (`DialogueIsolation::pitchOf`) read the character voice-over's lowest passages as nothing: at 75 to 85 Hz its fundamental lies under the correlation's 100 Hz band and its strong first formant makes the frame repeat at a third of its period nearly as well as at the period, so the shortest repeat was taken as the pitch, found no harmonics of its own, and the frame read as unvoiced. Now when the shortest repeat fails the harmonic test the longer repeats up to four times it are tried in turn, each asked for more (five harmonics standing over the gaps, one of them among the first three), so a chance repeat in noise gains nothing from the second look. The header's description, which still described the comb method replaced in milestone 319, now describes the finder as it is. Measured on the real recordings of milestone 319: the voice-over's loud frames voiced at the middle sensitivity went from 71 to 84 of 183 (39 % to 46 %), its quiet frames 13 as before, and Dialogue Isolation's cut on its loud blocks from 4.35 to 4.03 dB (worst 11.76 dB as before); the handheld room's numbers are identical; the shortwave has 865 of 1,163 loud frames voiced (864 before) and its isolation takes 2.20 dB off the loud blocks (2.21). A prototype of the finder checked against synthetic frames at 48 and 96 kHz (white, pink and red noise, lone tones at 150 and 440 Hz, a 525 Hz tone in noise, hum at 50 and 60 Hz with six harmonics, clicks in noise, buzzes at 80 to 300 Hz) reads them as before. Of the voice-over's 183 loud frames, 50 still read as nothing periodic (closed and nasal sounds with two harmonics over the floor, which the three-harmonic rule keeps out along with hum) and 46 repeat at 0.3 to 0.6 (a rough voice whose periods are unlike each other, which is what the sensitivity is for). Tried in the prototype and dropped: a period-to-period repeat measure (it raised the quiet frames' false voicing from 7 to 21 %), wider analysis frames (85 ms read 25 % of the loud frames as voiced) and shorter ones (noise read as voice), and trying every longer repeat under the old three-harmonic rule (a 525 Hz tone in noise then read as voiced over half the time). Test: an 80 Hz buzz with its fundamental at a fifth and its third harmonic six times over reads 80 Hz with voicing over 0.8 at 48 and 96 kHz, which the prototype of the finder before read as nothing periodic. Not listened to.

**Milestone 324: Apple silicon only**
- From here on SpectraBlade is built for Apple silicon alone. Releases up to 0.14.0 were universal, but their Intel half was never launched and no Intel Mac was ever tried, so rather than ship code nobody has run there is no Intel build: `Scripts/package.sh` builds arm64 only and stops if the app holds any other architecture, and the README (its badge, the download line and the install notes) says so. The plug-in scan always looks for Apple silicon code now (the check for which kind of Mac the app runs on is gone, since it runs on no other), and its message for a plug-in built only for Intel Macs no longer suggests opening SpectraBlade using Rosetta, which an Apple silicon-only app cannot be. Measured: the package script's build holds arm64 only (`lipo -archs`), and the suite's Intel-only test bundle is still reported as having no code for Apple silicon. Not tried by hand: the scan window's message.

**Release 0.14.0: previews on every effect, our own stretch, and the mastering and repair suite**
- `SpectraBlade-0.14.0.dmg` on the repository's Releases page, with its SHA-256: Milestones 287 to 323 below on top of 0.13.0. Universal app, signed ad hoc, not notarized (see [Download and install](README.md#download-and-install)).
- Checked on the development Mac (macOS 27): `Scripts/package.sh` built the app for arm64 and x86_64, signed it, and the whole suite (1403 checks) passed with the signed app as the plug-in scan worker; the disk image's SHA-256 matched its `.sha256`, its app verified (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), reads 0.14.0, holds both architectures, and carries a licence identical to `LICENSE` with LAME's and Signalsmith's licences. The image's app was not launched; the same code, signed for development, was launched for each milestone. The app's Intel half was not launched, and no Intel Mac was tried. Nothing in this release has been listened to; the parts of each milestone not tried by hand are listed with it below.

**Milestone 323: Level Speech's fastest change**
- Level Speech has a new setting, the fastest change (1 to 30 dB a second): each step's gain is held under a rise at that rate from the gains before and after it (a forward pass, then a backward one, before the ceiling and the smoothing), so a swing between passages takes its time and the gain never goes above what the passages ask, only arrives later. At 30, the largest setting and the default, the gains are exactly what they were (bit for bit in the tests): on the four real recordings of milestone 320 the gains moved at most 5.0 (the voice-over), 10.0 (the shortwave) and 12.6 dB a second (the handheld), all at pauses and changes of speaker, which is where a fader rider moves fast too, so no limit is imposed until one is heard to be needed; the setting is there to lower if the levelling pumps. Sequences and the batch recorded before carry no "speed" value and run as they did. Measured on the test's passages (-12, -30 and -21 LUFS, 4 s each, a second of room noise between): at 6 dB a second the gains rise at most 0.3 dB a step, never over the unlimited gains, and the quiet passage's last half second is 1.2 LU from the target (its 18 dB swing takes 3 s, and the gain starts down early for the louder passage after it); at 30 the gains are the unlimited ones bit for bit. Not listened to.

**Milestone 322: Reduce Reverb measures the room first**
